Crawlers Changelog

Every release, newest first: what changed and why. Play the game

v2.172.251

2026-10-16 16:38 EDT

A heap goes where it is placed; spades are fetched before anyone digs bare-handed; a half-dug block is finished first; a dig for supply takes only what is short; known benches are carried to the fire before stools are made; a compass rose on the map; coughing; no songs in the first minutes; Rooms is L.

  • "The coach points: the heap goes there. Nobody can just now." (the user: "They have two sledges, why can't they do the job?"): placing a heap by clicking asked for the heap to be moved as though it were a prop, which nobody could do. It asks for the heap's own move now. (The "Put the heap here" button in the ring always worked, which is why the test passed.)
  • Spades and picks in the store are fetched first (the user: "I made spades but they were still digging barehanded"): a tool was only fetched for work nobody could do without it, and earth can be dug by hand, only worse -- so the spades lay in the store while the earth was clawed at with knives. Now whoever would do work bare-handed while its tool lies in the store fetches the tool first.
  • A block somebody has begun to dig is finished before another is begun (the user: "they started two dig jobs and worked a lot on them, then abandoned them"). Digging is slow now, and a digger called away by hunger or sleep left the block for whoever came next, who as often as not began a different one.
  • A dig for supply takes only what is short (the user: "There was a pile of dirt at camp with 14 units but they went off to dig to make a raised bed"): the raised bed wanted 16, and a whole block of 64 was dug for the last 2. It takes the pieces wanted and leaves the rest of the block standing. And the yard -- the heaps counted as the camp's own -- reaches 10 m from the fire (yard_m, 6 before), so a heap set down just past the camp counts.
  • Benches and chairs (the user: "If there are known benches around they should claim them rather than building stools"): before a stool is made, a bench, chair or pew somebody has seen within 40 m (seat_fetch_m) is carried to the fire, and is the camp's own seat once it stands in the camp's room.
  • A compass rose on the map, north up (the map is drawn north up; the chronicle's "to the east" agrees with it).
  • Coughing (the user: "Do we use coughing sounds? more."): there was no cough. There are two sound slots now (cough, cough_f, on the Sounds page, for a recording to be dropped in): the sick cough often, someone digging, sweeping or crushing now and then, anyone awake once in a while.
  • No song in the first 20 minutes of a game (song_quiet; the user: "Don't start the game singing").
  • Rooms opens with L (the user: "'R' is both Rooms and Rotate"); R turns what is being placed.

v2.172.250

2026-10-16 16:06 EDT

The fuel system: seven fuels, each burning for its own time at a real pace, a fire lit only with tinder, charcoal from a clamp, and the company choosing what goes on.

The user's choices: fuels wood, paper and fibre, coal and charcoal, dung, and fat; they differ by "Burn time only"; a fire needs "Tinder first"; a "Realistic" pace (a 12 lb armful of wood burns about an hour); charcoal made "Both" in the ashes and in a clamp; "No restrictions" on what burns where; "They choose" what goes on; dung from beasts and lairs, fat from kills, fibre from growths, paper from books.

  • The fuels tab (new, in Work) gives each fuel its minutes of fire a pound, whether it is tinder, the order it goes on, and whether it is saved for the night: dung 4 min/lb (burnt first), wood 5 (an armful an hour), fat 12, coal 11 (a 45 lb piece burns about eight hours), charcoal 9 (coal and charcoal saved for when the fire is banked for the night), dry fibre 2 and paper 3 (tinder: the last two of each are never burnt as fuel, kept to light with).
  • The fire is fed up to two hours of fuel (fire_fuel_max, 12 minutes before) once it falls under half an hour (fire_low), a piece at a time, the least useful fuel first: "Rook feeds the fire with 1 load of wood (95 minutes)". A newly built campfire starts with an hour (fire_fuel_start). Feeding the fire went from a job every few minutes to one every hour or so.
  • Tinder first: lighting a fire -- the first campfire, or one that has gone out -- takes a piece of paper or dry fibre. Without any, the fire stays out ("The campfire is laid, but there is no tinder to light it") and a book is torn up for paper. The company arrives with 3 pieces of dry fibre (start_tinder).
  • Charcoal: the campfire leaves a 5 lb piece in the ashes for every 500 minutes it burns (charcoal_minutes; a twentieth of the wood), and a charcoal clamp can be built (16 pieces of dirt): when the camp is short of charcoal (clamp_keep 8) and long on wood, someone stacks 6 loads of wood in it, it smoulders 8 hours, and is broken open for a third of the wood's weight in charcoal (clamp_wood, clamp_hours, clamp_yield).
  • Where the new fuels come from: a tame beast at camp leaves a dung cake every 4 hours where it stands (dung_minutes), a heap to be fetched in; a nest pulled apart gives 3 cakes of old dung (lair_dung); a kill butchered gives a piece of fat for each step of its size (fat_per_size); picking a plant growth gives a piece of dry fibre as well as its wood; and a book or scroll is torn up for 2 lb of paper ("Tear it up for tinder") where it gave a load of wood. Coal is its own fuel now, kept in the yard's heaps, not turned into loads of wood.
  • Every fuel but coal is kept in the woodpile and counts against its room. The campfire's and the woodpile's info boxes list what there is to burn, by kind. Two dials went: stoke_fuel (the fuels tab says it now, per fuel) and coal_wood.

v2.172.249

2026-10-16 15:45 EDT

A stair down on the lowest story is a real shaft at last, not a black disc.

The user, with a picture of a pitch-black round hole: "this is still a problem". A stair down's shaft was drawn only as deep as the pit dug under it, and the pit stops at the bedrock -- so on a story whose floor stands a metre or two above the bedrock the shaft was one metre deep or none at all, and with none at all there was nothing to see but the black disc that closes the bottom. The shaft is now drawn three metres down (stair_pit) whatever was dug: the floor under the hole is left out of the picture -- at the foot of the pit, or where nothing could be dug, under the stair itself -- so the stone walls, the steps and the newel go on down into the dark, and the light from the story below glints at the bottom as it does elsewhere. The stone deck round the hole is laid on every stair down now, not only over a dug pit. And the stair's stone is lit as everything else in the world is (it missed the sky's soft light from above, so it read darker than the floor around it).

v2.172.248

2026-10-16 12:13 EDT

Everything carried has a real weight and takes real room; the store, woodpile and larder hold only so much; the march is packed by weight and room.

The user chose: carrying limited by "Weight and room"; the store, woodpile and larder "Limited by room"; a load of wood weighs 12 lb; food and salvage as "real items".

  • Weights and room, in the sheet (the goods tab has a litres column now): a piece of what was dug 15.6 litres (a 25 cm cube); a load of wood 12 lb and 17 litres; a ration 1 lb and 1 litre (meat brought home weighs a pound a ration, not four); a hide or leather 10 lb, cloth 2, bone 1, metal 8 for half a litre, and so on. A piece of kit carried rather than worn takes 0.7 litres a pound (kit_l_per_lb), a rolled bedroll 8 lb and 20 litres.
  • The store, woodpile and larder are limited by room: the woodpile holds a cubic metre (woodpile_l, about 58 loads), the larder 200 litres (200 rations), the store 300 litres of kit and salvage; before one is built, 100 litres of each is kept loose by the fire (camp_loose_l). Whatever will not fit is set down in a heap beside it -- stacked wood, sacks of rations, bundles of salvage, kit laid on the floor -- with a line in the ledger saying so, and is fetched back in only when there is room. Their info boxes say how full they are: "full: 140 of 300 litres". Nothing is fetched home to a full store.
  • What was dug lives in the yard: every heap within 6 m of the fire (yard_m) is the camp's own stock, to build with, and a piece brought home is set down there rather than vanishing into a count.
  • The march is packed thing by thing, by weight and by room: each crawler shoulders up to twice what they carry unburdened, less what they wear (march_lb_mult), in the room of their arms (15 litres, carry_l_base) and their packs (12 litres a point of carry, carry_l_per_carry); the sledge 400 lb and 300 litres, a wagon 1,200 lb and 1,000 litres, a big tame beast 150 lb and 120 litres. A pack's burden is its real weight. What was dug is not shouldered: it stays in its heaps, to be moved by order. Whatever is left over is bundled by the old fire as before, each bundle weighed for what is in it.
  • Measured on seed 3: 150 loads of wood, 400 rations, 60 hides and 70 pieces of rock added to a camp with nothing built yet -- 5 loads of wood, 100 rations and 12 hides stayed by the fire, the rest went to heaps beside it, and the rock to two heaps in the yard. Packing for the march then filled three packs with 51, 27 and 22 lb of rations by room and left five loads of wood and four bundles of hides by the old fire.

v2.172.247

2026-10-16 11:45 EDT

Walls, ramps and a new "Fill this square with earth" are built from the heaps, carried to the work.

The user: walls, ramps and filled holes use up what was dug. Until now a dry-stone wall or a ramp was paid out of the camp's stock wherever it stood, as though the stone walked there by itself. Now a wall (64 pieces of rough stone), a ramp (32 of dirt) and the new fill (64 of dirt: a square filled with a block of packed earth, fill_dirt, the work Labouring) are built of the pieces on the heaps beside them, and of the camp's stock only after those. While one waits, what it is short of is carried to it from the nearest heaps -- a heap move of just so many pieces, by hand or on the sledge, set down beside the work and never on its square -- and digging for it starts only when no heap of the stuff is left anywhere. The cost shown on a blueprint counts the dug pieces lying in heaps as well as the camp's stock, because those are what it will be built of. Measured on seed 3: a fill laid 8 m from a heap of 64 pieces of dirt, the heap carried over in four trips, the square filled, and no dirt left over or missing.

v2.172.246

2026-10-16 11:41 EDT

A heap can be ordered moved: pick it, choose "Move this heap", then pick where it goes.

The user: piles "can be ordered to be moved by player", and from the first message, "need the sledge to move them". A heap's order is "Move this heap"; a pale ghost of the heap follows the pointer until a square is picked, and "Put the heap here" sends whoever is best placed. By hand it goes a piece a trip -- 42 lb of earth, 63 of sand, 84 of rock, so a crawler of middling Might is slowed under rock. When the camp has a sledge nobody is using, whoever moves the heap fetches the sledge first and drags up to 400 lb a trip on it (sledge_lb: 9 pieces of earth, 6 of sand, 4 of rock), feeling a quarter of it as burden (sledge_drag), and leaves the sledge by the new heap when the work is done. What is set down goes on the heap there and spills to the squares round it when that is full. The move is a job of its own in the order of work, "Move a heap" (Labouring). Measured on seed 3: 20 pieces of earth moved, 20 arrived, by hand one a trip, by sledge nine.

v2.172.245

2026-10-16 11:31 EDT

Digging is slow and heavy now: a block comes away in 64 pieces, each set down on a heap, and the block shrinks as it goes.

The user: "rocks, dirt, sand: these things are super heavy. I want to do conservation of mass and volume ... I also want digging to be a slow expensive process", and their answers: a cubic metre of dirt weighs 2,700 lb, sand 1.5x, stone 2x; a block is dug out in 64ths; a cubic metre takes 2 hours of dirt, 1 of sand, 6 of stone; the spoil "take[s] up the same amount of room ... but look[s] like a loose pile (different for each material) spilling over the edge of the tile", put "in the nearest available tile that doesn't block any important paths", "Piles have 4 movement cost"; a square holds a block's worth; walls, ramps and fills use up what was dug; a part-dug block shrinks.

  • One throw, one piece. Each throw at a dig takes as long as a 25 cm cube of it takes to come away (dirt 112 seconds, sand 56, rock 5 1/2 minutes, coal 3 3/4), and a throw that succeeds takes one piece off -- 64 to a block, 32 to a half block, a slope or steps, and 32 to cut a ramp or steps into a block. Failing still teaches (rule 1); it only takes longer. The four hours are dials in the sheet (dig_hours_dirt, dig_hours_sand, dig_hours_stone, dig_hours_coal), and so is the 64 (block_pieces).
  • The block shrinks. A block part way dug is drawn as the pieces still in it, in its own stuff, the ones nearest the side it is dug from gone first and the top of each layer before the bottom; the rock round it is drawn as if it were open, so where the pieces went shows. It can still be pointed at.
  • The heap. Each piece is set down on the nearest heap of the same stuff with room in it, or else the nearest square that is flat, dry, not where the digging is done from, not under the camp or anything standing, and never the only way through. A square holds 64 pieces (pile_max). A heap is drawn as big as what is in it, and a full one spreads past its square: earth a lumpy dark mound with clods tumbled down it, sand a smooth pale cone, rock and coal a heap of angular lumps on the grit that came off them.
  • Weights. A piece of dirt weighs 42 lb, sand 63, rock 84, coal 45 (the goods tab).
  • A heap costs 4 to walk over (path_cost_pile), and the walk now pays what the cost view has always shown. The inclines (2) and growths underfoot (+2) the user asked for in v2.172.226 and v2.172.229 were only ever in the picture: the walk itself left them out.
  • Only what the camp is short of goes home. Every heap used to be carried back to camp a load at a time; with 64 pieces of up to 84 lb to a block, that would be a life's work. Now a heap is fetched from only while a blueprint or an order waits on what it is, a piece a trip, and the rest stays where it was dug.
  • What is built of it is counted in pieces too: a block of dry-stone wall takes 64 pieces of rough stone (a whole block's worth, what digging one gives), a ramp 32 of dirt, a cairn 16 of rock, a kiln 24 of rock and 8 of dirt, a raised bed 16 of dirt. A game saved before this counts what it had dug at 64 pieces a load.
  • Still to come: ordering a heap moved, the sledge hauling pieces, and real weight and room for everything else carried.

v2.172.244

2026-10-16 10:38 EDT

Someone laid up is dragged on a travois, not a sled or a sledge.

The user: "For a laid up person it should be a travois". v2.172.243 had renamed the laid-up crawler's sled to a sledge, to have one word; the right word for it is travois, and the sledge stays the camp's own, for hauling its things. The job is "Build a travois", the chronicle says "lashes together a travois", "takes up the travois poles" and "is harnessed to the travois", and the three dials about it in the sheet say travois too. It is drawn as one now: two long poles, their front ends up at the hands of whoever drags it and their back ends trailing on the ground, three crosspieces and a hide lashed between them, with the one on it lying along it -- where it was runners and a bed of poles, a little sledge.

v2.172.243

2026-10-16 08:37 EDT

The info box ends with Illumination and Elevation; one word, sledge; a boat hull is a sledge; no lingering map marks; only small things where the company wakes; the menu opens upward on a big screen.

  • The picked thing's box now closes on "Illumination n · Elevation n m" in the low, quiet spot where it used to say "light n" (the user pointed at that spot); the elevation is no longer up in the second line.
  • The sled built for someone laid up is called a sledge too: "Build a sledge", "lashes together a sledge", "the sledge's rope". There was one order for a sled and another for a sledge, for what reads as the same thing.
  • A Boat Hull is shaped like a sledge (two runners and a plank deck) and stands in for one, as a found cart does, where it was a wagon-shaped cart before.
  • The map no longer leaves a mark where a creature was last seen: those stayed for ten minutes of game time and read as ghosts that never went. A creature is marked only while it is in sight.
  • A new game puts only small and tiny creatures in the room the company wakes in.
  • On a big screen, the menu button opens the rest of the buttons upward from the strip at the bottom left, which stays where it was, and a list opened from it (Orders, Rooms, Company, Bestiary, Debug, Sounds) stands just above the strip at the left.

v2.172.242

2026-10-16 08:32 EDT

Halls and doorways at the edge of an area no longer end in a ledge you cannot reach.

The doorway corridors that join one area of the labyrinth to the next were dug at the height both sides agreed on, but the hall from each one into its own area started a square further in, at the height of the room it was heading for -- so wherever the two heights differed, the doorway was left a metre up or down from its own hall: a ledge nobody could climb to, and the next area beyond it cut off. Measured on seeds 1, 3 and 7: the areas next to the start were 0-8 squares reachable out of ~1,300 in four of twelve cases. The hall now starts from the doorway's own end, so it climbs from the doorway's height. And a new last step joins anything still cut off: a doorway or a stair landing that cannot be walked to from the rest of its area gets a passage a square wide dug through the rock to the nearest flat ground, climbing a metre a square on its straight stretches (5-7 such passages per five areas). Only rock, and ground nobody could reach anyway, is dug. What is still out of reach afterwards is the sealed rooms, on purpose.

v2.172.241

2026-10-14 15:18 EDT

The cost view marks ground nobody can walk to. The user, of a strip of numbers in the dark: "What is going on here? appears to be a ledge that didn't get connected? and would never be found by the player". From the picture alone it cannot be told: the cost view numbers every square somebody could stand on, seen or not, and a straight hall two squares wide seen corner-on reads as a diagonal strip. So the view now says which: a square the company cannot walk to from where they are is shown grey with a cross (×1), worked out again every two seconds while the view is on; a sealed room's squares stay violet. Searched for in two test worlds (seeds 1 and 3, the pieces round the start and theirs): every stretch of ground nobody could reach was a sealed room, a doorway running on into a piece not made yet, or a whole piece reached only through one not made yet -- nothing stranded.

v2.172.240

2026-10-14 15:14 EDT

A ramp in a corner between two banks is an inside corner ramp. The user, of a plain slope sitting where the ground was a metre up on two sides that meet: "error making this an inside corner ramp" -- the ground shaped wrong. The rule made an inner corner only where the ramps on both its low sides rose exactly toward the banks, which almost never happens: counted on seed 3, 80 slopes stood in such a corner (17 on seed 1), each showing a gap against one bank. Now a slope rising toward one of the two banks is made the inner corner -- in a hall as in a room, when the piece is made and again whenever the ground round it changes -- wherever the way on down past its low corner stays open (both low sides open at its height, the square across the low corner floor or a ramp). Left: 41 on seed 3 and 7 on seed 1, nearly all with solid rock on a low side, where a corner piece would cut the way down off. Rooms reached: 45 and 41, the same as before.

v2.172.239

2026-10-14 15:02 EDT

An arch shows only once somebody has seen it. The user: "arches are visible when they should not be" -- the arches over a sealed room's ways in were drawn from the moment the piece was made, floating in the black of ground nobody had seen, and they ignored the cut that keeps a story's upper floors out of the picture. Each face of an arch now waits until the ground in front of it has been seen -- the hall's face when the hall is, the room's face only once the room is open and seen -- and is cut like everything else. Seed 1: none of 18 faces shown at the start; a face shows once the squares in front of it are seen.

v2.172.238

2026-10-14 14:49 EDT

The shaft of a stair down is seen only through its hole; nothing is left standing where a stair stands; the asks sit beside the ledger. The user: "I don't want to see the inside of the spiral stairway shaft from under the ground, only from the round hole at 'surface level' - can you mask out the underneath part?" -- where the picture leaves out the rock in front of the shaft, its whole round wall showed through the ground beside it. Now the hole marks the screen where it is in sight (a stencil: the page asks for one), and the shaft's wall, steps, black bottom and the light from below are drawn only there; the kerb and the deck round it are drawn as anything is. And "there are two voxels of 'rubble' protruding into the stairwell": a hall aimed at the stair room's middle had left a block of its own where the stair stands; the three-by-three is cleared from the floor three metres up when the piece is made, so a saved game's stairs are cleared too. On a PC the stack of "Asked..." orders sits to the left of the ledger, along the bottom.

v2.172.237

2026-10-14 14:33 EDT

Arches over every way into a sealed room, rewards inside, a rewards tab in the sheet; loot on the ground is the real thing, and sparkles. The user: "archway to sealed room still not appearing. perhaps you must put arches on the inside and outside of all hallways connectors to sealed rooms. also put rewards in them. we will need a place in the sheet to monitor and modify the reward level of various areas like sealed rooms, lairs, biome hazards, the other 'gated' rooms, etc". The wall that seals a room was only a different stuff -- a texture among textures, never a shape. Now every run of it has an arch on both faces, the hall's and the room's: two dressed posts and a ring of voussoirs standing proud of the wall, in stone where the walls are worked, rough stone in natural rock, a timber frame in earth. Made again with the piece every time, so a saved game's sealed rooms have them too; dig the seal out and the arch frames the way in.

A new tab, World - rewards: for each kind of place, how many pieces of kit lie there (a fraction is the chance of one more), what share are good ones (a weapon, armour, a light, fine work), how worn, and the chance of something old to look into. Four rows, each wired: sealed (2 pieces, mostly good, a little worn, an even chance of a relic), gated (1 good piece, like new -- it was knobs gate_reward, which is gone), lair (a creature's nest, searched: half a chance of a well-worn piece), hazard (a room whose floor bites: a piece and a small chance of a relic). New places only: what lies in rooms already made stays as it was.

"apply the sparkles to untouched loot laying on the ground. Make the loot use the real model of what it is.": a piece of kit on the floor is built as a crawler would wear or hold it, everything but it left out, laid down at its own size (seed 1: 12 of 12 pieces their own model, none a box); a locked chest is still a chest, and a bundle left at an old camp still a bundle. Loot on the ground sparkles like a prop with something in it, all but the company's own bundles.

v2.172.236

2026-10-14 14:27 EDT

Folded cloth looks folded again; the stair's lid and seam are gone, and its corners are cut across but never stood on; an order's chip takes you to it; the picked thing's paper and the menu move on a PC. The user, all at once. The Folded Linen and Folded Cloth props were drawn as sacks -- three round lumps -- and now have a shape of their own, a stack of folds each a little askew (props tab: shape 'folded'). A stair down's kerb was a closed cylinder, so it had a lid across the shaft with a slice missing at its mouth; it is two open walls and a lip now. The stone deck round the shaft reaches past the hole's edge, over the seam. And "I said I wanted the corners of stairwells crossable diagonally, not walkable (opposite of how wall corners block diagonal movement)": the four corner squares of a stair's three-by-three are no longer somewhere to stand, up or down, but a diagonal step that cuts across one is allowed where a wall corner would forbid it (seed 5: every corner of all twelve stairs blocked to stand on, every one cut across). Tapping an "Asked..." chip at the top centres the view on where that order is. On a PC the picked thing's paper opens where a crawler's sheet does -- the two are never open together -- and the bar of buttons sits at the bottom left, everything that hung below it moved up to the top. The roadmap has the fuel system.

v2.172.235

2026-10-14 13:29 EDT

The changelog has a page of its own, beside the game. The user: "rule: send me a link to changelog and master sheet with every game build link". publish.py now writes the changelog as a page (tools/changelog_page.py: every release a card, newest first, its version and when it went up) and puts it up with each build at https://myrmecbruh.github.io/Project-Crawlers/changelog.html. The time a release goes up is now stamped before that page is made, so the newest entry carries its own. The twelve entries before this one (v2.172.223 - 234) had been written without the version's heading form the stamp looks for, so none was stamped; they are put right, timed by when each went up, and a superseded second entry for v2.172.225 is gone. Nothing in the game changed.

v2.172.234

2026-10-14 13:27 EDT

A saved game's spiral stairs are brought up to date.

Reported with a picture of a square shaft on story 0 -- the picture was of v2.172.231 still open, before the round shaft. But a saved game would not have shown the round one even after loading the new version: the ground of a piece the company has been through is made again from the seed every time it is loaded, while each stair's own record -- how deep its shaft is, the squares nobody may stand on, the deck round the hole -- was kept from when it was first made. So the shaft would have been drawn two metres deep in a hole three metres deep, and the stair's corners kept as squares nobody could stand on. Now, when a piece known from before is made again, its spiral stairs' records are brought up to match the stair as it is made now. Save and load again on seed 3: no error, the depths and decks as made.

v2.172.233

2026-10-14 13:21 EDT

Sparkles on what still has something to give.

"give any unclaimed props with a 'use' entry faint rising sparkles": a prop with a use in the props tab that has not been used yet (searched, looked over, eaten from) and is not taken up by the camp has five faint gold motes drifting up out of it, each fading in and out as it rises a metre, twinkling a little. All of them are one set of points drawn in a single go, refreshed once a second, at most 120 props at a time on the story in view. knobs sparkle (0.5) is how bright; 0 turns them off.

v2.172.232

2026-10-14 12:00 EDT

A round stair, a real shaft, bigger stair rooms; a block's elevation.

"round the shaft of the spiral stair, I don't want it square. therefore, crawlers can walk diagonally across the corners. sometimes stair rooms can be 7x7 and contain props also." The stair is round, 1.3 m across from the newel to the steps' ends: nobody stands on its middle square or the four beside it, and the four corner squares of its three-by-three are floor again -- over a shaft going down, a stone deck round the hole (a list of squares the ground rule treats as floor though the rock under them is gone, drawn in the landing's own floor picture). A stair room is five across, and one in three is seven; a seven is furnished like a shaft hall (the props tab's 'shaft hall' rows, and the kind of place's own: hall, cave, mine or crypt) round its edge, never on the five squares round the stair.

"sometimes the down stair just looks like this [a square black pit] ... I want it to be a real shaft, that you can peek into, that fades to black 3m down, with light (if any) from below glinting off the stone walls of the shaft." The shaft is now 3 m deep (knobs stair_pit 3, never into the bedrock) and round: a stone-brick wall seen from inside, darker with depth and black at the bottom, and over it the same wall again lit in the colour and strength of the light at the stair's foot one story down, strongest low in the shaft and flickering as that light does. A low round kerb stands round it, open at the mouth (wider at a through stair's two mouths). A stair down too near the bedrock for a shaft still shows its kerb and its dark.

"when a block is selected, list its elevation similar to how its illumination is listed": a block's line now reads "Illumination n · Elevation n m" -- the height of its top above the story's bedrock.

v2.172.231

2026-10-14 11:30 EDT

Blocks overhead can be dug; the light up a stair down is the light at its foot.

"they must be able to reach these to dig out: blocks 1m off the ground at the top of steps": a block was worked only from a square beside it within a metre up or down. A block whose foot is a metre over the ground leaves the square under it -- and the top of a flight of steps under it -- without the two metres of headroom a square needs to be stood on, so nobody could reach it. Now a block is also worked reaching up from the ground two metres below it, beside it or directly under it.

"make the spiralstairdown lighting based on whatever the lighting is like at the base of the stair one story down": the glow up a stair down is no longer the colour of the kind of place below (the themes tab's glow column is gone again). It is as bright as the game's own light on the square the stair comes out on below -- nothing at all when that is dark -- in the colour of whatever lights it most there (a campfire, a sconce, somebody's lamp, a tame glowing creature, else the faint green of glowing growth), flickering as that light flickers.

v2.172.230

2026-10-14 11:26 EDT

Spiral stairs: up, down, and through.

The user: "make 'stairs up' and 'stairs down' spiral staircases. I think this will need to be a 3³ impassible prefab to be believable. Use the stone block textures. 'Up' will need to fade off to not get in the way, 'down' should descend into darkness with a faint flickering light coming from below (colored based on the 'down biome')"; and then "make a third, continuous up/down spiral stair with landing". A stairwell's landing is now five squares across; the three in its middle are the stair, which nobody stands on, and the way onto it is the square beside it on one side. Sixteen wedge steps a turn round a newel post, in the stone-brick picture. Going up, they climb three metres and fade out from a metre and a half. Going down, the floor under the stair is open as a shaft (knobs stair_pit, 2 m, never into the bedrock), with a low stone kerb round it open at the mouth; the steps turn down into it, darker with each one, onto black, and the light of whatever kind of place is below flickers faintly up -- a new glow column in the themes tab: amber for ruined halls, pale blue for caves, lamp orange for a mine, a sick green for a crypt. And now and then (knobs stair_through, half the odd stories) a shaft carries straight on from the one below it: one spiral up and down through the story, one landing, the way down and the way up side by side at its mouth.

The larger landings needed the stairwells moved: the stair up and the stair down on one story could land within a few squares of each other, and an overlapping landing was left out -- its stair went nowhere (seed 3: four of six stairs joined before, two after, until the fix). Now a shaft from an odd story stands in the eastern half of the piece and one from an even story in the western, the even ones taking the north and south quarters by turns, so the two on any story never meet. Seeds 3 and 5, seven stories each: every stair whose other story was made is joined, its mouth is ground, its three-by-three is not. The two ends of a shaft now find each other by the stairwell's middle rather than by the square stood on. A piece first made before this version keeps its old stair and its old place, and so does the other end of any shaft whose far piece is one of those, so a saved game's stairs still meet.

v2.172.229

2026-10-14 11:08 EDT

Inclines cost 2, and are on the map and the cost view.

"inclines do not appear on minimap or move-cost-view. they should cost 2": a walk counts an incline -- a slope, either half of a gentle ramp, steps, a corner ramp -- as 2 (knobs path_cost_incline 1, on top of the 1 every square costs). The map left them out because only open squares over ground went on it, and an incline is the square itself; now every square a crawler can stand on does, and one seen before this, beside a drawn square, is drawn the next time the map is. v2.172.228

2026-10-14 10:51 EDT

A light is a flame at a height, and falls off as the crow flies.

Reported: black diamonds in the floor, and walls seen only to half their height, "where only the bottom half of walls are revealed. I assume this is because carried light sources are carried under 1m high? ... I want this to be an organic solution, not a hack." The cause was the mechanical light's arithmetic, not the height alone: it stepped down by one a square at a time, straight on or straight up, from the floor-level square the light was in. So every light was a diamond -- the dark wedges between two lights are the black diamonds, never seen because no light ever reached them -- and the wall's second metre cost one more step than its first, so at the edge of the light only the foot was lit, and sight is light. Now a light is a point at a real height -- carried at 1 m (knobs light_carry_height), a campfire's at 0.5, a sconce's at 1.6, anything glowing on the ground at 0.3 -- and it steps down by one for each metre from that point as the crow flies, so a light held at a metre is the same distance from both metres of a wall beside it, and a light is round. It still goes only through what is not solid, and the way round counts whenever it is the longer (in squares, allowing for going corner-wise), so it does not leap through a wall to the room behind. A light's reach is now a circle of its own level in metres rather than a diamond of one less: it reaches a little further on the slant than it did.

Sealed rooms, and walls that give them away.

Now and then (knobs hidden_room_chance, half the pieces) a room at the end of a single hall -- never the first room, never a stairwell's -- is walled up where its halls come in, head high. The wall that seals it is not quite like the walls around it, and what it is depends on them: a Bricked-up Arch (chiseled stone bricks) where the walls are worked stone, an Old Rockfall (cobblestone) in natural rock, Backfilled Earth (plain dirt) in earth or sand -- three new rows in the materials tab, with the same tags as the walls they stand in, so hovering one names it. Dig it out and the room is there. Measured, not hoped: the seal is taken down again if it would cut any other room off, or if the room can still be walked to some other way; the room behind is kept rather than filled in as unreachable. Seeds 1, 3 and 5: one sealed room each (a rockfall, two bricked-up arches), none reachable. Chosen on dice of its own, so nothing else about a piece moves -- except the two extra halls that join random rooms, which now never join the sealed one.

v2.172.225

2026-10-14 09:34 EDT

The walk, after Richard Williams.

The user sent Williams' walk charts (contact, down, passing, up from the side; the weight shift from behind) and asked that their notes be read. From the side: the knee bearing the weight gives just after the foot is put down, so the body is lowest at the Down, a quarter of a step after contact, and straightens through passing (knobs walk_down); the arms swing a quarter of a step behind the legs, furthest out at the Down, the forward arm bending at the elbow and the back one hanging straight (knobs arm_swing; it used to bend the arm going back). From behind, the three notes: "each time we raise a foot it thrusts the weight of our body forward and to the side over the other foot" -- the hips' slide is now complete by the Down and held through the passing position, rather than peaking at passing; "the lifted passing leg causes the weight to drop on its side" -- the pelvis tips down on the side of the passing leg, turning about the hip that bears the weight (knobs hip_tilt); and "the shoulders mostly oppose the hips" -- which needed the figure to bend at the waist: the body is now a pelvis and a chest, the head and arms hang on the chest, and the body's skin is blended between the two across the waist, so the shoulders tip down on the side of the planted foot while the hips tip the other way (knobs shoulder_tilt). The head goes with the shoulders. No ankle yet, so the heel cannot rise off the ground at the Up.

v2.172.224

2026-10-14 09:10 EDT

The walk: straighter knees, no folding legs, weight from side to side.

Asked: knees too bent on flat ground; legs flipping back over the body going up a slope; heads bobbing forward a quarter too much; "still needs side-to-side weight shifting". Measured first (tests/_gaitstat.mjs, seed 5, 900 frames, every walking crawler): the knee bearing weight was bent 28.6 degrees (median) on flat ground, and 134 frames had a leg folded or flipped. Cause of both: the hips were set from a stride formula and then dragged down by any planted foot, even one left far behind, and nothing stopped them sinking to the height of a foot stepping up a ledge, at which point the leg's sum had no answer and the thigh swung round. Now the hips ride over the foot bearing the weight on a near-straight leg (knobs stance_reach), a foot far behind no longer drags them, and they never come within knobs leg_fold of either foot. After: 19.8 degrees median on flat ground, 0 folded or flipped frames. The hips now slide toward the planted foot each step (knobs hip_sway, 4 cm each way) with the body leaning over it and the head kept level; the old 1.4 cm sway is kept only for the walk without planted feet. The head's share of the new weight springs is a quarter less.

v2.172.223

2026-10-14 00:21 EDT

Weight.

Asked: "I need more secondary motion, weight". The head, shoulders and arms are now carried by the body rather than glued to it: each is a damped spring driven by how the body actually moves (its speed along its facing, its rise and fall, its turning). Setting off, the head and arms trail; pulling up, they swing on forward, overshoot and settle; turning, the body leans into the turn and the head leads it; each footfall jolts down through the head and elbows. Only rotations above the hips, so the planted feet are untouched. A load makes it heavier. Standing still it settles to nothing, so idle poses are unchanged. New dial: knobs weight (1; 0 is the old glued-on look).

v2.172.205

2026-10-13 09:58 EDT

Water, first part: springs and still water, a bath and a quick wash, and what dirty water does. The user's choices: clean water comes from springs and the filter; still water starts dirty; washing in dirty water cleans less, may sicken and disheartens; a quick wash at a basin and a full bath are two acts.

  • Springs. Each body of water is fed by a spring now and then (spring_chance, on a hash of where it lies, so nothing else in the piece moves). Spring water is a material of its own, Spring Water, tagged clean, and is drawn clear blue; every other pool is still water, tagged dirty, and drawn murky. Springs are bluer on the map too. Whether water is water, and whether it is clean, is read off the materials' tags.
  • A quick wash and a bath. Only topping up, a crawler takes a quick wash at a basin (the filter, or a found basin, tub or trough): wash_quick_seconds, a little cleaner, and only damp (wash_quick_wet). When it presses, they bathe: in a spring if one is known, else at a basin, else in still water. A bath is the new bathing row -- fully clean, some sanity back -- and soaks them through as wading always did.
  • Dirty water. Bathing in still water adds the in dirty water row (cleans less, costs sanity) and a Persisting throw at sick_need; failing it is a sickness (sick_minutes): the sick row wears them down, they walk at sick_slow, they look paler (sick_pallor), and they carry the tag sick until it passes. Failing the throw teaches Persisting, as any failure does.

Still to come in this pass: the waterskin, drinking folded into what there already is, the filter cleaning water carried to it, and a washbasin to build.

Whoever digs now knows the hole they made and the rock round it, lit or not, so the tunnel and the next block are drawn as they go.
  • Low walls have tops (the user: "look at the tops of the walls"). A wall stands two metres over its floor and its top metre fades, so a wall's top was never drawn -- but since v2.172.188 a wall lower than two metres no longer fades, and with no top it stood open like a hollow box, showing the inside of the rock. A seen block whose top is under two metres above a floor beside it now has its top drawn.

v2.172.197

2026-10-11 23:47 EDT
  • A light that burns for under two minutes says how many seconds (a torch: 90 seconds), not "2 minutes".

v2.172.196

2026-10-11 23:46 EDT
  • The build and make options say exactly what they do (the user: "building and workbench crafting menu option mouseovers should tell the player exactly how the options mechanically function"). Hovering a build option in the order ring says what the thing does, with the sheet's own numbers (a workbench takes 1 success off each throw of making and mending; a root cellar stops a larder over 8 rations losing one every 30 minutes; a kiln turns 2 dirt and 1 fuel into a brick whenever the camp has them to spare...), where it can go, what it costs and that the blueprint waits for what the camp lacks, and the work: how many throws of which skill (and which two attributes), how many successes each needs with the tool and without, and which kit counts as that tool. A make option says what the kit does when worn -- its slot and weight, what work it is a tool for, its weapon grade, its light and how long it burns, its armour, warmth, dryness, carrying and standing, each with what that number does -- then its cost, whether it needs a workbench, and the work. Every number is read off the sheet when the tooltip is asked for.

v2.172.195

2026-10-11 23:39 EDT
  • An armour stand can be taken up as the camp's store too (the user's edit on the sheet page).

v2.172.194

2026-10-11 23:37 EDT
  • Inclines answer to the inclines touching them (the user: "inclines must remain context sensitive to other inclines touching them and change shape accordingly"). The inner and outer corner pieces were worked out once, when a stretch of the labyrinth was made; a ramp dug or heaped beside another, a block dug from under one or a wall built across one left the corners round it as they were. Now every change to the ground asks the same question again of the squares round it: two ramps meeting at a right angle with the ground a metre up across the corner make an outer corner; banks a metre up on two sides with ramps rising to them on the other two make an inner corner; and a corner whose ramps or banks have gone turns back into flat ground, or a ramp up to the bank it still has. The squares touching the change answer always, the rest of a room's inside too; never a room's middle square.
  • A job nobody can do stays on the list until somebody can (the user: "if nobody can execute/reach a job, it should still remain until someone can"). A job nobody had the skill for was struck off (with "Nobody here has the skill to ..."), and came back only once somebody could; now it stays, said once, waiting. A load of salvage nobody got to in time stays too, for whoever can reach it, and the camp looks for the same thing elsewhere as well. Jobs struck off in a saved game come back.
  • Books burn (the user: "book props can serve as fuel"). A book, ledger, letter, scroll or decree is a load of fuel once it has been read: torn up for the fire by hand ("Tear it up for the fire"), or by the company when the fire wants feeding -- never before it has been read, so nothing in it is lost. One burnt or soaked through gives nothing. Their stuff reads "paper".

v2.172.193

2026-10-11 23:28 EDT
  • A tool rack and a weapon rack can be taken up as the camp's store (the user's two edits on the sheet page: serves = store).

v2.172.192

2026-10-11 23:25 EDT
  • The "new version is up" notice has a cross to wave it away (the user: "allow Xing out of new version popup"); it stays away until a newer version still is up.
  • Rooms are bigger (the user: "increase initial room generation size: 6-18m"): a room's sides now run 6 to 18 metres, from 5 to 12 (room_min, room_max in the sheet).
  • No more high ledges (the user: "'high ledge' doesn't make sense given how we can already manage climbing and digging. remove it and add 'Heavy doors' (expensive smashing labor or difficult lockpicking) and 'Cave in' (very heavy lifting, or long mining job)"). A heavy door -- black oak bound in iron -- is smashed in (Labouring, 8 units of work, an axe helps) or its lock picked (Tinkering, hard: 3 successes with picks, 6 without). A cave-in -- great slabs of the roof heaped to head height -- is heaved aside (Labouring, very heavy: 4 successes with something to haul with, 5 without) or mined through (Labouring, 12 units, a pick near enough needed). The company takes whichever way somebody among them is best placed for, by their skill against what it needs with the tools they have, and the shorter work on a tie. In a saved game a high ledge is a cave-in now. The ladder you build is no longer shadowed by the ledge's own "raise a ladder" work, which had the same name.

v2.172.191

2026-10-11 23:22 EDT
  • Planter boxes get used (the user: "planter boxes don't get used?", and chose "cuttings from picking"). A planter box was planted with a cutting, a cutting came from picking a wild plant, and the wild plants were all replaced by the carpet of growths -- so no camp ever had a cutting, and the planting and harvesting work had been taken off the list. Now: a box standing empty sends somebody to pick the nearest growth that does not sting, and picking any growth while a box stands empty brings home a cutting of it -- a throw at the planting task, so it can fail and teach. The cutting is planted in the box, where the growth rises out of the soil over the time one takes to grow back wild (30 minutes), and is harvested for what picking it gives (a fungus is food, a plant is wood), times the bed yield; then it grows again. Planting and harvesting are back on the work list, in a saved game too.

v2.172.190

2026-10-11 23:16 EDT
  • Nobody digs out the only way through (the user: "sometimes they dig the ground out of their only exit, presumably for the dirt"). When the camp wants dirt, it digs the nearest earth it can; a floor square only counted as a little further off than a wall. The hole is a metre deep and nobody climbs a metre without a ramp, so a floor square in a passage was the end of the passage. A floor square is now dug only if the ground round it still joins up without it; walls are dug as before.

v2.172.189

2026-10-11 23:11 EDT
  • The dead are buried (the user: "burial of the dead?"). Three things kept a body lying where it fell. Burying was 55th of the work list, under hunting and butchering, so the living searched shelves and wardrobes first; it is now next after caring for the wounded, in the sheet and in a saved game. A death is what most often makes a camp unsafe, and breaking camp called off every job -- the burial of the one who had just died with them; now the company buries its dead before it goes, as it packs. And a body whose burial was called off or never put up gets one again, wherever it lies. Checked on seeds 1-3, a crawler dying by the fire: before, seed 1's lay unburied for good (the death set the company moving, which called the burial off) and seeds 2 and 3 took 2 to 4 minutes; now all three are under a cairn within 90 seconds.

v2.172.188

2026-10-11 18:44 EDT
  • The map: a click anywhere on it looks there, drawn or not (the user: "should be able to click anywhere on minimap to center. should not be able to select characters on minimap"). A square nobody has drawn yet used to do nothing; it is looked at from the height the view is at. Nobody is picked from the map any more.
  • What is picked grows back only from its own kind (the user: "plants should only regrow if: they are not wiped out and the local population does not exceed starting level. make regrowing slower"). A growth or a wild plant picked or grazed comes back only while others of its kind still grow in the same room; the last of a kind in a room, taken, is gone for good. Nothing new is ever added -- only what was taken comes back -- so a room never holds more than it began with. And slower: a growth takes 30 minutes to come back (was 10), a wild plant 75 (was 25), both in the sheet (growth_regrow, regrow_seconds).
  • Walls fade by what stands there now (the user: "if i build dry stone walls two tall, the top of the second should fade off. inversely, dug out hallways should not fade out only 1m up. the fading effect must be evaluated dynamically when terrain changes"). A wall fades over its top metre only when it stands the whole two metres over the floor in front of it, and anything lower never fades. A dry-stone wall built two tall has a top somebody could stand on, so it counted as a ledge and never faded; a wall lower than two metres faded over the whole of its height, gone a metre up. The fade is worked out from the ground each time a stretch is redrawn, which a change to the ground does at once.
  • Company is somebody to talk to (the user: "crawlers keep socializing even after they are left alone"). With nobody awake left close by, sitting for company ends: they had sat on alone, talking to nobody, their loneliness easing. And "chatting" is said only while the one they chat with is still there.

The walls fade off at the top so what is behind them can be seen -- but the pointer still took the faded part as solid wall, so a cirrus crystal floating a metre and more up, seen through it, answered as the wall. A faded wall top now lets the pointer through to whatever is behind it, and is the answer only when nothing is.

v2.172.175

2026-10-11 15:55 EDT
  • The picture keeps moving when the game cannot keep up (the user's frame: "the game itself 173.5 ms", 9 crawlers; "framerate rose drastically when they went to sleep"). The game's own time is mostly the crawlers' thinking -- more awake crawlers, more of it, which is why sleep helped -- and at speed it fed on itself: a slow frame left the next one more game time to catch up, so it did more steps and was slower still. Now the game gets step_budget_ms (20, new knob) of each frame to catch up in, and lets the rest go: at a speed the machine cannot keep up with, the game runs a little slower than asked instead of the picture stopping.
  • The light costs less than half what it did. Every step anybody carrying a light took spread the whole light map again from every source; now each source's spread is kept and only a source that moved (or the ground changing, or a piece being made or forgotten) is spread again. Measured (seed 1, 40 min in, 9 crawlers, 249 creatures): the light 0.43 -> 0.17 ms a look, a step and a look 1.79 -> 1.53 ms; and the same light to the square (tests/_lightsame.mjs: 60 looks, 136,265 lit squares, 0 different).
  • The freeze recorder's notes can be timed (window.__crumbProf), and the sight has notes of its own: tests/_stepcost.mjs says what a step costs, part by part.

A creature's walk went straight through whoever stood in the way; the step onto them was refused, and the same walk was planned again, for as long as they stood there. On seed 1 two tame cooers stopped 11 m from the fire, in the hall into camp, behind a crawler, and stayed there. Now a walk goes round anyone standing in the way when there is a way round, and only waits when there is not. Wild creatures walk the same way.

v2.172.165

2026-10-10 08:24 EDT
  • The thin dark lines down the walls (the user's screenshot: "Vertical lines ... They are not on the edges of wall blocks, they are about 15cm inside the edges"). Rock keeps a shadow shell a little way inside every face (v2.172.147), so a lamp's light cannot slip through rock nobody has seen. Where the rock turned in, the shell ran on under the next face so the two met; where it turned OUT -- an outside corner, a pillar, the end of a wall, a ledge's lip -- it also ran on to the very corner, and its edge stood exactly on the face round the corner, a face somebody sees. Every shadow-casting light then counted that edge as something in the way of the face it touched, and drew a hairline of shadow down it, one shell-depth in from the corner (0.19 m at the sheet's blur and shadow detail), flickering as the fire swayed and the shadow was redrawn every other frame. Found on seed 3's camp: a wall face at x 52, the line at 0.152 m from the corner at z 28, 18-42 px long and changing every frame. Now the shell stops the same distance short at an outside corner, and the two shells meet inside the rock; the line is gone from the same frames. Left: a short sliver at the edge of a half block's side (3.6 cm in), which is the half block itself, not the shell.

Pulling the shell back at outside corners let no light round them.

v2.172.164

2026-10-10 02:49 EDT
  • Feet planted when striding and creeping too. A crawler who makes a good Maneuvering throw strides for a few squares, and one sneaking creeps; both were still drawn the old sliding way, and between them they were most of the walking a measurement caught (frame to frame, the stiller foot moved 0.41 of what the body did with v2.172.163's footwork on, 0.36 with it off -- no better). Striding takes a longer swing with the hips a little lower, creeping a short one, crouched; their stride matches the legs the same way, and their footfalls follow. A foot coming back into use far from where it was last is put down under its hip, not swung across the room.
  • Measured after publishing (seed 3, the live camp, 60 drawn frames a run, frame to frame against how far the body moved; tests/_feet.mjs): the stiller foot moves 0.04 of the body's movement (median; 0.02 to 0.19 middle half) with the footwork on, 0.37 with it off (0.16 to 0.50); a foot all but still (under 0.15) in 71% of walking frames against 22%. The first two runs of the instrument were wrong and said so only by agreeing with each other: one compared frames three apart, longer than a step at this machine's frame rate, and read 0 both ways; one ran while the walkers it watched were mostly striding, which the footwork did not yet cover.

v2.172.163

2026-10-10 02:42 EDT
  • Feet planted (the user: "all animations should have feet planted on the floor - some walk cycles slide a bit, and their feet ignore the elevation of inclines" and "they also don't 'step up' when ascending stairs or half-blocks"). Each foot is now a point in the world. Bearing weight it stays where it was put; swinging, it travels from where it left the ground to where it will land, lifted over any step up in between; standing, it rests under its hip. Every foot stands on the ground as it really is under it -- a slope's height at that spot, a stair's tread, a half block's top -- and the hips ride on the feet, so stairs are climbed a step at a time. The legs bend to reach their feet (two-joint reach, hip and knee), and a heel or toe that would dip into the ground lifts the foot. Walking, carrying and standing; running, creeping, crawling, sitting and lying keep their own poses for now.
  • The walk's stride was the cause of the slide: half a turn of the stride covered L A pi of ground (0.92 m) where one leg's swing covers 2 L A (0.59 m), so a foot kept up with the body only in the middle of each step. The stride is 0.59 m now, steps come a little quicker, and the footfall sounds follow it.

v2.172.162

2026-10-10 02:24 EDT
  • A pile looks like what it is (the user: "piles of wood should look like planks. piles of fabric should look like folded cloth. etc etc"). Wood is planks laid two to a layer, each layer across the one below; cloth and wool are folded in a stack with the folded edge showing; iron and copper are ingots; bone is bones with their knobbed ends; fibre is bundles tied in the middle; leather is rolled hides; crystal a cluster of six-sided points with a faint glow of its own; chitin plates and shells overlap like scales; feathers fan out; charcoal is black sticks; earth and sand are heaps with a few clods; stone and coal stay lumps. One piece for each load, up to six, in the colour of the stuff it is (pine, oak, linen, iron...).

v2.172.158

2026-10-10 01:49 EDT
  • A place in the light pool that is lighting nothing no longer redraws its shadow. At the camp, with every hand light put out, the second shadow place kept drawing the scene six times every other frame for a lamp nobody held (found while hunting the user's "horizontal lines flickering on walls as crawlers move by"; logged per frame: slot 1 at intensity 0, castShadow on, redrawn on its turn). It is drawn again the frame it lights something.
  • Figures are drawn between their last two simulation STEPS, not their last two pictures. When a frame ran several steps (a slow frame, or the game sped up), the old in-between laid one step's share across all of them, so walkers moved in fits and starts and their shadows jumped on the walls. The step before the last is now taken as that share of the way along; a jump of more than 1.6 m is still drawn as a jump.

v2.172.157

2026-10-10 01:08 EDT
  • Looking over a study prop says what it gives (the user: "takes something from it -- What something?"): nothing in the hand; it steadies the mind, and the log says by how much ("... and their mind steadies for it (sanity +1)", or that it was steady already).
  • No overlapping letters in the title lettering (the user: "the 'be' in workbench is overlapped - why?"). Pixtura12 carries one kerning rule -- b, d, o, p, q followed by e, o, q are pulled 300 of 1200 units together, the biting curves of blackletter where round letters share a stroke -- and at 24 px that put 4 px of 'b' on top of 'e', and the same in 14 other pairs ('oo' in wood, door, rooms). Kerning is off for every font (* { font-kerning: none }; the font shorthand resets it on buttons, so it is set on everything), in the bestiary and the loading screen too.
  • Loading screen (the user: "keep version number top center. torch glow should not cut off. fade bottom of flame"): "Crawlers v..." at the top centre; the canvas is 96 pixels wide instead of 48, so the glow (38 pixels each way at its widest) is never clipped; the flame's foot narrows and fades into the rag over its lowest fifth instead of starting as a straight line.
  • A building keeps the turn it was given (the user: "blueprints ignore rotation on initial build"): the outline was turned, but the building going up was drawn from its job, which did not carry the turn, and that drawing stayed when it was finished. The job's turn is passed on, and a structure's turn is applied every frame.

v2.172.156

2026-10-10 01:03 EDT
  • The campfire's and the sconces' shadows move smoothly (the user: "stool shadows refresh very infrequently ... specifically from the campfire"). Their shadows were redrawn every second frame as asked, but the light itself only moved once a game second: each light's flicker and drift run on the game clock plus a seed to set them apart, and for a fire or a sconce that seed was the number of its square -- about 4.5e15, where a double can only count in whole units -- so 0.1 s of game time added to it was lost, and the flicker, the drift and every shadow of the fire jumped once a second. Measured with tests/_stoolshadow2.mjs (the view on a stool, seed 3): the fire's position and brightness changed on 1 frame in 10. The seed is the square's number modulo 997 now. Carried lights and creatures already used small seeds.
  • Drift is a tenth of flicker on every light but the glowing ones (the user: "set Sway to be 1/10 the rate of flicker on all lights except stuff with glow in the name"): candle, torch, campfire, sconce, candle cap and rushlight 0.1 m, the ember tin 0.06, the lantern 0.03; Glow Lamp, Glowmoss Wreath, Glowworm Jar, Glowpaint and Glow Gland stay still.

v2.172.153

v2.172.152

2026-10-09 22:49 EDT
  • The moonlight's shadow costs less (the user picked both: "the moon's shadow updates half as often" and "a coarser moon shadow, with blurrier edges"). moon_refresh 2 -> 4 and moon_shadow_size 1024 -> 512 (a quarter of the shadow pixels). And it is no longer drawn again on every frame the view moves -- which, riding a walking crawler, was every frame, so the turn-taking saved nothing then: its box (moon_shadow_reach each way) is bigger than the view, so it is drawn again early only when the view has slid so far that the view's edge would pass the edge of what was drawn (the room is the reach less half the view's ground footprint, worked out from the camera each frame).

v2.172.148

2026-10-09 21:43 EDT
  • Skylight and moonlight are one light (the user: "combine skylight and moonlight into one light with the properties of both to save performance (a bluish fill light with specularity and a half-strength angled shadow)"). One brightness (moon 1.6), one colour (moon_colour), one bearing and height: the part from the moon's direction lights what faces it, glints, and casts its shadow at moon_shadow 0.5 (half); moon_fill 0.3 of it reaches every surface evenly -- the old skylight, now in the moon's colour (0.3 of 1.6 keeps the old fill's brightness: #8a9ab8 at 0.72 is as bright as #9ab4ff at 0.49). The fill's shaping on props and built things (sky_form) now comes from the moon's side instead of a fixed direction, so a prop's lit top and sides agree with the moon's shadows. skylight and skylight_colour are gone from the sheet; the debug menu's Moonlight section carries brightness, fill, shadow, height, direction, glints, props lit from its side, ao on the fill, colour; exposure has its own section.
  • What it saves: next to nothing. The fill is three.js's ambient term -- one colour added in every surface's shader, no shadow, no extra pass -- so merging it saved no work; the moon's shadow is the cost (the scene drawn into a 1024 map every second frame) and it is drawn as before, only at half strength.

v2.172.147

2026-10-09 21:38 EDT
  • Light no longer peeks through rock (the user: "Still peeking under walls. Why?? weld the verts or something"). Not a crack between pieces -- two holes in what a shadow is made of. (1) A face of rock is only built where somebody has seen it, so a lamp shone straight through the unseen side of a wall, or down through the unseen face of a drop onto the floor below. Every unseen face of rock touching what has been seen now goes into an invisible shell (SHADE_LAYER, a material that passes no depth test on screen, so it is drawn only into shadows). (2) Rock is hollow to a shadow (only faces turned away from the light are drawn into it), so the soft edge's samples that slipped into a wall or into the ground under a lamp ran on inside it and lit the floor at the wall's far foot. A second shell sits inside every face of rock, facing in, reaching on under the next face where rock turns inward so the two meet; its depth is worked out (shellDepth: how far the softening spreads sideways at the end of the longest light's reach, 0.75 m now, never more than the block's metre), so a sample from a lit surface never meets the shell behind it.
  • Measured in one build, a lantern stood at 12 places near walls on seeds 3 and 1 (tests/_leak2.mjs): pixels of floor lit where every one of the shadow's 27 look-round directions runs into rock 421 -> 7 (what is left is single pixels on the sharp edge at a wall corner); one drop on seed 3 went 283 -> 0. The deeper wall foot (wall_skirt, now a sheet row at 0.3) was tried at 0.95 and changed nothing, so it stays. At the camp (tests/_shellfig.mjs) the shells change 535 of 33,600 pixels; the biggest patch was a lantern on the far side of a 2 m wall lighting that wall's back through the rock.
  • Cost: the shells add about 10% (seed 3) to 22% (seed 1) to the triangles a frame hands the graphics card, all of it in shadows; limited to rock seen or touching what was seen, which took it down from +36% / +106%. The shadow blur and detail sliders now rebuild the ground so the shell follows them.

v2.172.135

2026-10-06 13:57 EDT
  • Props and built things keep their shape in the dark (the user: "why do some prefabs like this column have no form from skylight?"). The skylight is one ambient light, the same strength from every side, so away from any lamp every face of a prop came out the same colour: a pale column was a flat grey cut-out. Blocks escape this only through their creases (the ao_sky shading at their corners), and props and built things have none. skyForm() in render.js now tilts the skylight on their materials: it comes from above and a little to one side (world direction 0.6, 1, 0.25), so a top is brightest, the two sides a camera sees differ, and an underside is darkest. The new knob sky_form (0.7) says how strongly; 0 is the old flat look. It applies to every room prop and every camp structure; walls, floors, crawlers and creatures are untouched. A first version only darkened faces turned down, and both visible sides came out the same shade; that was the form that was missing, hence the tilt. Checked by tests/_skyform.mjs: all 56 prop meshes on seed 3 carry it, the shader compiles, and a prop's base shows three shades with it on and one with it off.

v2.172.134

2026-10-06 09:59 EDT
  • Colour in the Wardrobe (the user: "add color selection to wardrobe"). Under each worn piece there are two rows of swatches: what it is made of (its family's rows in the stuff tab) and, for cloth, wool and leather, what it is dyed (the dyes tab, "undyed" showing the stuff's own colour). Those are the two things that colour kit in the game, so every colour offered is one a crawler could actually come by; there is no free colour wheel, because no such colour exists in the game. A piece named for its colour (Red, Grey...) keeps it. This is a preview only: a crawler's own kit is still found or made as it comes, and nothing is written to the sheet. COLOUR_WORDS is now exported from world.js so the wardrobe asks the same question makeKit does.

v2.172.133

  • Picking a look writes it to the sheet (the user: "can simply click selecting one write it to the spreadsheet?" then "allow that AND option A"). The game page cannot write the sheet, so there are two ways in. (1) The sheet page shows the gear tab's look column as six pictures, one per look; a click keeps that pick as a pending edit, and it goes into the sheet with the other edits. The pictures come from tools/look_thumbs.mjs, which draws each piece with the wardrobe's own kitThumb in each of its six looks. kitThumb gained a look to draw, a light, smooth drawing and a background. Both failed, because the drawing is not quite the same twice and a held piece is merged into the forearm. (2) The Wardrobe has a "Copy my picks" button all the time, not only after a pick, so the list can be pasted to be written in.

v2.172.127

2026-10-05 21:45 EDT

v2.172.123

2026-10-04 23:24 EDT
  • Every floor blended with rock, whatever was next to it (the user: "All these dirt tiles are blending their edges to rock, even when not adjacent to rock"). Since v2.172.120 a floor found its level neighbour by the open cell standing on it, and read that open cell's material -- air, which is material 0, rock -- instead of the block underneath. Hidden until v2.172.122 because rock-over-anything was itself drawn wrong. It reads the block under the open cell now.

v2.172.122

2026-10-04 19:05 EDT
  • Floor blends missing (the user, with pictures): every seam where rock spread onto something else was drawn wrong. A blend is filed under the pair of materials it joins, as over * 1000 + under; rock is material 0, so rock-over-dirt came out as dirt's own number and was drawn as the old pecking-order fringe of the wrong stone, and the rock never crossed. Rock is the commonest stone in the labyrinth, so a great many seams looked unblended. The pairs are numbered from a million now. Measured on one scene: rock-over-dirt 0 -> 149 blended edges, rock-over-andesite 0 -> 10, rock-over-cracked-bricks 0 -> 34.
  • The wall-top fade, checked in the same scene against v2.172.113 (before any blending): the same in both. If a wall still does not fade, it is likely one with a top somebody can stand on, which by an older rule counts as a ledge and never fades.

v2.172.121

2026-10-04 18:44 EDT
  • Slopes and walls blend where they meet (the user: "the sloping edges do meet the vertical face of walls quite often"). A wall standing beside a slope, stairs or a half block takes on its surface from the line where the two meet -- slanting up the wall with the slope -- and the slope's top takes on the wall's stone along that edge. Covered by the wall-and-floor switch.

v2.172.120

2026-10-04 18:38 EDT
  • The blend crept from the floor onto the tops of half walls (the user: "erroneously creeping from the floor on the top of half-walls, not the sides"). A top now only takes on a neighbour's surface where the two meet at the same height along their shared edge -- worked out for half blocks, slopes, stairs and floors alike -- so a half wall's top no longer blends with the floor at its foot, and a slope blends with the floor only at the end that is level with it. The floor at a half wall's foot creeps up its sides instead, and its stone out onto the floor, as with a full wall (the wall-and-floor switch covers both).

v2.172.119

With the shape-aware blend, the floor's surface creeps up the foot of a wall standing on it, and the wall's stone creeps out over the edge of the floor, by the same reach and contrast. Its own switch: Debug > Picture > "blend where a wall meets the floor", and the blend_wall_floor knob (1 on, 0 off).

v2.172.116

The bar a neighbour's stone had to clear to cross the seam rose so fast that past the first few centimetres almost nothing did, and even surfaces like brick and dressed stone never cleared it. blend_contrast 0.25 -> 0.06 and blend_width 0.4 -> 0.5, so the two sides of a seam meet in the middle of the square.

v2.172.115

2026-10-04 09:53 EDT
  • Walls blend too (the user: "Do the walls too, I said all terrain textures"). A wall face beside or under a block of a different stone takes the prouder parts of it at the seam, the same way the floors do: neighbours to either side along the wall, and above and below. It fades with the wall's top metre like the wall itself.
  • The blend sliders did nothing: each edge's shader had taken its own copy of the numbers when it was made. They now share one set of dials, so "blend reach" and "blend contrast" move every edge at once, with no rebuilding.

v2.172.114

2026-10-04 09:39 EDT
  • Shape-aware floor blending, on everywhere for the user to look at (the user: "Try 6 on all terrain textures and I'll see how it looks"). At every seam between two different floors at the same height, each square takes its neighbour's surface over its own wherever the neighbour stands prouder -- read off each texture's light and dark against its own average -- so moss fills the cracks of cobble and the stone tops show through; the further in from the seam, the prouder it must be (blend_contrast, new, 0.25), and never past blend_width (now 0.4 m). Both squares of a seam do it to each other and agree at the seam itself. Laid against laid (cobble against brick) is blended too, on purpose, so it can be judged.
  • ground_blend is now 0 off, 1 the pecking order (v2.172.107), 2 shape-aware (the default for now). Debug > Picture has a chooser for the three and a "blend contrast" slider; the test flight compares off against whichever is chosen.

v2.172.113

2026-10-04 09:17 EDT
  • Debug > Picture > "Test flight: floor blending off vs on" (the user asked for it). The game holds still, the camera circles the spot it was looking at on a fixed 4 m path for an unmeasured lap (so the ground is made), then twice more for 8 seconds each -- blending off, then on -- and prints both frame rates, the slowest frame in twenty, and the difference, and copies the line to paste. Everything is put back as it was after. The test machine has no graphics card, so frame rates are measured on the player's own device. tests/_flight.mjs checks it runs and puts the camera back.

v2.172.100

2026-10-03 16:11 EDT -- only the two lights that matter most cast shadows

The user: "only the CURRENT MOST RELEVANT TWO illumination sources draw shadows - meaning a distant campfire is lower priority than a close candle". Every lamp, torch, fire, sconce and glowing beast is now ranked each frame by how much it lights the middle of the view: its brightness, faded by its distance from there against its own reach (a light out of reach of the middle counts for nothing). The two that rank highest cast shadows (shadow_lights, 2, was 3); the rest still light, without shadows. Before, fixed fires always came first whatever their distance. Each shadow-casting point light redraws the scene six times a frame, so this is eighteen passes down to twelve. Seed 3 at the camp: the campfire 4 m from the middle and a candle 2.5 m from it cast shadows; nothing else did.

v2.172.99

2026-10-03 16:09 EDT -- portraits are drawings, made when the look changes

The user: "the portraits update too slow anyway so lose the realtime animation on them. Instead, make them look like pixelart medieval drawings on paper, updated only ANY TIME a character model is updated (wounds, gear, etc.). Make sure that the pixels are zoomed just enough to make out both eyes."

  • A card's portrait is no longer a live picture of the crawler in the world. It is their own figure built standing, head and shoulders, turned nearly to the front, and drawn once.
  • It is drawn again only when their look changes: what they wear (and its wear, dye and material), wounds, scars, a bandage, hair, skin, or being down or dead -- one card a frame at most.
  • It is made into a drawing on the card's paper: colours faded toward a sepia wash and stepped to a few tones, a dark brown ink line round the figure and along sharp changes of tone, the deepest shadow lightly hatched, the ground left bare so the paper shows through [potential slop: every number].
  • portrait_ppm (60, new knob) sets how fine it is: at 60 game pixels to the metre both eyes show as their own marks (48 showed one).

v2.172.85

2026-10-03 13:42 EDT -- the freezes the recorder caught

The user's freeze recorder sent five stops. Two causes found and fixed:

  • 24 s, never came back (on "Search the rickety pine cart" with Rowan studying a nebulob): with the company between camps there is no list of work, and making the search job read the camp's list and threw -- inside the game's step, so the frame never asked for the next one and the game stopped for good. Reproduced on seed 1 (TypeError reading the camp's jobs). An order that needs the camp's list now says "there is no camp to work from yet", adding a job with no camp does nothing, and a fault in any one step is written down and the game goes on instead of stopping. The same order on five worlds now runs clean.
  • 5 s in "panels" on picking a crawler: their sheet opens on the gear, and every piece's picture was made at once, each a figure built and drawn and read back. One picture is now made per frame and the sheet fills in over the next few frames.

Not found: the 3.5-8.4 s stops "at frame start", with nothing before them. That stretch is the game's own steps (up to 64 a frame when it falls behind); the new Debug > Frame cost readout will say how long "the game itself" is taking when it happens.

v2.172.84

2026-10-03 13:35 EDT -- a frame-cost readout, and lighter portraits

The user: "audit FPS". The test browser draws in software, so its frame rate (3-4 fps) says nothing about a real screen, and its picture work is all paid at the first moment anything is read back from the picture -- which is the portrait on a crawler's card. That is why the portraits measured 315 of 357 ms a frame there, and why switching them off left the frame rate the same: the drawing still had to happen. What the browser itself spends, measured with the portraits off, seed 3, normal speed: the game's own steps 12 ms, building the ground 4 ms, handing the picture over 24 ms, everything else about 4 ms.

What changed:

  • Debug > General > Frame cost: a running average of each part of a frame in milliseconds on the device it is played on -- the game itself, what is seen, building the ground, things in the world, figures and camera, the pointer, drawing the picture, panels and portraits (and of that, portraits alone) -- with the total, the time spent waiting on the screen, and the draw calls and triangles of the last picture. This is the instrument for a real phone or computer; the numbers above are the test browser's.
  • A portrait no longer re-casts every light's shadows (three lamps, six sides each: eighteen passes of the world for one small face): the lights stop casting while it is taken, which also keeps stale shadows off the face.
  • Each portrait card is redrawn twice a second rather than five times, so the picture is read back from the card less often.

v2.172.83

2026-10-03 13:25 EDT -- every material is named by what it is

The user: "everything with a wood tag must have a wood type, same goes for all materials. some things are still missing material tags like workbench for instance". Tags now name the stuff, not its family, wherever there is one:

  • Camp buildings carry a stuff for every material they took (a workbench of pine and granite), recorded as it is built; ones built before this, and kinds the camp makes without a blueprint, take steady picks from their square. The building is named for its main one (Pine workbench, Granite kiln, Ash barricade, Pine wagon) and tagged with all of them, first. A workbench, kiln and the like were tagged "structure, rest"; they are now tagged for what they are (bench, kiln, rack...).
  • Props are tagged with the stuff they are named for; relics' stone, iron, wood and bone tags become granite, flint, oak, antler and so on.
  • A piece of kit's tag row names its stuff: a hide pack is "hide, carrying, hauling", not "leather".

Not done: blocks of the world (cobblestone, sandstone and so on are already a kind of stone), and dirt, sand and charcoal, which have no kinds in the stuff tab to choose from.
