v2.172.77

2026-10-03 13:01 EDT -- a blueprint answers the pointer only where it is

The user: "woodpile still has a huge mouseover target. check them all." The blueprint box itself was the right size since v2.172.39; its pale outline is drawn with lines, and three.js counts a ray as hitting a line anywhere within Line.threshold -- 1 m by default. So every blueprint took the pointer a metre round its outline. Measured on a woodpile blueprint, seed 3, 900x700: 30,056 screen pixels answered to it, now 3,536. The threshold is now 1 cm for every line in the game. Every built camp building was checked the same way (all eighteen kinds, pixels that pick it against the outline of what is drawn): none answers outside its own drawn shape.

So in most games the sledge still comes, just later.

v2.172.68

2026-10-03 10:16 EDT -- cancelling a half-built thing, and taking things apart

Three of the user's reports. (1) "Canceling a build leaves it as 'being built' forever": a blueprint struck out after work had begun was still drawn as a half-built structure, because the picture listed every unfinished job with work in it and never asked whether it had been called off. It now does, and a half-built structure can itself be cancelled from its own panel. (2) "Trestle bench cannot be disassembled": the bench had been taken up by the camp as a seat, and a taken-up prop became a camp structure whose panel had no "Take it apart". It has one now. (3) "Toppled statue cannot be disassembled or moved": the statue was one of the old things left in rooms (relics), which only ever offered "Look into it". Relics can now be taken apart for what their tags say they are made of (stone gives 2 rough stone; iron, wood or bone 1) and moved like any prop. Their picture now goes away when they do.

v2.172.66

2026-10-03 09:50 EDT -- the coal seam stops nagging

When fuel ran low the camp marked a coal seam to dig; if nobody was strong enough, "Nobody here has the skill to dig out rock" was said, and a few minutes later a fresh seam was marked and refused again (five times in one run, seed 3). Now, once nobody can dig it, no seam is marked again until someone's Labouring gets better or a stronger crawler joins.

Now the job is posted only on the stricter of the two, and a stool, sledge or wagon job waits untouched while the wood is short, the way a blueprint already did.

v2.172.62

2026-10-03 06:31 EDT -- the gear is a paperdoll

The Belongings tab's gear list is now a Diablo-style paperdoll: back, head and neck across the top, weapon -- body -- offhand down the middle, then gloves, belt, legs, feet and the two trinkets. Each filled slot shows a pixel-art still of the actual piece, drawn by the game's own renderer (kitPicture in render.js: the crawler's figure built wearing only that piece, the body painted a key colour and hidden, framed to the piece's box at world pixel density, cached per item). Pointing at a slot gives the item's card as a tooltip; tapping it opens the card, with its pin, under the grid. Empty slots name the slot and what is worn there bare.

v2.172.60

  • Loot the room first (the user: "they would loot the whole room as soon as they secure it and before exploring further (unless they can't use anything there, desperately need food and find none, etc)"). A room is secured when nothing hostile and alive is in it (roomSecured); a crawler out exploring who stands in a secured room with kit lying in it or a prop still holding something sees to that before leading on (lootFirst) -- unless they are starving and the room holds no food. Props are searched three at a time (loot_jobs), anywhere secured and not only near the fire. What cannot be got at is not waited on for ever (loot_wait, 90 s a room). Measured, seeds 1-4, 15,000 steps: 13 pieces of loot taken against 7 on v2.172.59, and as many rooms seen.
  • Look into every room before moving on for good (the user: "they should also fully investigate rooms before moving on for good"). Unless danger or hunger is driving them, a move waits while any room within read_before_move_m (40 m) has been seen and never investigated; free crawlers with a light are sent to read them, for read_before_move (600 s) at most.

v2.172.44

2026-10-03 02:27 EDT -- the camp takes up the props in its room

The user: "they should be able to claim and reuse any props in the camp room - sit on benches, light candles, sleep in beds, etc". A new serves column in the props tab (bed, seat, light, store; worked out from the names once, 57 of 588 rows, the sheet the authority after) says what a prop can be to a camp. Once the camp is built, a prop in the fire's room whose column says so is taken up as a camp building of that kind -- a bed as a bedroll, a bench as a stool, a candle or a lamp as a sconce, a chest as the store -- keeping its own look, so everything the camp already does with those works on it: beds are claimed and slept in (and no bedroll is laid while one is free), seats are sat on, a light is lit by whoever has a flame and burns as a candle, a lantern or a brazier would, the store keeps kit. A prop still holding something is searched first. Seen over five seeds: two candles claimed and lit (seed 1), a candle and a bench (seed 2); the other three camp rooms held nothing that serves.

v2.172.43

2026-10-03 02:22 EDT -- stinging growths are a hazard; the bestiary takes growths; jobs say what; the third portrait
  • Stinging growths (the user: "stinging plants would be cut/burned/etc as a hazard - I just almost lost a whole squad to a plant in the hallway"). The squares on and beside a live stinging growth now cost a walk path_cost_sting (12), so the company goes round where it can (markSting, every 3 s); and one that has stung somebody, or grows within sting_clear_reach (12 m) of the fire, gets a job: Cut down a stinging growth (Labouring, a cutting tool helps; a flame in the hand burns it out twice as fast). Missing a stroke can get you stung. Cut down, a woody one leaves its wood in a pile. Three seeds, 20,000 steps: on two, nobody was stung at all; on the third, 14 cut down for 3 stings.
  • The bestiary takes growths: the first one of a kind seen in the light is written in, as creatures always were.
  • A job names what it is done to (the user: "still says 'walking to search it' search what??"): "walking to: search the crate", "take the barrel apart", "bring the pack back to camp" -- jobLabel(), used everywhere a job is put in words.
  • The third portrait drew the world behind them: a portrait's camera saw layer 2 + the crawler's number, and the third crawler's 4 is the layer the labyrinth is drawn on. Portrait layers start at 6 now (portLayer).

v2.172.42

2026-10-03 02:15 EDT -- ganging up, and breaking when it goes badly

The user: "crawlers should help each other in fights more often, they would try to gang up on enemies. unless they start losing or are a coward, in which case they'd break and flee". Nobody ever joined a fight: meet() passed over any creature already in one. Now a crawler within join_reach (10 m) of a fight weighs it with the ones already at it on their side (decide(s, c, cr, fight); the anxious want better odds, the steely take worse) and, if it suits them, runs over and piles in. Each round, a side worth less than rout_odds (0.5) of the creature breaks and runs -- each crawler judged by their own nerve, so the anxious break first -- unless the creature has them cornered. sideWorth and creatureWorth are now the one sum both choices use. Seen on seed 2: a second crawler piled in, two on one for 110 of 121 rounds; on seed 4 the nearest crawler chose to help but the fight was over before they arrived.

v2.172.41

2026-10-03 02:08 EDT -- a fighter's stance, and secondary motion everywhere
  • Fighting (the user: a brawler's idle stance; "people are in a lot of motion when engaged"): bladed, left foot forward and the rear leg near straight so both feet stay down, bouncing on the balls of the feet, weight rocking foot to foot, shoulders turned and chin down; bare-handed, the guard is up by the chin with the elbows in, the lead hand flicks out a jab and the rear hand throws the round's blow; armed, the weapon weaves while it waits.
  • A bug found doing it: gripOf names the grips 'bare hands' and 'a pair', and the pose only ever matched 'bare' and 'pair', so a fist-fighter and a pair-wielder have always taken the one-handed stance. Both names now match.
  • Secondary motion in every pose (the user: "I want way more secondary motion in all animations"), scaled by a new secondary knob (0 is the old stillness): the breath in the chest and shoulders; walking, the shoulders turn against the hips, the hips drop and rock, the head steadies against it, the forearms trail the swing; standing, the weight shifts from foot to foot and the head looks about; at work, the body heaves with the effort. Each crawler has their own rhythm.

v2.172.40

2026-10-03 02:04 EDT -- the user's Blender pass on the body; blood that holds still; fliers that walk
  • The body, as the user placed it in Blender (crawler-body-redone.glb): kneecaps further forward, the shoulder balls moved in and widened into a cap over each shoulder, the pelvis taller and set back, the seat pieces deleted, the waist tipped forward and narrowed, the ribcage lower, taller and tipped back, the neck tipped forward and thickened.   • Blood sparkled because its ragged edge and its spray of drops were hashed in cells of 2.2 and 1.4 cm -- a third of a game pixel -- so every pixel caught a different cell as anything moved, and four samples a pixel could not average it. The cells are now two and one and a half game pixels (from px_per_m).
  • Fliers walk, and take off only to fight or flee (a floating stone stays up); aloft their wings beat three times as fast, their legs are drawn up, and they bob and weave across their square instead of hanging still.

v2.172.39

2026-10-03 02:00 EDT -- the overnight list, part one

The user's list before bed, the first half:

  • The see-through outline (addGhost): "30cm seems like a lot. I only asked you to exempt the ground." The margin is now a hair -- ghost_floor_m, 6 cm -- since v2.172.38 put the feet on the floor; anything standing properly in front still lights it.
  • Hunger always drains. A new alive row in the activities tab (hunger -0.1 a minute, always, asleep or resting too); idle and sleeping lose their own small drain to it; walking -0.15 and moderate work -0.25 on top; a new heavy work row (-0.7 hunger, more fatigue and filth) for a fight or any job in heavy_work_skills (Labouring, Building, Grappling, Brawling); a new healing row (-0.5) while wounded.
  • Shields on the outside of the forearm. The side was read off the elbow joint's own position, which is always 0 in its shoulder, so every shield went to the right-hand side -- the inside of the left arm. The elbow now knows its arm.
  • Portraits look square at the upper chest from in front, the camera riding the body (portrait_chest_m), and are drawn at the world's own pixel density: portrait_m of crawler at px_per_m (about 20 pixels tall).
  • Walking without a pause each step. Every step eased in and out (smoothstep), so a crawler came to a stop on every square, and the step after waited for the next tick and lost the time past the square. Now a step that follows another starts in the same tick with the overflow, and easing is only at the start and end of a walk. Two walkers meeting head-on shuffle past in pass_seconds (0.4 s), not the 15 s meant for a creature in the way. Measured mid-walk over 3,000 ticks: frozen ticks 8.7% -> 2.3%, the slowest tenth of ticks 0.002 -> 0.06 m.
  • Looting first. Bring kit back, disarm and pick come straight after the campfire in the order of work (the tasks tab's row order), and a crawler out exploring with kit lying within loot_reach (3 m) breaks off the leg to take it.
  • A blueprint's box is the size of the building, measured off the finished model.
  • Buildings can be moved like props: Move it, then the next click is where (a ghost of the building shows it); someone carries it there whole, with whatever is kept in it. A moved campfire takes the camp's fire with it. Not sconces or graves.
  • A prop is searched before it is taken apart.
  • ROADMAP: the glare shafts reminder.

v2.172.30

The Wardrobe had its own renderer and its own look: no inside-smoothing pass, soft shadows of its own, a flat-coloured checker floor, and a studio daylight. v2.172.20

2026-10-02 21:00 EDT -- dyeing at camp

A crawler's own orders gain "Dye something they wear" (chosen: "Found, and they can dye"). Checking whether it can be done throws no dice, so the panel never moves the world's dice. Measured on seed 1: a grey wool cloak came out moss green.

v2.172.16

2026-10-02 20:38 EDT -- the normal maps the right way up, and the shine maps used

The user: "normals look inverted sometimes". Measured against each map's own height (its alpha): red falls where the height rises to the right, as three.js expects, but green falls where the height rises DOWN the picture -- the other convention, in all four maps checked (andesite, acacia planks, cobblestone, stone bricks; correlations -0.42 to -0.87). Lit from one side the bumps read right, from the other they read as dents. Green is turned over in the shader, and the depth is worked out from red and green alone because the pack keeps something else in blue. The pack's _s shine maps are now built in and read: red is how smooth a texel is, and roughness is (1 - red)^2 against the sheet's 0.92 (the shine slider still scales it). +0.2 MB.

v2.172.14

2026-10-02 20:29 EDT / v2.172.15 -- hard outlines with smoothed insides; tools across the fist; click to place; tabbed bestiary
  • Smoothing only inside things (the user: "can we antialias only WITHIN units/objects? ... things have a hard pixel-art edge on their silhouette, meanwhile geometry and blood splatters within their model are antialiased"). The labyrinth's blocks are on their own layer (WORLD_LAYER). The picture is drawn hard as before; then the blocks' depth alone and the things over it are drawn at twice the size each way into a half-float target, and a pixel whose four samples ALL landed on a thing takes their average (drawSmooth). An outline pixel (one to three samples) and all ground keep the hard pixel. The four-samples-a-pixel MSAA of v2.172.11 is gone -- it blurred every wall corner. The figures' see-through "ghost" (drawn where a body is hidden) sits on its own GHOST_LAYER and is left out of the second pass: in the first test it painted every crawler as a flat tan cut-out.
  • Tools and weapons are held across the fist at grip_angle (45 degrees forward from straight down the forearm), turned about the hand; sheathed and stowed ones are not turned. Staffs, lights and shields are held as before.
  • Moving a prop: after "Move this", the next click on the ground is where it goes -- no second order. A see-through blue ghost of the prop follows the pointer, outlined in the selection's yellow where it may go and red where it may not (placeAt in orders.js, syncPlaceGhost). Escape leaves the move.
  • The bestiary sits clear of a picked portrait and is tabbed down its left side, the tabs torn from its own paper like the ledger's (parchment.js picture() takes vert tabs); one kind open at a time, each tab saying how much is known.

Make them real buildings just automatically assigned"). Campfire, Store, Woodpile and Larder are rows of the buildings tab (place camp, so the coach's build menu leaves them out) with their own tasks. The fire's place and the three stores are laid out as blueprints the moment the camp is chosen or raised, wait for their materials like any blueprint, and the supply rules dig the stone. start_wood 6 -> 2: exactly the campfire. Bedrolls are still carried down, not built.
  • The fire's wood is kept for the fire: any building but the campfire, and any craft, pays wood only from what is over wood_low (fuelKept, have). The emergency torch may take the fire's wood, but only once the fire is built -- in the first test it ate a load before the campfire was laid and the camp never got a fire.
  • Scavenging for wood: short of wood for the fire or a blueprint, somebody picks the nearest woody growth (Flora, which grows back) or else takes apart the nearest wooden prop, scavenge_jobs (2) at a time within prop_reach.
  • Creatures are no longer drawn on ground nobody has explored (a glowing one lit its own square and showed through).
  • Carpet critters get the selection outline: a stand-in of the one instance, built from the batch's own geometry.
  • Smooth edges: the low-resolution picture takes four samples a pixel (smooth_edges, Debug > Visuals to switch it on one device, reloads), so thin and small things stop sparkling.
  • Debug gains a Visuals tab (picture scale, cobwebs, smooth edges, normal depth -- normal_depth 0.7 -- and (.12) shine, which takes roughness down towards polish). Torches do light the normal maps: they are held high and close, so the relief catches less of their light than the knee-high campfire's.
  • The seal's C is a hand-drawn 12-pixel C in the title font's style, half as big again as the font's own.

v2.172.9

2026-10-02 18:03 EDT -- a freeze recorder, the debug menu in tabs, and a wax seal
  • Freeze recorder (src/freeze.js): the page leaves a trail of notes -- which part of the frame or step it is in, which crawler it is thinking for, every order given and everything picked -- and a helper running beside the page (a worker) watches for the heartbeat. Silent for freeze_seconds (3, new knob) and it writes the trail into the browser's own storage, and keeps updating how long the stop lasted and whether the page came back. Debug > Freezes shows the last six with a copy button. Hidden tabs are ignored, so putting the page away is not a freeze. Built to catch the "look it over" / "put the covered table here" freeze, which never reproduced here.
  • Debug menu in three tabs: General (sound, god mode, cobwebs, picture scale), Lighting (every light, shadows, skylight), Freezes.

v2.172.6

2026-10-02 17:23 EDT - stone that grew legs

The user: "lithoids shouldn't have heads - they should all be various iterations of strangely-shaped mineral deposits - they may be weird tripedal cuties, rolling loops of crystal, slithering snakes of pebbles, clouds of sparkling dust (swarm), floating rotating veined boulders, translucent, dull, shiny, glowing, or sparkly ... save this idea to the style guide".

  • docs/STYLE.md is new: the style guide, with that entry, and the four reference pictures in art/concept/lithics/.
  • Lithics have their own builder (growLithic): no head, no face (they are faceless now, like plants, fungi and oozes, so no eyestalks, horns or fangs are listed on them either). The kind's own dice pick a form that fits how it moves -- a walker is a tripod on three crystal legs, a rolling loop of crystal round a hub (it turns as it goes) or a crystal cluster shuffling on stone nodules; a creeper, burrower or swimmer a snake of pebbles; a flier a veined boulder (it floats and turns); a rooted one a geode split open on its crystals; a swarm a cloud of glinting dust -- and a finish: dull, shiny, translucent, glowing or sparkly. Shards, spines, plates and glow still grow on them, as mineral.

v2.172.5

2026-10-02 17:18 EDT - a cloud of growth, folder tabs, a folded bestiary
  • The carpet grows in clouds (the user: "'carpet' is laid one critter per tile uniformly - doesn't look natural. place with noise cloud style density? also remember small and tiny CAN fit into the same squares"): a soft noise over each room, growth_cloud (4 m) across, says how thick it grows square by square -- thick in places, bare in others -- and a broader one says which kind holds which stretch, so kinds come in patches. A square (and a wall face) holds growth_room_per_square (4): a tiny growth takes 1, a small one 2, anything bigger the lot -- so up to four tiny things or two small ones share a square, drawn a little apart. v2.172.4

2026-10-02 17:07 EDT - what sets an ambusher off, counted

Every spring now records its trigger (springWhy: caught, close, back; and for a turned back, how far, and what the crawler was doing). Shown to the user with two narrower readings of "turns their back"; they kept it as it is (A): anyone it can see who faces away is jumped.

v2.172.3

2026-10-02 16:56 EDT - lying in wait, worked out in the open

The user designed it, the numbers were run first, and these are their choices:

  • A look when the light rises. Every time the light on an ambusher lying in wait goes up, each crawler who has it in front of their head throws Investigating for it, with one success more for every level of light on it, against its hiding: its own Sneaking pair (agility and intellect) thrown as dice, plus ambush_still (3) for keeping still. They must beat it; a tie stays hidden. In front means within 90 degrees of where their head is turned: within ambush_cone/2 (45) full on, beyond that only a 6 counts (the corner of the eye); behind, no look at all. Knowing its kind lies in wait is ambush_known (2) dice to the good. No set distance: light falls off a level a metre, so how close you must come is how bright your light is. (v2.172.2's 6 m was arbitrary, and is gone.)
  • Where they look. On the move a crawler looks ahead, and every glance_every (3) steps glances at the darkest, most hidden square in front of them that their light reaches -- never behind. The head is seen to turn. To be looked at again in the balance pass (ROADMAP).
  • When it springs: when it is caught, when someone comes within ambush_reach (2 m -- it closes and strikes in one), or when someone it can see (lit, and near enough to notice) turns their back on it. Nothing walks past one unharmed.
  • A rule for every system (AGENTS.md rule 8): emergent, never arbitrary.
  • The numbers it was chosen on (typical crawler, 7 dice, against a typical ambusher, 6 dice, +3 still): spotted before it springs, straight ahead / 2 squares aside / 4 aside -- torch 100 / 74 / 29%, lantern 84 / 44 / 4%, candle 7 / 1 / 0%.

v2.172.2

2026-10-02 16:05 EDT - Investigating, and spotting what lies in wait
  • "Studying" is "Investigating", everywhere (the user): the skill's name in the skills tab, every task that uses it, the code, the rulebook and the roadmap. The pair is the same (Intellect and Willpower), so every crawler's rating, pips and lessons carry straight over. The orders "Study it" and the word "studied" for creatures are left as they were.
  • Hiding is a contest (the user: "Make Ambushers hiding a Contest roll!"): an ambusher used to be invisible until its kind's temper had been studied, and then always seen. Now a crawler who comes within ambush_spot (6 m) of one, with light on it, throws Investigating against its hiding -- its own Sneaking pair, agility and intellect, thrown as dice -- and must beat it (a tie stays hidden). Knowing its kind lies in wait is ambush_known (2) dice to the good. Spotted, it is drawn and seen by all for ambush_rehide seconds; missed, that crawler looks again in ambush_spot_rest (30 s). The look goes in the ledger, and a miss teaches like any failure.
  • The opening line counted "Six crawlers" whatever came down; it counts them now.

v2.172.0

2026-10-02 15:57 EDT - the carpet

The user: "I want a ton more non-mobile autotrophs, growing out of the floor and walls of most areas" -- and then, asked: all rooted kinds may grow on walls; the carpet is the rooted kinds plus some light-eaters made rooted; it is harvestable, grazer food, a hazard and a light; about 30 a room, thick in caves and thin in built rooms.

  • Fourteen light-eaters are rooted now (kinds tab move root): Thornado, Zephyrose, Gustle, Spellen, Sentreed, Starburr, Dandelume, Aegilily, Glowbell, Solvia (Flora, up to medium), Dazzle, Aureole (Fungi), Glowb, Solace (creeping oozes). With the 32 already rooted that is 46 kinds.
  • The carpet (src/growths.js, world.growths): every room gets growth_room (30) for each growth_area (40 m^2, up to twice), a quarter of that (growth_built) in a room somebody built, in patches the size of each kind's group (a pair 2, a pack 3-5, a swarm 6-10) spreading square by square, weighted to the small and harmless and to kinds whose element belongs there. Beside a wall, growth_wall (0.45) of them grow out of it. Never on a room's middle, stairs, gates, kit, props, relics or forageables, and nothing hostile where the company comes down. On their own dice.
  • They are not thinking creatures: a few thousand of those would stop a phone. Each is its kind, with its name and tags, drawn as that kind is built, its pieces merged by material and every growth of a kind drawn as one instance -- a handful of draw calls for the lot. The rooted kinds no longer turn up as full creatures (populate skips them).
  • Uses: "Pick it" (Foraging) gives a plant's biomass as wood, a fungus as food, the rest what their lineage leaves, carried home; it grows back in growth_regrow (600 s). Plant-eaters graze the rooted plants and fungus-eaters the rooted fungi. A hostile one beside a crawler's step may try to sting (growth_sting 0.25): a Maneuvering throw to keep clear, or a wound -- never the last one -- and not again for growth_sting_rest (30 s). One with the glow feature lights glow_growth (2) round it.
  • Measured, seeds 7 and 3 (pieces held after 4,000 steps): 492 and 1,002 growths of 44 and 45 kinds, 101 and 212 on walls, most common Smudge, Cobbler, Kindling, Tinderbell; full creatures 62 -> 46 and 67 -> 59; a simulation step 0.43 -> 0.33 and 0.58 -> 0.49 ms; a drawn frame 2.9 -> 4.5 and 2.9 -> 5.5 ms (headless, software). Grazers ate 7 and 1 growths.

v2.171.0

2026-10-02 15:43 EDT - nature is not square

v2.170.2

2026-10-02 15:41 EDT - blueprints stay, props come apart and move
  • Blueprints are drawn until they are done (the user: "Show blueprint until complete... faint hollow stroke outline... selectable with awaited material listing"): they used to vanish the moment work began. Now a faint outline of the whole thing (a block to dig, a building's own size, a sconce on the wall) stays until it is finished, with an unseen box inside to point at. Hovering or picking one says what it is, what it still waits for ("waits for 2 rough stone"), who is at it and how far along it is; its one order is "Cancel it".
  • Props come apart (the user: "Prop deconstruction materials"): a new parts column on the props tab says what each is made of, read off its colour and shape -- wood, rough stone, metal, cloth, fibre, bone, crystal, charcoal, two of it for a large prop and one for a small; 44 (puddles, scrolls, candles and the like) give nothing. "Take it apart" (Building) leaves a pile where it stood, carried home like any dug pile; wood goes to the woodpile.
  • Props move (the user: "Ability to Move/disassemble props"): "Move it", then pick a square and "Put the ... here"; whoever takes it (Labouring) lifts it, carries it there and sets it down.
  • The woodpile went to -1 in testing: feeding the fire, relighting it and setting a snare all took a load without asking whether somebody had taken the last one on the way. They ask now.
  • Measured on seed 7: an altar taken apart came home as 2 rough stone; a lunch pail moved to the square chosen.

v2.170.1

2026-10-02 15:37 EDT - a round of fixes
  • Torch sconce built and then gone (reported): with no torch in the store and no wood on the pile, the work finished and nothing was put up.   • Map taps on a phone (reported: only the first works): could not be reproduced in mobile Chromium, where every tap moved the view; the map now answers the touch's release directly (pointerup, touch-action: none) instead of waiting for a click a phone browser may decline to send.
  • "Look it over" freezing Firefox on a phone (reported): could not be reproduced in Chromium or desktop Firefox -- the order went through in under 0.2 s and the game kept running. Not fixed; waiting on more detail.

Orders on a prop: Search it / Look it over. Hover says what it is, its room tag and what it holds. Measured: 11 and 6 props searched in 20,000 steps, everything they had seen near camp.

What is switched off is kept in that browser and new pieces of the labyrinth stop using it; the sheet's on column decides first.

v2.169.5

v2.169.4

2026-10-01 00:04 EDT - each stone its own spin

A floating stone's turn is its own now, drawn once from its phase: still (two chances in seven), or 0.15, 0.35, 0.6, 1 or 1.6 radians a second, either way round, a little faster while it moves -- and about an axis of its own (the user: "also different axes"): upright, tipped any way, or end over end.

v2.169.2

2026-09-30 23:59 EDT - not on the trader

Reported: "they just built a campfire on a trader." Traders live in world.traders, not among the crawlers, and occupied() -- the one answer to "who is standing where" -- never asked them. So a camp could be laid with its fire (or a bedroll) on the square a trader stood on, and paths walked through them. occupied() now counts traders, the fire is never put where its 3x3 of floor holds a trader, and no bedroll is laid under one.

v2.169.1

2026-09-30 23:58 EDT - blood, not lumps

The user: "wounds should show not as geometry, but as bright blood splatters projected onto the unit models -- red for mammals & birds, green for bugs, yellow for mollusks, orange for reptiles". src/splat.js patches a body's material so that up to eight spheres in the figure's own space paint whatever surface passes through them in the blood's colour, with a ragged edge and a spray of drops; two splatters a wound, laid out from the body's own number so they stay put. Crawlers wear it on their one body material, creatures on per-creature clones of theirs; the old dark lumps (gash) are no longer drawn. Blood colour is a new blood column on the lineage tab: red for mammals, birds, fish and worms, green for arthropods, yellow for molluscs, orange for reptiles and amphibians, and -- not named by the user -- sap green for plants, pale for fungi, sea-green for oozes, stone dust for Lithic. v2.169.0's reds came out pink (the body's own glow and the tone curve washed them); v2.169.1 deepens the reds and cuts the blood's own glow.

v2.168.1

2026-09-30 23:54 EDT - a blueprint waits, and is supplied

The user: "building orders should remain and wait for fulfillment, crawlers will work to supply them".

  • Every build order is offered whatever the camp holds (the menus still name the cost), and a blueprint it cannot yet pay for is laid and left: "X marks out the work. It waits for ...; the company will fetch it." jobNeeds names what any blueprint takes (the buildings tab, the dry-stone wall, ramp, cairn, stool, planks); waiting holds it out of freeJobs until it can be paid.
  • shortfall totals what the waiting blueprints want beyond the stores. Every supply_look (10) game seconds, for each dug material short (rough stone, dirt, sand, coal) and not already coming (piles lying about, digs open), a dig goes up on the nearest block of it within 20 m of camp and at least 5 m from the fire -- a wall for rock, a wall or failing that a floor for earth and sand. The pile goes home as any dug pile does. Wood short counts as fuel short, so the hunters go after what burns.
  • A supply dig nobody can do (rock with no pick in the company) is cleared away and that material rested for twelve looks, rather than piling up: the first cut of this (v2.168.0, published for a minute) put up fifty refused digs in half an hour.
  • Measured headless, seeds 3 and 7: a dirt blueprint laid with empty stores was supplied by 1 and 2 supply digs, each dug, hauled and paid, and the blueprint finished.

v2.167.0

2026-09-30 22:37 EDT - fuel is hunted and mined

Asked how gathering fuel worked, the answer was that it was free: a "Gather fuel" job on any patch of earth or moss brought back three loads forever. The user had imagined otherwise -- "gather biomass from plant beasts, and a coal vein would be a huge boon", "some lithic and maybe pyr beasts will be flammable" -- and chose to remove free gathering outright and to make coal a scatter of blocks dug one at a time.

  • The gather job and its order are gone. Short of fuel, the warning says so once in ten minutes.
  • Flora and Fungi now leave wood (lineage tab), and a new leaves column on the affinity tab gives Pyr beasts coal -- so a Pyr Lithic, which leaves no meat, is a fuel kill. A kill that burns leaves beast_fuel loads per step of size; wood goes straight on the woodpile, coal is worth coal_wood (4) loads apiece.
  • Hunting now starts when fuel is low as well as food, and when only fuel is short the hunters go only after what burns (Lithic kinds included).
  • Coal seams: a new coal material (Coal Seam, coal_ore texture). Each piece of the labyrinth makes coal_seams tries at coal_seam_chance, each a random walk of coal_seam_blocks coal blocks through natural rock beside a floor. Only what the rock is made of changes, never its shape, on dice of its own, so nothing walkable moves. Measured on seeds 3/7/11 at the camp: 11 / 0 / 0 coal blocks in the two pieces held.
  • Coal digs like rock (a pick, near hopeless bare-handed) into a load of coal, hauled home and turned into fuel. When fuel is low and somebody has stood beside a seam within 30 m of the fire, a dig job goes up on its nearest block.

v2.166.0

2026-09-30 20:01 EDT - a crowded fire is a cold welcome

The company starts as three, not six. A stranger at the fire is harder to welcome the bigger the company is, unless the larder can feed everyone: the Welcome job needs one more success for every head (the newcomer included) the stores fall short of at welcome_food_head rations each (4). A well-stocked camp still grows to company_max (9); a hungry one turns people away, because the job becomes impossible and the stranger walks on. The figure is recomputed every tick, so it follows the larder as it fills and empties.

v2.165.1

2026-09-30 19:47 EDT - no grinding the impossible

"Careful not to allow almost-impossible-but-feasible learning exploits", and yes to two of the answers offered:

  • Rock cannot be dug, nor stone crushed, bare-handed: their need without the tool is 19, past anyone's dice, so the attempt is below the wall -- it is refused, and a refused attempt teaches nothing. With a pick it is 1.
  • Rough work without its tool tears the hands: a failure at digging earth or sand, unblocking or widening a way, done without the tool the work wants, has a bare_hurt (15%) chance of a wound; they leave off. Never the last wound, so nobody goes down digging with their fingers. Which work is rough is the hurt_tasks knob (digdirt, digsand, unblock, widen) -- sweeping and clearing the camp's rubble are left out.

v2.165.0

2026-09-30 19:37 EDT - sandy shores, and digging by the tool rule

"Yes, use the earth footstep sounds, they can still crush stone if they have tools and workbench. ... use tool difficulty adjustment rules to make it so stone is virtually impossible to dig without a pick and dirt is very slow to dig without a spade. Fit it in emergently, not new shoehorned mechanics".

  • Sand is a new ground material (tags sand, earth, natural, loose; its own texture). About half the pools (sand_shore 0.5) have the room's ground beside the water turned to sand, chosen by a hash of where the pool is so nothing else about the piece moves. It carries the earth tag, so it walks with the earth footsteps. Seeds 1, 3, 7: 0, 93 and 15 squares of sand beside 0, 122 and 25 of water.
  • Digging is three tasks, one per material, under the tool rule every job already follows (need with the tool / need bare-handed): rock dig wants a Pick (1 success with one, 6 without -- near hopeless, and every failure still teaches), earth digdirt wants a Spade (1 with, 3 without: slow), sand digsand (1 with, 2 without). Digging sand leaves sand. The dig order is the task for what the block is made of.
  • The Pick is new kit (mainhand, iron, tool, mining; heavy, pierces; 7 lb), drawn in the hand, found and started with like the Spade.
  • Crushing stone into sand now happens at a workbench and wants a pick (1 with, 6 without).

v2.164.3

2026-09-30 19:20 EDT - what a trader carries is worked out from weight and worth

"That should be determined dynamically based on weight:value". The unsellable list is gone. A new goods tab gives each material what a load weighs (lb) and is worth (coins); a trader takes a material only if a pound of it is worth at least trade_min_value (0.1 coins). As first set: rough stone (40 lb, 1 coin), dirt and sand (30 lb, half a coin) and bricks (20 lb, 1 coin) are left behind; charcoal (5 lb, 1 coin) and every kind of salvage are taken. A material's worth in payment is now its own coins (was 1 each), and its weight is what a crawler carrying it bears (was 3 lb each) -- so a load of rough stone is 40 lb in someone's arms. A material the tab does not name keeps the old 3 lb and 1 coin.

v2.164.2

2026-09-30 19:18 EDT - traders will not haul stone

"Merchants wouldn't haul the weight:value ratio of regular stone or sand, they will not buy it". A new knob, unsellable (rough stone, dirt, sand), names the materials no trader takes: they are left out of what the company is worth to a trader and out of what is handed over in payment. Bricks and charcoal still sell.

v2.164.0

2026-09-30 19:12 EDT / v2.164.1 - eight more buildings

The rest of the buildings the user approved ("Proceed"; the filter as "#2": rough stone, sand and charcoal), all ordered on a square of open ground and all blueprints. What each takes and does is the new buildings tab (kind, name, task, needs, place, does); each has its own task and look.

  • Workbench (2 wood, 1 rough stone): making and mending at camp need bench_ease (1) fewer successes.
  • Drying rack (2 wood): meat brought home counts rack_meat (1.5) times over.
  • Kiln (3 rough stone, 1 dirt): with dirt to spare, the camp fires brick_dirt (2) dirt and a load of fuel into a load of bricks. Bricks are kept; nothing uses them yet.
  • Root cellar (2 rough stone, 1 wood): without one, a larder of more than spoil_keep (8) rations loses one every spoil_minutes (30). This spoiling is new and only bites a big larder.
  • Raised bed (2 dirt, 1 wood): a garden bed anywhere; it is planted first, and adds one to how many beds the camp keeps.
  • Barricade (3 wood): the company passes, wild creatures cannot -- in their paths and their steps. A tame one can.
  • Water filter (2 rough stone, 1 sand, 1 charcoal): somewhere to wash at camp without a pool. Sand is crushed from rough stone (the crush job, which the camp starts by itself while a filter waits for sand); charcoal is left by the campfire, a load for every charcoal_minutes (30) it burns.
  • Plank bridge (planks_wood 2 wood): from a square beside a one-square drop with ground at the same height beyond, planks laid across the gap make it walkable.

A blueprint that cannot yet be paid for waits (v2.164.1: the first filter gave up when its sand was not ready and its outline vanished). Proved on seed 7 with the camp built and materials to hand: all seven buildings ordered at once were built, sand crushed and bricks fired along the way, and the filter, once charcoal was to hand, had crawlers washing at it. Not yet proved in a match: the barricade turning a creature, and the plank bridge (no one-square gap turned up by the camp to try it on). Worth knowing: a trader will take rough stone and sand in trade, as it takes any other material.

2026-09-30 18:04 EDT - building with what was dug: walls, ramps, cairns

The first three of the eleven buildings the user approved, the three built out of the piles digging leaves (all ordered on a square of open ground, all blueprints):

  • Dry-stone wall (drywall, Building, 3 units, drywall_stone 1 rough stone): a block of stone set on the square, a wall one block high. It waits while anyone or anything stands there.
  • Dirt ramp (ramp, Labouring, 2 units, ramp_dirt 1 dirt): offered only beside a ledge exactly one block higher; the square becomes a slope climbing it, so a ledge nobody could climb becomes a way up.

The user's next answer is awaited on the water filter (in place of the rain barrel): what it should need.

v2.163.1

2026-09-30 18:01 EDT - one block at a time

"Dig one block at a time, not two". A dig order now takes out exactly the block clicked, and only if somebody can stand beside it (at its height, a step below or a step above); it leaves one load. The load lies in the hole if the hole can be stood in, else at the digger's feet. The blueprint outline is one block. Seed 7: the block dug, the one above it left standing, one load of rough stone carried home.

v2.163.0

2026-09-30 17:50 EDT - blueprints and digging

"Allow blueprint building. When clicking on a spot, you can issue a build order, as well as digging. Anytime they dig they create rough stone or dirt piles of equal volume that can be moved or used in crafting and farming and terraforming. Buildings include torch wall sconces".

  • Digging. A block of rock anyone could stand beside, with ground under it, offers "Dig this out (rough stone)" -- or dirt, where the rock is earth. The order is a Labouring job (the new dig task, 4 units, pickaxe heard as each blow lands) for the rock at the height of someone standing beside it: two blocks, floor to head, so the square dug out can be walked. Each block dug leaves one load of the same volume on the floor there, as a pile you can see and point at.
  • Piles. A pile is carried home a load at a time (the new haulpile task) and joins the camp's materials as "rough stone" or "dirt", where the salvage already is. What they are used for -- crafting, farming, raising ground -- is still to come; so far they are only dug, carried and kept.
  • Blueprints. On a square of open ground: "Build a torch sconce here" (where there is rock at head height beside it) and "Build a stool here". An ordered dig or build is a blueprint: a pale outline stands on the spot until it is done, and if the one told to do it stops, anyone free takes it up.

Proved on seed 7 with the camp built: a dig ordered next to the camp took the rock out and left 2 loads of rough stone, both carried home into the materials; a sconce ordered on the next square was built and lit. (The dig broke through behind a sconce the camp had hung earlier, and that sconce fell -- the rule that was already there.)

Small and tiny creatures of a pair or a pack were bundled three (or nine) to a square as one creature that moved and fought as one. v2.162.11

2026-09-30 16:25 EDT - the campfire, found: files of your own were losing the built-in sounds

The user's screenshot of the mixer settled it: "Campfire loop -- silent: no sound loaded yet", the same for the torch and the drips, with the labyrinth's hum marked "4 yours". Loading had two halves racing: the built-in sounds decoding, each slot on its own, and the player's own dropped-in files. The built-in ones that finished while the player's files were still being read were written to a list that was then thrown away, so which sounds survived depended on timing -- and only in a browser with files of its own, which is why no test browser ever showed it (introduced in v2.162.1, when each slot started loading on its own). The player's files are now laid over the built-in sounds as they stand once the reading is done. Reproduced both ways: the same browser with four files of its own in the hum's slot lost the torch on v2.162.10 and keeps every sound on v2.162.11.

v2.162.1

2026-09-30 15:15 EDT - the sounds squeezed

The page had grown to 14 MB, almost all of it sound kept as raw .wav. The build now turns every .wav in art/sounds into a small mp3 (64 kbit/s for one channel, 96 for two) as it puts it in the page, made once and kept in dist/.snd; the two sounds that came as big mp3s (the campfire at 320 kbit/s, the ambiences) were turned back into .wav sources so they are squeezed the same way. Page 14.0 -> 5.9 MB. And each slot's sounds are now heard as soon as they are ready, instead of every slot waiting for every other: in the headless test browser the first sound went from about 70 s to 3 s. The start-up script installs the encoder (lameenc); without it the build puts the .wav in as before.

v2.162.0

2026-09-30 15:11 EDT - a voice for each crawler

"invisibly tag each crawler as male or female for vocal consistency. I don't really every want gender to be a part of the game for humans". Each crawler is given one of two voices, once, worked out from the match seed and who they are, and keeps it (it is saved with them). Nothing else in the game reads it and nothing shows it. The sounds tab gained voice and voice_of: a row naming another row in voice_of is that sound in the other voice, and a crawler of that voice uses it; where a sound has only one voice, everyone uses it. Every sound a crawler makes with their own voice -- pain, death, fear, eating, talk, laughter, sighs, singing, snoring, murmurs -- goes through this. The first with two voices is the murmurs: the user's female recordings, cut the same way into 43 phrases (art/sounds/mutter_f). All the sounds together now take about a minute to be ready in the headless test browser; the game plays while they load, silent until then.

v2.161.3

2026-09-30 14:14 EDT - ambushers

The user said yes to building "ambusher" as offered: an ambusher lies still where it is and cannot be seen, even in light, until a crawler comes within ambush_reach (2.5 m). A hostile or territorial one then strikes first from hiding -- the one it springs on is ambush_first (2) successes short in the first exchange -- and any other bolts. Once the company has studied its temper they know to look for it and it is seen like any other; left alone ambush_rehide (180 s) after springing, it hides again. Measured over five seeds, twenty minutes each: 12 to 31 ambushers per match, and ambushes fired on seeds 7 and 13 (bolting and striking both seen).

v2.157.2

2026-09-30 12:15 EDT - the blink

"The screen is blinking every few seconds." Every time a crawler's look changed, their figure was thrown away and not built again until the next frame, so they vanished for one frame; and the thrown-away figure's shapes and bones were never handed back. Dousing and relighting a lamp bumped the look too, 16-28 times a game minute across the company, although the flame is already put out on the figure as it stands. Now: dousing no longer rebuilds anyone, a rebuild happens in the same frame, and the old figure's shapes, bones and body material are freed.

The thing on the back of the shoulder in their screenshot is the same with or without v2.157.1's body-joining (compared in the wardrobe): it is carried kit, not a fault of the join.

v2.154.0

2026-09-30 10:00 EDT -- saving the light; settling at the camp

The user: "They should conserve handheld light sources, dousing them as an action, whenever they are at camp or another area with a better static light source. when resting they are now walking around. they should be sitting on stools and half walls, laying down, squatting, and chatting".

  • Saving the light (conserveLight, crawlers.js). A crawler who has stopped at the camp with its fire lit, or where a fire or sconce lights the ground to douse_light (4), puts their light out; it takes douse_seconds (2) and it stops burning down, and the flame is gone from the figure. Out of that light again they light it as they go. lightOf is now the light they are SHOWING; carriesLight is the one they HAVE, and every "does this crawler have a light" question (exploring, leading, hunting, jobs that need a light, making torches when short, the orders list, trading lights, the company count) asks the second, so a doused light still sends them out.
  • Settling (loaf, in wander). With nothing to do within 8 m of the fire, a crawler settles for about loaf_seconds (45): a free stool, the edge of a half wall, their own bedroll to lie on, the floor near the fire to squat, or a square beside someone already settled, to talk (facing them, with the talk marks). Nobody picks a spot someone else has claimed. The picture sits them, lays them down, or has them talking where they stand. Seed 5, work orders turned off: of 226 idle samples, 174 settled (83 talking, 52 on stools, 24 on walls, 13 squatting, 2 lying down), 24 on the move. [potential slop: every weight and number]

  • The hazards tab gains affinity, opposite and tint. Burn is Pyr, cold Hydōr, poison Gaea, shock Aēr, blight Erebos, glare Aethēr; burn opposes cold, shock opposes poison, blight opposes glare. [potential slop: Gaea for poison, the pairs, every colour]
  • populate takes the piece's biome: biome_strict (0.8) of rooms hold the quarter's own affinity, and the rest are never the opposite one. Lair guards and web-spinners obey the same rule. Measured on seed 5 (a cross of pieces four out in each direction): west (cold) 23 of 32 groups Hydōr and no Pyr, east (poison) 43 of 47 Gaea and no Aēr, south (burn) 26 of 35 Pyr and no Hydōr; before the guard fix, north (shock) had one Gaea, a lair guard.
  • Rock and built stone are tinted by the quarter's colour (a tint vertex attribute multiplied into the block shader), up to biome_tint (0.35) once hazard_ramp pieces out; the start piece is untinted. The lighting is not changed.

v2.150.0

2026-09-29 19:47 EDT -- hostile means hostile; food runs out

The user, on "hostile creatures don't always attack": "All three. Also there should be no hostile creatures in the starting room. So I was picturing a LOT more food plants (and animals to a lesser extent) that the crawlers would eat to extinction and have to farm or move on".

  • A hostile creature strikes after 2 seconds over somebody (menace_strike 4 -> 2).
  • Backing away no longer resets that count while it gives chase (c.chasing), so it catches up and strikes.
  • Hostile outranks skittish and light-shy: a hostile kind no longer runs from a light or on its temper.
  • populate skips hostile kinds in the room the company comes down in.
  • Far more wild plants (plants_room 3 -> 10, caves still double) and a few more creatures (creature_room 0.6 -> 0.75).
  • A wild plant picked wild_picks (3) times is picked out for good, and the log says so. Animals already die out when a group is wiped out. So the ground round a camp is eaten bare: farm the cuttings, or move on. Grazing creatures still only set a plant back; they do not pick it out. [potential slop: every number]

v2.149.0

2026-09-29 16:59 EDT -- gates and hazards, and a biome to each quarter

The user, on "blockages and hazards that need building/tools to access/traverse ... gated areas like Metroid or Zelda": "Build them all. Yes, common and rewarding. Elemental stuff organized into biomes, NSEW? Different layout every seed".

  • src/gates.js, and two new tabs, gates and hazards. A room away from the start may have a GATE across every way in (gate_chance): chasm (bridge), high ledge (ladder), deep water (raft, or wade soaked), collapsed passage (dig), rotten floor (board over, or a Focusing throw under a load), locked gate (force), sealed door (Studying), thorns and webs (cut, or burn -- which wakes what is inside; webs always have an Arthro behind them), a squeeze (the other ways in filled with rock; nothing bigger than small gets through until it is widened), a lair (a big hostile guard; open once it is dead or tame). Blocking ones are left out of World.steps, so no walk goes through. Behind each, gate_reward kit.
  • Seen within gate_reach of the fire, a gate is a job like any other, paid in wood, stone and cloth from the camp. Done, it stays open, and the picture shows what was built (planks, ladder, raft, boards).
  • HAZARD rooms: six elements, one to each quarter (north, east, south, west, up a story, down a story), shuffled per seed, commoner further out (hazard_room, hazard_ramp). Every hazard_seconds on the floor, a Persisting throw against hazard_need less the kit's resistance to that element; failed, a wound. Burn, cold, glare and shock have a job to make the room safe (causeway, brazier, shade, learning the strikes); spores need a Breathing Cloth (new, cloth:1, poison 2); a dark hollow needs a light of blight_light. Walks avoid hazard floors (path_cost_hazard).
  • Hover shows each gate and hazard with what it takes; barred ways are amber dots on the map. Old saves gain the new jobs.
  • World.edge answers "no way out" for a square of rock instead of throwing; a squeeze filled a square something was asked about, and the first run crashed on it.

[potential slop: every row, word, number and picture]

v2.148.4

2026-09-28 20:24 EDT -- nine damage types

The user: "More damage types" -- burn, cold, poison, shock, blight (Erebos), glare (Aethēr) and acid -- and "combine acid and poison damage". So nine: cut, pierce, crush, burn, cold, poison (venom, spores, rot, acid), shock, blight, glare. How they are dealt and resisted is [potential slop], all in the sheet:

  • An Arcanist strikes with its affinity's element (Pyr burn, Hydōr cold, Aēr shock, Erebos blight, Aethēr glare; Gaea crush). Fungi (spores) and Oozes (acid) deal poison unless their bodies carry something else. Of the 432 kinds, 67 now strike with one of the new types.

  • Armour and clothes have a resistance column for each new type: the warmest cloaks keep off cold, a hood or hooded cloak glare, the hide shield burn, the slicked cloak and chitin coat poison, the glowmoss wreath blight. The Venom Knife now deals poison.

v2.148.3

2026-09-28 20:20 EDT -- fish still breathe water; traders deal in living things

From the user's rulebook review:

  • "aer fish can SOMETIMES TEND TO FLY vs other fish, but they are still fish so they need to return to water to breathe": a fish that flies or burrows is born in a room with water, holds its breath away from it (fish_breath 180 s), makes for the nearest water when a third is left, and past it takes a wound every fish_gasp seconds until it dies ("dies, too long out of the water"). Seen: a gustpike 8 steps from water went back and breathed; one with no water in reach died.
  • "Mostly livestock, some working animals, rarely slaves. Merchants TEND to specialize in one thing or another. There is a morale cost (more severe among certain personality traits) the more unethical the exploitation": each trader deals mostly in kit, livestock, working beasts or people (trader_kit/livestock/beast/person 5/3/1.5/0.5), with a little of the others. The company buys livestock when the larder is low and slaughters it when it runs lower; a working beast when it has none; a person only when short of hands, and only through someone unscrupulous (Honesty-Humility -2 or lower). A bought person wears an iron collar (rule 4). Buying a beast costs the company a little sanity; buying a person a great deal, most among the fair-minded and the kind, and keeping someone in a collar wears on the fair-minded every person_unease_every minutes. Seen: a bobber bought and slaughtered for 3 rations, a nimbooze bought to pull, and a person bought -- the fair-minded Marlo fell to 1.9 sanity, the buyer stayed at 5.

Every number, word and weight is [potential slop].

v2.148.0

2026-09-28 19:46 EDT -- rooted tame ones are dug up and carried

The user, on rooted creatures that can be tamed but cannot follow: "1 c" [gospel] -- option (C), "a tamed rooted one is dug up and carried in the packs, and planted again at each camp".

  • Tamed, a rooted one is dug up by whoever tamed it and carried in their arms back to the fire, and planted there.
  • When the camp moves it is dug up again, takes a load of room in someone's pack, is carried on the march, and is planted by the new fire. With no room anywhere it is left where it grows, and is wild again.
  • If whoever carries it falls or leaves, it takes root where it is dropped.

Seen on seed 11: a magmallow carried 24 m and planted by the fire in 22 seconds; at the next move, dug up and planted again at the new camp. How it is held, and the words, are [potential slop].

Also from the user: the idle catch-up (time away played out quickly) is "not till post-release" [gospel].

v2.146.1

2026-09-27 23:09 EDT -- the watch passes to the least tired, with no clock

v2.146.0

2026-09-27 22:58 EDT -- making it up, and gossip

Two more things crawlers do with each other (the user: "crawler interactions. think 'the sims'" [gospel]; these two are my design [potential slop]):

  • Making it up. Two with bad blood, sitting near each other, may try to make it up instead of arguing (makeup_chance, more often from the agreeable). A Bargaining throw, harder when it is a real grudge: taken, the bond rises by two and both remember it; refused, it stings the one who tried.
  • Gossip. A talk turns to someone who is not there (gossip_chance, more often from the sly). If it takes, how the teller feels about them rubs off on the listener, a point either way -- so a grudge spreads round the fire, and so does a good name.

v2.145.0

2026-09-27 22:52 EDT -- a sledge, and a wagon

The user, long ago: "they will eventually break camp and move on as they exhaust areas, perhaps even building sledges and eventually wagons pulled by tamed beasts" [gospel]. When each gets built, and every shape, is my design [potential slop]:

  • A sledge is built at camp (Building, sledge_wood loads of wood) once the camp holds more than their packs could carry away. When they move, the strongest free back drags it, and it holds sledge_loads loads in place of their pack.
  • A wagon is built (Building, harder, wagon_wood) once a large or huge tame creature lives at camp. When they move, the beast is put to it, and it holds wagon_loads in place of what the beast would carry on its back.
  • Both are real things: parked near the fire at camp (runners and planks; a plank bed on four wheels with shafts), drawn loaded behind whoever pulls them on the march, and drawn up by the new fire when they arrive. Nobody free to pull one: it is left by the old fire.
  • The watch handover in v2.144.0 never happened: a tired watcher was dropped by the old rule before the handover was looked at, and a shift of 60 minutes was longer than a night (nights run 3-5 minutes of game time). The handover is now checked first, and a shift is 1.5 minutes.

v2.144.0

2026-09-27 21:09 EDT -- sleeping together, and keeping watch in shifts

The user, asked for ways the company could mostly sleep at the same time: "All of the above" and "But don't forget keeping watch in shifts" [gospel]. Everything below is my design [potential slop], and every amount is a sheet row.

  • Sleepiness is catching: awake within sleep_catch_reach of a sleeper tires you faster, and a friend asleep more so (activities rows 'someone asleep near', 'a friend asleep near').
  • The last ones up are lonely ('last ones up'), once half the company is abed.
  • The fire is banked once most of the company sleeps: its light falls to fire_banked_light and it burns at fire_banked_burn of the normal rate. Only the watch feeds it (stoke jobs already went to the watcher).
  • Morning noise: a rested sleeper near somebody up and about can be woken (wake_noise_chance).
  • Noise spoils sleep ('noisy sleep'), and a crawler whose sleep has been spoiled waits for quiet before going to bed.
  • A hot meal and a hearty stew make them drowsy (fatigue -0.5).
  • Lights out: every lights_out_seconds, if most of those up at camp are flagging, the best leader rolls Leading ('Call it a night'); on a success the tired ones turn in, at most once per lights_out_rest.
  • Safety in numbers: nobody goes to bed of their own accord unless a watch is set or somebody is already asleep (dropping from exhaustion still happens).
  • Shifts: after watch_shift_minutes, or once the watcher is worn down, they wake the most rested sleeper to take over.

Measured, seed 3, 30 minutes: sleep came in shared nights (up to 6 of 8 asleep at once) instead of scattered naps, the fire was banked, and lights out was called and heeded.

v2.143.3

2026-09-27 19:41 EDT -- lights out for the night

The user: "They would put out portable light sources when sleeping" [gospel]. A crawler's own lights -- the lamp, torch or candle in their hand, and anything lit that they wear -- go out when they settle in for the night: they light nothing, and a torch or candle does not burn down while they sleep. The lamp set down beside the bed is set down dark, and the flame comes back when they are up and have taken it in hand again. Measured: a sleeper's square went from light 3 to 0 (away from the fire), and their candle's time left did not move over a minute asleep. The camp's fire and its sconces are not portable, and stay lit.

v2.143.2

2026-09-27 19:12 EDT -- going to bed and getting up, as a person does it

The user: "Make real laying down and getting up animations. Probably 5 seconds, longer if they are doffing and donning gear, which must also be animated as real human movements, not just tilting" [gospel]. The tilt is gone; it is a sequence of the body's own joints now, on the game's clock:

  • Kit off, one piece at a time (doff_seconds 2 each): a weapon or a light -- crouch and set it down at the side with that hand; the pack -- hands up to the straps, shrug it off the shoulders, crouch and lower it; heavy armour -- arms up and crossed, pulled off over the bowed head, then crouch and lay it down. Each piece leaves them, and appears beside the bed, the moment their hands let go of it.
  • Lying down (lie_seconds 5): a squat; down onto the seat with the legs sliding out and the hands going back to take the weight; back onto the elbows; flat, arms at the sides, on the bedroll.
  • Getting up is the same run backwards, then the kit goes back on in the other order, each piece taken up from the pile as their hands reach it. Nothing else happens meanwhile: measured, a crawler woken with somewhere to go stays on their square through 5 seconds of getting up and three pieces of kit, and sets off after.
  • The panel says which: "putting their kit down", "lying down", "getting up", "putting their kit back on".

[potential slop: every angle and the order of the kit]

v2.143.1

2026-09-27 18:44 EDT -- lying down and getting up work again

The user: "I don't think laying down or getting up animations are working" [gospel]. They were not, since v2.143.0: putting kit down for the night rebuilds the figure (it is drawn without the kit), and the animation's progress was kept on the figure, so the new figure started from nothing and jumped straight to lying flat -- and the same on waking. The progress is kept with the crawler now. Measured with kit being put down: the body's tilt goes 0, -0.02, -0.46, -1.15, -1.56, -1.57 over the second lying down, and -1.15, -0.56, -0.05, 0 getting up.

v2.143.0

2026-09-27 17:52 EDT -- kit put down for the night

The user: "While sleeping, Crawlers should stow handheld items, backpacks, and heavy armor on the ground or leaning against walls near their bedroll. I want to see the real thing, not placeholders" [gospel].

  • Settling into bed, a crawler puts down what is in their hands, the pack off their back, and armour too heavy to sleep in (stow_armour_lb 8: the gambeson, the chitin coat and the bone lamellar). Their figure is drawn without them; waking, they take them up again.
  • The real things: each piece beside the bed is built by the very code that draws it on the body -- the same sword, lantern, pack or coat of plates -- not a stand-in. The pack and armour shapes were pulled out of the figure into their own pieces so the body and the pile share them.
  • Anything taller than 60 cm (a staff, a spear, a long-handled tool) leans against a wall of their square if it has one; everything else lies on the floor along the side of the bedroll, the pack stood upright and armour laid out flat. A lit lantern or candle set down keeps its flame. [potential slop: the placing]

v2.142.4

2026-09-27 17:46 EDT -- getting up takes time in the game, not only in the picture

The user: "Close that side effect by giving time to the act of getting up and laying down" [gospel]. Lying down and getting up now take lie_seconds in the game itself, not only in the picture: for that long after waking a crawler does nothing but get up, so they no longer slide off across the floor while still rising; settling in, they are "lying down" and sleep on. Measured: woken with somewhere to go, a crawler stays on their square for the whole second the figure takes to stand, and sets off the moment it is up.

v2.142.3

2026-09-27 17:44 EDT -- lying down and getting up

The user: "Add animations for getting up and laying down in bed" [gospel]. Going to sleep, a crawler now sinks into a crouch and then rolls back until they lie flat along the bedroll; waking, they sit up, come to a crouch and stand. It takes lie_seconds (1.2) of the game's clock, so it runs faster at higher speeds and stops when paused. A fall, a collapse or being carried is still sudden. Measured: the body's tilt goes 0, 0, -0.56, -1.23, -1.57 over the second lying down, and back the same way getting up. [potential slop: the shape of it]

vIdeas kept: boardgame tactility (offered after v2.140.0, not built)

The user asked for ideas for "boardgame tactility" (rulebook 11, "the hands") and asked for them to be kept here. None is built; they wait on the user's picks. [potential slop, every one]

1. Dice you see -- every throw drops real six-sided dice into its ledger line, successes standing out, not a string of numbers. 2. Needs as pegs -- each of the six needs a peg in a row of six holes on the crawler's card, moving a hole at a time. 3. Wound tokens -- three empty slots on each face card that fill with red tokens; creatures too, once studied. 4. Kit as cards -- each piece of gear a small card; drag one from the store onto a crawler's face to hand it over, or drop it on them to pin it. 5. The order of work as a hand of cards -- drag to reorder, flip one face-down to switch that job off. 6. Coin and materials as stacked chits -- little stacks in the camp panel; drag chits onto a trader's wares to buy. 7. Moving camp with a banner -- drag a banner token onto a room on the Map to suggest moving there. 8. An exploration flag -- drag a flag onto the Map or the world to say "explore over there". 9. Pips placed by hand -- a new skill point drops a wooden pip into its slot with a click. 10. An hourglass for speed -- the speed buttons as an hourglass you turn; laid on its side is paused. 11. Pressed wooden buttons -- buttons sink when pressed and cast a small shadow. 12. A table edge -- panels on a dark wooden rim round the screen, like pieces laid on a table.

My suggestion at the time: 1, 3, 4 and 5 first.

v2.139.0

2026-09-27 11:07 EDT -- traders and coin

The user, on the rulebook's traders: "A & B" -- living traders in rooms and coins, and swapping goods [gospel]. Everything below is [potential slop].

  • Traders are people already living down here, found when a room is investigated (trader_chance, 12% of rooms). They never join the company and never leave their room: they squat by their bundled wares with a lantern, which lights the room. Each has a few pieces of kit and some rations. Point at one to see what they have and what it costs.
  • Coin: the company comes down with a few coins (start_coins) and turns up old ones now and then in rooms they investigate (coins_chance). A price is what a thing is made of, a coin a material, plus a base and its grade.
  • Trading: a crawler goes to a trader within trade_reach of the camp when the trader has something that would better one of the company's kit -- or rations, when the larder is low -- and the company can pay. They pay in coin first, then in goods from the store (a material a coin): both ways the user asked for. The deal is a Bargaining throw (the tasks tab's trade row; fine clothes make it easier): made, the price comes down by what they threw over; failed, the trader will not deal for a while. Kit bought is carried home to the store. The coach can order it: pick a crawler, pick the trader, "Trade with ...".
  • tests/trade.mjs: seeds 1, 7, 23, two and a half hours -- 8 traders found, 16 haggles, 10 deals (rations, a torch, a tool belt, a board shield). Coin piles up (24 to 63 by the end): balance, for later.

v2.138.0

2026-09-27 11:02 EDT -- investigating, and rooms that act by what is in them

The user, on the rulebook's section 9: "call it 'investigating' here and in-game, not reading. These options should be emergent behaviors. (A collapsed room has difficult terrain and rubble; an infested room itself doesn't cause anxiety, the presence of dangerous creatures does; etc)" and, on the mood: "more grimdark and desperate" [gospel].

  • Reading a room is investigating it now, everywhere it is said: the order, the ledger, the doing line, the Rooms window ("not investigated"), and the title ("the Investigator").
  • A room's name no longer does anything by itself. The activities tab loses haunted, burial, silent, chapel, library, refectory and bathhouse; what is left is physical (moss, water, soot). In their place, two things that are really there: something hostile in sight frays the nerves, and the dead close by -- remains, a grave, a body nobody has buried -- weighs on them. A collapsed room was already rubble underfoot, which takes a better throw to cross. The camp is still chosen by how a room feels, which now means only what is physically in it. [potential slop: the distances and rates]
  • The rulebook's mood paragraph is rewritten, darker and more desperate [potential slop: every word].

v2.137.1

2026-09-27 10:54 EDT -- a die per floor; animals do no human work

The ANIMAL rule (every Intellect skill harder) is struck from the rulebook; in its place, creatures never attempt human work -- building, bargaining, reading, mapping, studying, singing, teaching. Nothing in the code let them, so no behaviour changed there; the rulebook now says it. Both are listed in the roadmap under the balance pass at the end.

2026-09-27 10:33 EDT -- study takes a lifetime, depth, and rooms named for who lives there

The rulebook review, section 9, the user's answers [gospel]: hearing -- "yes" (already built: someone reading hears best, someone hammering worst); study -- "B", each stage twice the last; depth -- "A, and deeper mean further from zero, up or down"; how crawlers decide about a creature -- "yes"; room names -- "They might slap an additional name on it like 'Bob's House, abandoned dwarven mill'".

v2.136.1

2026-09-27 10:23 EDT -- nobody sleeps at the old camp

v2.136.0

2026-09-27 10:05 EDT -- taming

The user: "Proceed with taming" [gospel] (parked since "3C: wait"). Everything below is [potential slop].

  • Who can be tamed: a creature whose kind is tameable (124 of the 432), on its own (not a pair, pack or swarm), once the company has studied its temper -- that is how they know it can be.
  • How: when the larder holds a few rations, someone goes to it with one in hand and throws Grappling ("handling animals"; the tasks tab's new tame row, 2 successes, a die more for large, two for huge, one more for a hostile kind). Taken, it trusts them a little more; three trust and it is tame. Refused, it shies off -- or, a hostile kind given nothing at all, goes for them. Each visit leaves it alone a while (tame_rest), so it takes several. A creature being coaxed lets its coaxer come close unless it is skittish or shy of their light. The coach can order it too: pick a crawler, pick the creature, "Tame it".
  • A tame creature wears a rope collar, keeps within a few metres of the fire (or of its keeper when there is no camp, or on the march), eats a ration from the larder every hour, and trusts them less for every meal missed -- with none left it goes wild again. A hostile or territorial one growls at anything hostile coming near, and the sleepers wake. A large or huge one carries loads when the camp moves (beast_loads), before anyone else is loaded -- the start of the user's "wagons pulled by tamed beasts". It is never hunted, foraged, snared or fought by the company, and a shifting piece of the labyrinth does not take it.
  • Knobs: tame_reach, tame_rest, tame_food, tame_max (3), tame_heel, tame_feed_minutes, beast_loads.
  • tests/tame.mjs (temper known and the larder kept stocked, 150 minutes): seeds 1, 7, 23 -- 30 throws, 3 tamed, and a tame one kept near the fire 98% of the time. Found on the way: the foragers gathered a tame plant-creature up to eat. No growl has been seen in a run yet: the kinds that got tamed were gentle ones.

v2.135.2

2026-09-27 10:00 EDT -- a steady ledger, buttons that fire, a map button

The user: "Ledger scrolling stopped being smooth movement - what happened? Keep the ledger to 3 lines so it stops resizing. Also I can see a seam where the top border connects. Buttons often won't fire, especially at higher speeds. Add a map button to the quick menu bar" [gospel].

  • What happened: on a phone the small ledger was measured to fit its last three entries every frame and then jumped to the bottom, which both resized it and cut short the smooth scroll. It is now a fixed three lines (either way up), so the newest entry slides in. On a big screen it is a fixed height too.
  • The seam: the scrolling paper now fades in over the still paper along its top and bottom few pixels instead of meeting it at a line.
  • Buttons: the panel and the windows rebuilt their buttons whenever their words changed, and at speed that is nearly every frame, so a press and its release landed on two different buttons. Nothing that holds buttons is rewritten now while a finger or mouse button is down on it.

v2.135.1

2026-09-27 09:43 EDT -- Guard, and a shield is its tool

The user: "a shield is a tool that makes the Guard action easier (includes dodging and parrying etc concepts)" [gospel]. Whoever a creature is about to hit now throws to Guard -- ducking, parrying, taking it on a shield -- with the skill of what is in their hands (the weights tab: a shield's weight first, then the weapon's; bare hands Brawling), against the creature's hits. A shield is the tool for Guard (the tasks tab, guard), so it makes the throw easier, as any tool does its job. Guarded, the blow misses; a hard one wears the shield a rung. v2.135.0

2026-09-27 08:40 EDT -- shields block with a throw of their own

The user, asked whether blocking is armour or its own throw: "yes it is done by skills. Make light medium and heavy categories like weapons" [gospel]. Shields no longer count as armour. A crawler with a shield who is about to be hit throws to block: the skill the shield's weight uses (the same weights tab as weapons: light Focusing, medium Brawling, heavy Labouring) against the creature's hits, made easier by the shield's grade. A block stops the blow; a hard one wears the shield a rung. Failing a block teaches, like any throw. The Buckler is light, the Hide Shield and Shell Targe medium, the Board Shield heavy. A shield's card says its weight and the skill it blocks with. v2.133.0

2026-09-27 03:53 EDT -- a chipped blade is seen

Rule 4's own example -- "A chipped blade is seen" -- was only half true: mending dulls a piece's colour a rung at a time, but a blade showed nothing of its wear, and kit worn right through (wanting mending) looked whole until it was mended. Now a sword, a knife, a spade or a hatchet carries a dark notch in its edge for every rung it has worn down, and one more while it wants mending (up to three), in the hand or sheathed; and a tunic, breeches or a cloak worn through shows a dark tear. The figure is made again the moment a piece wears through. [potential slop, the marks]

v2.132.0

2026-09-27 02:53 EDT -- a commotion in the dark

The rulebook (section 10) has always said: "Noise out of sight still registers, as a commotion: an icon marks where it came from, and nothing is written down, because nobody saw it happen." It was never built. Now a creature killing another, or one getting loose from another, or one smashing through rock, in the dark or out of sight but within commotion_reach (18 m) of anyone in the company on that story, leaves a red "!" hanging over the spot for commotion_seconds (60), fading as it ages. Nothing goes on the ledger. It can be pointed at: "A commotion -- something happened here that nobody saw". Rare: one in three 33-minute games (seeds 2, 5, 13). [potential slop: the mark, the reach]

v2.131.0

2026-09-27 02:23 EDT -- what happens to the one left behind

Following on v2.129.0's "or maybe even leave them behind" [potential slop, all of it]: whoever the company leaves lies where they were and, every selftend_seconds (120), tries to see to their own hurts -- a Tending throw needing selftend_need (3), harder than being tended. If it takes they get up alone, scarred, and go after the company (to its camp, or where it is marching). Anyone who makes it back is one of the company again, but every bond with them falls abandon_grudge (3), their temper turns pricklier, and everyone remembers it. If hunger or the dark take them first, they die alone.

v2.129.0

2026-09-27 02:12 EDT -- a sled for someone who cannot walk, or left behind

From the roadmap, the user's own note: "They could build a sled for someone who cannot walk, or maybe even leave them behind. This is a grimdark universe. Note that for later." Until now the company refused to move while anyone was down. All of the how is [potential slop]:

  • Anyone still lying where they fell is brought in first ("not before the fallen are brought in").
  • Once the company has decided to move and knows where, each laid-up crawler gets a "Build a sled" job (a new task row: Building, 3 units, sled_wood 2 loads). Packing waits for it. When they set off the strongest back (Labouring) takes up the sled's rope -- or, with no sled, takes them on their shoulders. A sled costs its bearer sled_loads (2) of pack room; shoulders cost all of it, so more is left behind.
  • With no wood for sleds, a pressing reason to go (danger, or nothing to eat) and nobody there who counts them a friend, each check has abandon_chance (0.5) of leaving them behind. Everyone remembers it; so do they.
  • At the new camp they are laid down and a tending job goes up.
  • The sled is drawn: two runners and a bed of poles, trailing behind whoever drags it, the one on it lying along it.

tests/sled.mjs: seed 3 with the wood, sled built, dragged 2,672 steps, laid down and tended at the new camp.

2026-09-27 02:02 EDT -- v2.127.1 -- margins, portraits, close buttons, riding what you picked, the watch

The user, in one list: a little margin inside the parchment; portraits each with their own torn edge; portrait pixels matched to the game's, zooming in on faces if need be, and a SnorriCam; "a red square x button in the other font to close menus"; closing a character's panel keeps them selected and followed, and the camera follows a picked creature too; "watching" becomes "keeping watch", milling about the room, sitting, eyeing the exits. A portrait's canvas is sized so one of its pixels is one game pixel on the screen (it follows the zoom); the frame shows portrait_m (0.85) metres, head and shoulders.   • The watch: the ledger and panels say "keeping watch". Every watch_stay (25) seconds, half again either way, the watcher moves to another square of the camp's room -- near one of its ways out seven times in ten, a little way in from it -- turns to face the nearest way out, and sits (watch_sit, 0.4) or stands there.
