v2.121.0

2026-09-27 00:41 EDT -- v2.121.1 -- nobody walks on the fire, and the store has places of its own

The user: "Crawlers are frequently stepping on the fire ... Nobody would ever step on a campfire in real life - it would probably cause severe injury (add that if they fall/get pushed onto one)"; "The Store should be a separate area(s) from the campfire and follow the rule that things are always visible: staves lean against walls, fuel is stacked neatly along the wall, ingredients hang in crafted baskets away from vermin"; and "How did campfire movement cost drift?"

The fire. The number never moved: path_cost_fire has been 50 since v2.0.13. What changed is everything round it. A cost only makes a walk prefer another way; it never forbids one, and a walk treats a square somebody is standing on as a wall. As more work came to be done beside the fire (cooking, crafting, mending, mapping, welcoming, dressing wounds, the seats and stools), the ring round it filled with people, the way round was shut, and the cheapest way left was straight across, 50 or not. And a cold hearth is a square like any other: seed 1 had someone resting on it when it was relit. Measured over 6,000 steps: v2.120.0, 278 (seed 1) and 469 (seed 7) crawler-steps spent standing on a lit fire; v2.121.1, 1 and 0 -- the 1 is the moment of relighting, and they step off. Now a lit fire is never part of a walk (not a cost: a wall), nobody wanders onto one, a walk that would step onto one stops, and anyone on a hearth when it is lit steps off. Falling in [potential slop]: a stumble, or a blow taken in a fight, right beside a lit fire puts someone into it one time in fire_fall (0.3): a wound, pain, a burn scar on the cheek and forearm that stays, and the ledger says so.

The store [potential slop, every shape and place]. It was a list hung on the campfire, drawn nowhere. As soon as the camp stands, three places are set against walls 2.5-7 m from the fire: the store (a plank shelf on stones: long kit -- staves, spears, clubs, axes, spades, shields -- leans against the wall, the rest lies on the shelf in its own colour), the woodpile (a log for every load, stacked in rows along the wall on two stones) and the larder (a rail on two pegs, below the wall's fading top metre, with a woven basket hung for every two rations, greens hanging in bunches, a pot beneath when there are hot meals or stew). Each can be pointed at and says what it holds. Kit is taken where it is kept: somebody who would take something walks over to the store first. Seeds 1, 3, 7, 11 after 5,000 steps: all three places set in every camp, 2-3 pieces of kit taken from the store in each, no errors.

v2.119.0

2026-09-27 00:28 EDT -- sconces face their own wall, for real this time

Reported with four views of one: "sometimes they are on the wrong side of the voxel and float in midair opposite the wall they are supposed to be on." The cause was never the height (v2.115.0) or the rock (v2.116.0): a sconce is first drawn from its job while it is being fitted, that picture was made without the wall it belongs to, so it took the default facing -- and the facing was set only when the picture was first made, so it kept it after the job was done. Every sconce whose wall was not on that one side hung on the far side of its square. The job's picture now carries the wall, and the facing is set every frame. tests/sconce.mjs checks where each plate really is against its wall: v2.118.0, 6 of 16 wrong (seeds 1, 3, 7, 11); v2.119.0, 0 of 16.

2026-09-27 00:01 EDT -- a smooth ledger, sconces on their walls, small things first under the pointer

v2.114.0

2026-09-26 23:47 EDT -- faces, temper in the body, and temper that moves

Asked: yes to temper showing on the figure "plus facial expressions. Give them any features needed like eyebrows etc. (First lose the stripe across their forehead)", and yes to big events shifting a leaning. All shapes and choices here are [potential slop].

  • The stripe: two things read as one line across the forehead. The eyes were two wide blocks set high that met across the nose, and the hair sat on the head as a brim whose underside the fire lit pale. The hair now meets the head flush.
  • The face: two small eyes lower down, a brow over each (hair colour, darker), a mouth in two halves -- each its own mesh so pose() can move it. Brows tilt (cross or worried) and lift, the mouth turns up or down and opens, the eyes close. Temper sets the face at rest (Prickly cross, Anxious worried, Lively smiling); what happens takes it over: asleep or dead eyes shut, down in pain, fleeing afraid, a shout when driving off, teeth set in a fight, singing, arguing, laughing at a joke or a tale, talking, chewing, frayed, grieving, cold or hungry, and pleased after getting what they wanted. A dressed wound's band moved up off the eyes.
  • The body: standing, walking and sitting, each leaning shows by how far it leans. Anxious hunch and glance about, Steely stand straight chin up, Lively bounce on their toes, Withdrawn look down, Prickly stand elbows out, Careless slouch, Diligent stand upright, Curious cock their head and look about, Sly throw sideways looks.
  • Temper moves: sheet tab temper_shifts -- a friend's death makes someone more Anxious, a kill more Steely, a falling-out more Prickly, a new friend more Lively, being given half a meal more Fair, reading a room more Curious. One step at a time, the same leaning at most once in temper_shift_hours (2) of play; the ledger says so.

v2.113.0

2026-09-26 18:57 EDT -- temper: HEXACO laid over the six attributes

Asked: "Try (mark slop) to tie HEXACO to the current attributes. Have it effect behavior in the appropriate ways depending on strength of leaning" [gospel]. Everything else here is [potential slop].

Every crawler leans somewhere on all six (src/temper.js, sheet tab temper), from -3 to 3. The attribute pulls the leaning (temper_attr), the person does the rest (temper_self), and each step of a leaning moves a choice by temper_pull (15%). Agility leans Sly, Endurance Steely, Presence Lively, Might Prickly, Willpower Diligent, Intellect Curious. A leaning never touches dice (rule 2); it changes what they choose:

  • H Fair / Sly: sharing a meal with someone who is not a friend; how much taking good kit is grudged.
  • E Anxious / Steely: the odds wanted before a fight; how long dread lasts; how fast they fray.
  • X Lively / Withdrawn: starting talks; wanting company and songs; how fast they get lonely.
  • A Gentle / Prickly: arguing; grudging kit.
  • C Diligent / Careless: chasing their own wants; the careless now and then pass a job up.
  • O Curious / Incurious: how far they explore; the incurious now and then pass up exploring, reading and studying.

The leanings are worked out from the seed and the person, not thrown on the game's dice, so worlds are unchanged and old saves and newcomers get one. The character sheet shows a Temper row ("very Lively, a little Sly..."). Not yet visible on the figure (rule 4) -- a posture per leaning is the next step.

v2.112.0

2026-09-26 18:04 EDT -- a save code, to carry a game to another device

Asked, choosing option 2 of three ("copy a code, paste it on the other device") over a file or an online account. "Save code" on the bar shows the whole game as a code (the saved game, gzip-squeezed and written in letters, behind a CRAWLERS1: mark) with a Copy button. "Paste a save code" on the opening screen takes one, reads it all the way through before replacing anything, keeps it as this device's saved game and opens it. A stray paste or a cut-short code is refused with a reason. Measured on seed 7 after 900 steps: about 10,000 letters. tests/savecode.mjs carries a game from one browser to a second one with its own storage and a different world.

v2.111.0

2026-09-26 17:55 EDT -- saved games

Asked: "Work on save games". Option B from the roadmap: the match is kept on the device and picks up where it was left. It saves every autosave_seconds (60, a new knob) and whenever the page is put away or closed. The opening screen offers Continue (with the date, time played and how many are alive) or New game.

How: the match is a web of objects pointing at each other, so it is written as a numbered list (src/save.js). Sheet rows are written by tab and name and read back as the sheet's own rows, so a save follows the sheet as it is now; creature kinds are written as their three tags; the ground is not written at all -- it is regenerated from the seed and only the record of changes and what was seen is kept, the same record the endless world keeps of a piece it forgets. The dice carry on from where they were. The ledger and chronicle moved onto the match so they are kept too. Measured on seed 7 after 1,200 steps: 45 KB, 93 ms to write, no object of an unknown kind skipped (tests/saveload.mjs). [potential slop: 60 s interval.]

v2.109.1

2026-09-26 01:06 EDT -- v2.109.4 -- getting past in narrow places

Found when the orders test went red: an ordered sleep never reached the bed.

  • Blocked by a creature, they gave up the walk before the old "squeeze past it" rule could kick in -- and now that creatures sleep, one lies across a way for a long time. With no way round a creature, they keep the walk and squeeze past.
  • Two people head-on in a one-wide corridor both gave up. On the march they already shuffled past each other; now anyone walking does. Someone standing still (working, sleeping) is still waited for or gone round.
  • Partners swapping bedrolls: anyone on their way to a bed that just changed hands starts again for their new one.
  • "Dig a latrine" is in the camp orders (v2.109.1).

Two of the creature diets did nothing. Lighting the sconce again, or feeding the fire, is the answer when it gets through.

v2.106.10

Grazers eat in the dark, so a plant just vanished. Now a picked or grazed plant leaves a patch of stubble, which can be hovered, and its tooltip says it was eaten down by something (or picked), and how many minutes until it grows back; a grazed garden bed says so too.

v2.106.6

2026-09-25 23:19 EDT -- v2.106.7 -- tales by the fire [potential slop]

Sitting together, a crawler may tell another a tale of something that happened to them, taken from their "Lately" list -- "Pell tells Rue about the time they killed the dazzlewyrm", "Rue tells Sage about beating Corin at knucklebones" (tale_chance). An Entertaining throw: told well it lifts the listener; told badly they lose the thread (and learn, rule 1). A violet speech mark. Setting a snare and searching a nest now have their own work movements (v2.106.6).

The reach census now covers the older mechanics as well (fishing, locks, mending, making, the watch, strangers, sconces, moving camp...). In 8 hours over four worlds everything fired but three that are rare by design: a grazer seen at it (they graze in the dark), gathering a rooted creature, and driving one off (only chosen when the company cannot win the fight, and nine crawlers nearly always can).

v2.106.5

A plant tagged 'light' (the Glowcap) gives a little light of its own (glow_plant, 2 -- a candle is 3) until something eats or picks it, and its caps shine. So pale patches show in the dark of the caves, and a creature standing among them can be seen.

v2.106.0

2026-09-25 23:04 EDT -- v2.106.1 -- known for something [potential slop]

A crawler who has done one thing often enough, and more often than anyone else living in the company, is known for it, and the title goes after their name in the tooltip, the panel and the Company window: the Slayer, the Snarer, the Singer, the Cook, the Teacher, the Lucky, the Reader, the Mapmaker, the Nest-robber, the Steadfast, the Mender, the Fisher, the Forager, the Gravedigger. The words and how many deeds each takes are a new titles tab (People). It only names them; nothing else changes. First cut gave a title to anyone past the count, and nearly everybody was the Cook or the Mender; now it is the company's best at it. After 2½ hours on seed 5: three of nine are known for something.

v2.105.3

2026-09-25 22:59 EDT -- shooing grazers off the garden [potential slop]

A grazer within 6 m of a garden bed is run at by whoever is awake within garden_watch metres of it -- the same Leading contest as driving off anything -- and if it is driven off it does not eat. Not yet seen in a match: over 90 minutes on seeds 5 and 11 no grazer went for a garden at all (125 grazings, all wild).

v2.105.0

2026-09-25 22:47 EDT -- v2.105.2 -- grazers, and the plants come back

Found while building grazing: no plant had grown anywhere since v2.79.0. The eight forageables (Cave Mushroom, Pale Tuber, Glowcap, Rock Lichen, Blind Cress, Bone Ear, Cellar Root, Drip Moss) lived only in the workbook, not in its mirror, and when v2.79.0 rebuilt the tabs by category the tab came out empty. Nothing errored: foraging fell back to plain rations, gardens had no cuttings to plant, and every test still passed -- a whole part of the game gone silent for 26 releases. Restored from v2.75.0's workbook and written into the mirror, and the build now refuses any tab with no rows.

Grazing [potential slop]: a plant- or fungus-eating creature, every graze_seconds or so, goes to something growing within prey_seek -- wild, or a camp garden bed once it is graze_bed grown -- and eats it down; wild plants grow back, a bed starts over. The ledger says so when a crawler sees it, and a raided garden is a warning.

Over 90 minutes on seeds 5 and 11: 418 and 575 plants, 125 grazings, 12 plant forages, 7 beds planted, 27 hearty stews.

v2.104.6

2026-09-25 22:41 EDT -- "Lately" on the character sheet [potential slop]

Each crawler's sheet has a new fold, open by default: the last memories things that happened to them, newest first, green for good and red for bad, with how long ago -- friends made and fallen out with, a partner taken or lost, a game won or lost, a lesson, a laugh, comfort given, half a meal shared, a hearty stew, a want met, a pip earned, a death seen, a night on the bare rock. It changes no number; it only remembers what already did, so a mood has reasons you can read.

v2.104.5

2026-09-25 22:40 EDT -- a bowl while eating [potential slop]

Rule 4: a crawler eating now holds a bowl in their right hand, raised to the mouth. (Also v2.104.4: the campfire's tooltip says how many snares are set.)

v2.104.1

2026-09-25 22:21 EDT -- v2.104.3 -- the food chain, for real this time

v2.93.0's food chain never fired: a meat-eater only ever struck at something already within prey_reach (3 m), and creatures keep apart, so over 90 minutes on two seeds not one creature was taken -- a mechanic nobody could reach. Now a hungry meat- or blood-eater goes looking, up to prey_seek metres, stalks what it finds, and when it gets there the two tussle for prey_brawl seconds -- both in their fighting stance, where it can be seen -- before the throw settles it. The loser dies (and waits for scavengers or a butcher); prey that wins gets loose and runs. Over 90 minutes on seeds 5 and 11: 5 tussles, 4 kills.

v2.104.0

2026-09-25 22:16 EDT -- nests [potential slop]

A creature makes a nest the first place it beds down in a room, and goes back to it to sleep. A nest shows as a ring of heaped stuff in its maker's colours and can be hovered: whose it is (named if the kind is known) and whether its owner is in it. When the owner is away (nest_clear) and the nest is near enough the fire (nest_reach), the camp sends someone to search it (Foraging) for what the creature sheds -- one of each thing its body leaves, meat aside -- and the nest is pulled apart. Ordered, too: "Search the nest". Over 90 minutes on two seeds: 38 nests made, 7 searched.

v2.103.0

2026-09-25 22:04 EDT -- v2.103.1 -- hearty stew [potential slop]

Something growing brought home -- a plant foraged, a garden bed harvested, a rooted plant- or fungus-creature gathered -- counts as greens as well as rations. A cook with greens in store makes a hearty stew instead of a plain hot meal, and stews are eaten first: a hot meal's lift plus a little more (the hearty stew activities row). The campfire's tooltip shows stews and greens. Rare so far -- over 90 minutes on two seeds, two stews -- because the company mostly eats meat.

v2.102.5

2026-09-25 22:03 EDT -- hands that say what is going on [potential slop]

Seated by the fire, what passes between two crawlers shows in their hands: knucklebones are tossed and caught (head down, watching them), a lesson is pointed at, an argument jabs with both hands; ordinary talk is as it was. (Also: signs share their materials and shapes, v2.102.4.)

v2.102.3

2026-09-25 22:01 EDT -- partners bed down together [potential slop]

When two become partners, one swaps bedrolls with whoever sleeps nearest the other, so they sleep side by side -- at a new camp too.

v2.102.2

2026-09-25 22:00 EDT -- ordering a snare

With a crawler selected: point at a square and "Set a snare here", at signs and "Set a snare on these signs", or at a sprung snare and "Set the snare again".

v2.102.0

2026-09-25 21:47 EDT -- v2.102.1 -- snares [potential slop]

Food without a fight (rule 3). The camp keeps up to camp_snares snares set between snare_near and snare_reach metres from the fire -- on fresh signs of something small if anyone has seen some -- each a stake, a bent withy and a noose (Tinkering; a load of wood). A tiny or small creature stepping into a snare's run (snare_span) throws its Agility against the setter's Tinkering; lose, and it is caught and waits to be butchered. Sprung snares are set again. Hover one to see whether it is set, and who set it.

First cut caught nothing in 90 minutes: small creatures came within 12 m of the fire often, but never onto the one square. Set further out, on the runs signs show, and covering the squares beside it, seed 11 caught three.

v2.101.2

2026-09-25 21:33 EDT -- v2.101.3 -- two more ways to die alone, closed

Still on seed 2:

  • A chase came before everything. Arden died 94 m from camp still creeping after drifters to study them, exhausted. Now someone worn down -- comfort, fatigue, sanity or loneliness at 2 or below -- gives up a hunt or a creep and goes to see to it (hunger aside: a hunt is how hunger is answered).
  • Too far out to find a seat. Quill, lonely, went on exploring 90 m out because the way to a seat by the fire could not be worked out from that far. Now they start back towards the fire and look again when there.

Seed 2, three hours: two deaths when this started, none in the last run.

v2.101.0

2026-09-25 21:25 EDT -- v2.101.1 -- signs and tracking [potential slop]

Creatures leave signs where they pass -- scratches and droppings in their own colour, fading over sign_minutes. A crawler who steps on fresh signs in the light, when it matters (the larder is low, or nobody knows what made them), stops and reads them: a Studying throw, and failing teaches. Read, the creature that left them is tracked for track_seconds and can be hunted though nobody has seen it -- the hunt follows its trail. Signs can be hovered: what left them (named only if the kind is known), how old, and whether they have been read. Knobs: sign_chance, sign_minutes, sign_max, sign_rest, sign_need, track_seconds.

v2.100.0

2026-09-25 21:23 EDT -- knucklebones [potential slop]

Sitting together, two crawlers may play a game of knucklebones (game_chance): a Bargaining contest, nerve and reading the other. The winner is cheered, the loser a little sore -- and the loser, having failed, learns (rule 1). Between friends it is only a game; between the cool it can sour. A tan speech mark.

v2.99.3

2026-09-25 21:12 EDT -- v2.99.4 -- nobody left to die of something they could have seen to

Traced on seed 2, one death at a time:

  • An empty larder froze the rest. Hungry with nothing to eat, a crawler used to give up on seeing to ANY need, because hunger came first. Brin, lonely and hungry, wandered alone until she dropped. Now hunger with a bare larder steps aside for the next need they can do something about.
  • A stumble could kill. Each stumble costs comfort, and a candle-less march in the dark with packs on took Corin from comfortable to down in five minutes. A bruise now never takes anyone below 1.
  • A sleeping creature could wall off a hall for good (a creature asleep never moves, and v2.96.0 let them sleep anywhere). Marlo spent twenty minutes 56 m from camp, backing away from a sleeping petrooze across the only way home, and died of cold. Creatures now bed down in rooms, never in halls.

v2.99.2

2026-09-25 21:00 EDT -- nobody works until they drop

On seed 2 two crawlers died of exhaustion with a camp around them: turned back from their beds (someone in the way), they picked up chores -- mending, a rescue, relighting the fire -- and then the company broke camp and marched them into the ground. Now someone worn out who cannot reach a bed lies down where they are, and the company will not set off while anyone is that tired.

Rule 4: the new tools each have their own shape on the figure -- a pot hung at the hip, a notebook, a spool of chalk and cord, a pair of picks, a card of hooks -- instead of the same small box every trinket wore.

v2.99.0

2026-09-25 20:51 EDT -- tools for the work that named them [potential slop]

Nine tasks named a tool that no piece of kit in the game carried -- butchering and gathering (cutting), fishing, gardening, cooking, lockpicking and disarming (picks), mapping, reading and studying (lore), foraging, stoking (hauling) -- so the easier throw a tool gives could never happen. Now:

  • what obviously is one says so: knives and axes cut, the spear fishes, the spade gardens, satchels and pouch belts forage, packs haul;
  • five small tools can be made at camp: Bone Hooks, Lockpicks, a Notebook, Chalk and Cord, a Tin Pot;
  • when work is waiting that nobody has the tool for, and the makings are there, the camp makes one; and a tool is worn in preference to a keepsake;
  • the build now refuses a task whose tool nothing carries.

Singing by the fire always named an instrument as its tool, and nothing in the game was one. v2.97.0

2026-09-25 20:45 EDT -- partners [potential slop]

Two crawlers with nobody else whose bond reaches partner_at (6, the top) are together, and the ledger says so. A crawler never walks out on a living partner, however lonely; a partner's death is grieved twice as hard; and if the bond falls back below a friend's, they part. The sheet shows "partner" in Friends and grudges.

v2.96.0

2026-09-25 20:43 EDT -- creatures sleep [potential slop]

Every creature keeps its own clock: up about 40 minutes, asleep about 20 (creature_awake, creature_asleep). Asleep it lies low with its limbs folded and head down, breathing slowly; it does not move, menace or hunt, and it senses a sneak with half its dice -- so a sleeping creature is the one to creep up on, to study or to ambush. A fight, a new wound, or anyone walking up openly within wake_reach wakes it. Its tooltip says "asleep", and the bestiary has an Asleep button.

Rule 1 holds -- only falling short teaches, so the lesson IS the failure; matching it teaches nothing. A green speech mark. teach_chance, teach_gap in the knobs.

v2.90.0

2026-09-25 17:45 EDT -- walking speed follows Maneuvering

The user: "make maneuvering command movement speed, keep laboring negate some carry weight." Walking speed follows the Maneuvering rating (Agility plus Endurance plus practice) instead of Agility alone, each point adding less than the last: 1.2 m/s at 2, about 1.9 at 7, 2.1 at 12. So a crawler who fails their way to better Maneuvering walks faster (rule 1). Labouring still eases the burden of a load.

v2.89.0

2026-09-25 16:37 EDT - v2.89.1 -- crawlers with each other, a first cut [potential slop, all of it]

The user: "roadmap: crawler interactions. think 'the sims'", then "use your discretion, mark as potential slop".

  • What happens. Two crawlers sitting, resting, eating, warming up or idling by the fire near each other now and then:
  • talk (Bargaining);
  • joke (Entertaining);
  • comfort one who is low (Tending);
  • argue (a Leading contest), when either is hungry, worn out or frayed, when there is bad blood, or now and then anyway (argue_chance). Each is one throw of a skill already in the grid, so failing teaches and succeeding does not. A good talk lifts company, a laugh or comfort lifts sanity, and losing an argument costs sanity.
  • Bonds. What passes between two people builds a bond from -6 to 6. friend_at (3) makes friends, grudge_at (-3) a grudge that makes talk harder and arguments likelier, and both are announced in the ledger.
  • What shows. The character sheet has a "Friends and grudges" fold. Talkers turn to face each other and gesture. "Talk with ..." from the orders panel starts one at once.
  • tests/social.mjs, seeds 1, 7 and 13, two hours each: 58 talks, 29 jokes, 41 arguments, 8 friendships, 2 grudges. Comfort came up earlier runs and not this one: frayed crawlers argue before anyone comforts them. That is balance for later.
  • v2.89.1 fixes a crash found by that test: a crawler standing on a square the world had shifted to rock, or forgotten, had no floor height, and the picture stopped drawing. Such a square now gives its own height.

v2.88.0

2026-09-25 16:22 EDT -- encumbrance, a first cut [potential slop, all of it]

The user asked for pounds with "Encumbrance is coming up" and later "use your discretion". So:

  • Everything worn and carried weighs its pounds: kit, hauled kit, meat, salvage, packed loads and a carried person.
  • A crawler can carry carry_lb_base (20), plus carry_lb_per_might (6) for each point of Might, plus carry_lb_per_carry (5) for each point of carry in their kit.
  • Past that they are burdened, slower as when hauling, and Labouring eases it as before.
  • The sheet shows "Carrying 27 of 46 lb", in red past the limit.
  • Seeds 1, 4, 9 and 13: nobody starts over; the closest is 33 of 37.

v2.87.0

2026-09-25 08:20 EDT -- orders to one crawler, as in The Sims

The user: "when a crawler is selected, you should be able to issue them orders to do pretty much any activity they normally do. Think 'the sims'."

  • Picking. Pick a crawler, from their portrait or in the world, and they take orders until the panel is closed.
  • Their own panel lists what they can do anywhere: eat, sleep, wash, warm up, rest, keep company, keep watch, sing, go back to camp, explore, gather fuel, feed the fire, cook, clear the filth, draw the map, make or mend kit, and stop.
  • Click anything else and its panel lists what they can do with it:
  • a creature: hunt, attack, study, drive off, gather it to eat, butcher the remains;
  • the fire: feed it, cook, warm up, keep company, sing;
  • a bedroll or a stool: sleep or sit there;
  • a grave: sit by it;
  • a vent: warm up;
  • a square of floor: go there, read the room, wash or fish in water;
  • kit on the floor: bring it back;
  • another crawler: talk with them, make them welcome, talk them into staying, see to their hurts, lift them. "Give orders to them instead" hands over the orders.
  • Carrying them out. An order uses the machinery they use of their own accord: the same jobs, the same acts and the same throws. It only says what to try next, so nothing is made to succeed. An ordered act lasts at least order_act_seconds (60), and after an order they stay put for order_hold (30) before minding themselves again. Needs at their worst still come first.
  • A refusal says why ("cannot: the larder is empty").
  • tests/orders.mjs presses Sleep, Feed the fire, Study it and Stop from the panel and checks each is carried out.

v2.84.0

2026-09-25 00:59 EDT - v2.84.1 -- how the crawlers carry themselves

The user: "fighting stances, sneaking stances, crawling, running, animations for all actions."

  • Fighting: feet apart, knees bent, and a blow once a fight round, by grip:
  • bare-handed guard and jab;
  • a one-handed chop, with a shield held forward if they have one;
  • a two-handed overhead swing;
  • alternating thrusts with a pair.
  • Sneaking is a crouched creep, running a long stride with pumping arms (falling back, pressing on or in a fight), and crawling on hands and knees for anyone badly hurt. Carrying holds the load in both arms.
  • Work has a pose for each task:
  • dig, sweep, chop, crouch at the fire, stir, fine handwork, pick;
  • tend the hurt, fish, think, write the map, talk, sing, reach up to a sconce;
  • anything else keeps the hammering.
  • Three real faults found by tests/figures.mjs, which now poses every one of these from eight angles (2,016 cases, 0 in pieces):
  • the pack's chest straps stood a hair off the chest, and anyone bent forward showed the gap;
  • the singing sway slid the body sideways off the hips (now it turns);
  • a hand could part from its forearm.

2026-09-25 00:48 EDT -- 432 creatures grown from their own traits, one piece each

The user: "unique models (smoothed except lithic) and animations for all 432 creatures (don't base them on real animals, don't keep reusing the same ones - get rid of those descriptors, just vibe off the info you have) and make sure there are no floating parts."

  • a plan from its body and how it moves: walker, swimmer, creeper, burrower or rooted;
  • proportions from its attributes;

2026-09-25 00:34 EDT -- shields, pounds, lights that free the hands, two-handed tools
  • Renamed: Great Axe -> Felling Axe, War Pick -> Pickaxe.

  • Hands, as the user meant them:
  • A medium weapon or tool works in one hand or better in two. Before a fight, a light in the off hand is set down on purpose (idea 9) and keeps burning where it lies. A shield is kept.
  • A medium tool in both hands works quicker (grip_speed).
  • A heavy tool counts only with the other hand free, so a light is set down beside the work and picked up after.

v2.80.0

2026-09-25 00:02 EDT -- clothes all keep off wet and cold; trinkets mix and match; hands
  • Every piece worn on the head, neck, torso, hands, legs or feet gets +1 dry and +1 warm, so even rags are worth wearing (the user). Thresholds moved to match: warm_kit 4 -> 7, dry_kit 0.2 -> 0.1 a point (never below a fifth wet).
  • Trinkets are one kind (slot = trinket) and go in either trinket slot (fits() in sheet.js).
  • Hands (the user):
  • A heavy weapon needs both hands: when a fight starts, whatever is in the off hand goes on the ground. A dropped light keeps burning where it lies, and they go back for it afterwards.
  • A medium weapon with the off hand empty, or a light weapon with a second light weapon in the off hand, makes the fight grip_ease (1) easier. Never a free success.
  • A second light weapon is taken from the store into an empty off hand behind a light one.
  • Seeds 9 and 1, with everyone given a maul: 11 drops, 11 pick-ups, no errors.

v2.79.1

2026-09-24 23:49 EDT -- looking the part is a tool, not a free success

The user: "I don't want to ever give free successes". Standing no longer adds a success to any throw. Enough of it (standing_need points) counts as the tool fine, and a task naming that tool is easier, the same way a spade is for digging: Welcome a stranger, Talk them into staying and Talk over moving on need 1 success instead of 2. Talk over moving on is now a task row like the others, so the move_suggest_need knob is gone. The skills tab's standing column is gone too.

v2.79.0

2026-09-24 23:38 EDT -- clothes do something; the sheet's tabs by category, and sortable

The user: "all of the above, but not all buffs to all gear." The gear tab gets four columns, each worn piece has one or two of them, and the character sheet shows the totals:

  • dry: a crawler is soaked less (dry_kit a point) and dries quicker. Wet only costs comfort above wet_felt.
  • warm: replaces the old count of wool and padded pieces; warm_kit points wrap them up.
  • carry: eases a burden alongside Labouring (carry_ease).
  • standing: reaching standing_need points carries a success on the skills the skills tab marks standing (Entertaining, Bargaining, Leading). Rags go below 0 and cost one.

Padding: Work Gloves stop 1 cut and the Feather Cloak 1 crush. The Gambeson already did.

The sheet's tabs are named by category ("Kit - gear", "Creatures - kinds", ...). The build knows each tab by the part after " - ", so they can be renamed or regrouped freely as long as that last part stays. Two tabs with the same last part stop the build. On the sheet page, clicking a column heading sorts by it: once ascending, twice descending, three times back to the sheet's own order.

v2.78.3

2026-09-24 23:32 EDT -- the Cosh is a Blackjack

Renamed at the user's request, in the sheet, the recipe and the figure.

v2.78.2

2026-09-24 23:26 EDT -- gear you can open, a quieter sheet, a picked portrait that shows

A worn item in the character sheet opens when clicked. It shows what the item does as a weapon (weight, damage, grade and the skill it uses), what it stops, its light and burn time, which tasks it makes easier, its condition and its tags. The line under a crawler's name no longer says "crawler", "carrying light" or their illumination. The picked portrait gets a red frame, stands out from the column, and its name turns red.

v2.78.1

2026-09-24 23:19 EDT -- a torch lights the face that carries it

Reported: a crawler's own face stayed dark beside the torch in their hand. Small figures were shadowing their own faces: the shadow map's grain is coarse against a person-sized figure, so each face tested as behind itself. Tested on the portraits with the figure's shadows switched off, with the whole shadow offset raised, and with a push along the surface; the last lit Ash almost exactly as switching shadows off did, so that is what ships: shadow_normal_bias 0.04 m, a new knob.

v2.78.0

2026-09-24 23:10 EDT -- a food chain, sight at a distance, hunting together

No kinds were added: there are still exactly 432. Rooms hold creatures more often (creature_room 0.35 -> 0.6), and which kind lives in a room is now weighted by where it feeds. The weights follow the rough tenfold loss per step up a real food chain: grazers (light, stone, heat, plants, fungus) 10, scavengers 3, small hunters 1, large and huge hunters 0.15 (all trophic_* knobs). A crawler now sees a creature standing in lit space they can see into, up to creature_sight (30 m). Before, it had to be within 12 m. Several crawlers may hunt at once, up to hunters_per_quarry (3) after one creature, and a second hunter joins a fight already under way instead of giving up. Seeds 1, 4 and 9, one hour each: 199-229 creatures, up to 5 hunting at once, joint kills, and no deaths. The larder is still thin (4 meat a medium kill), which is tuning for later.

vSetup

The project starts clean. Brought over from the first game: the rulebook (word for word), the user's concept art and texture pack. Not brought over: any of the first game's picture code, tests, or notes — they carried assumptions about the old look and an habit of long test runs. agents.md renamed AGENTS.md so every tool finds it; rule 7 (quick check only) added at the user's go-ahead. CLAUDE.md only points at the other documents.

vRulebook review

Every line of the rulebook tagged. [gospel]: the ten rules the user wrote, and the user's own words found quoted in the first game's records or said in this project's setup, added beside the lines they belong to. [potential slop]: everything else — possibly the user's, possibly an AI paraphrase, possibly the old code describing itself. Nothing deleted. Rule 10 is slop-tagged because its dice have changed since the user wrote it; "nothing is for sale", "the only thing that lights a room" and the goblin trader were checked and turned out not to be the user's words.

v2.0.0

2026-09-22 23:40 EDT — the first build of the new game

Built from the rulebook, not from the first game's code. The world is stacked one-metre blocks with half-blocks and shaped blocks (slope, gentle slope in two halves, stairs); walking allows a half-metre step, so half-blocks are steps and shaped blocks are the only way up a full metre. Three levels of rooms, a tree of halls per level plus loops, ramps between levels, a chasm room whose upper halls cross it on plank bridges. Room dressing is undone if it takes away floor. The picture is three.js drawn into a small buffer and blown up with hard pixels; textures are the user's pack, mip-mapped down so a metre is 32 pixels wide; normal maps are used, the shine maps are not yet. Everything above the watched floor + 2 m is cut away and the inside of the rock shows black. Only blocks the crawlers have seen are drawn. Illumination steps down one per metre through open space; the drawn lights use the same range and flicker. Crawlers behind a wall show as a flat silhouette. Gear here is placeholder (sheet tab gear), used only for looks, light, and "right tool" tags.

v2.0.1

2026-09-22 23:57 EDT — travellers, and a stroke round the selection

Figures rebuilt after the concept art: hooded and open cloaks (neck slot), travel packs and frame packs with a bedroll, pot and bundle, satchels, tunics with skirts, a quilted gambeson, leg wraps, pouch belts, staffs, a hanging lantern, an old sword; barefoot when the footwear slot is empty. Parts are merged per joint (about a dozen meshes a figure) and each carries a one-pixel dark outline. Figures carry a faint fill of their own colours so the coach can always see them. The selection is now one stroke of pixels round the whole silhouette (the thing is drawn alone into a mask, and the stroke is every pixel outside it that touches it), for crawlers, structures and blocks alike; the pointed-at thing gets a paler stroke. New gear rows are placeholders in the sheet for the user to rename or delete.

v2.0.2

2026-09-23 00:08 EDT — still when paused, feet that stay put, a steady camera

Figures, flames and light flicker now run on the game's clock, so pausing freezes them (the camera still turns on real time). The stride is driven by distance actually covered: hip swing x leg length x phase-per-metre = 1, so a stance foot moves back exactly as fast as the body moves forward and stays planted; knees bend only on the swing, hips drop as the legs spread. Camera: turning and tilting now arrive exactly (they used to creep forever, and the pixel snap flickered with them), nothing is snapped mid-swing, and following a crawler rides their exact position rather than chasing it, with the watched floor only changing a quarter-metre past a level.

v2.0.3

2026-09-23 00:14 EDT — a steady picture, a working close button, portraits

The camera now only stands on a grid where the whole picture moves by whole pixels and whole texels at once (at 2:1 a metre of ground is exactly 16 x 8 game pixels; the grid is 1/8 m, and 1/4 m at the peek-down angle, which moved to 48.59 degrees so a metre of ground there is exactly 12 pixels tall). Stopping between those points is what made wall textures wiggle while the view slid, and what made following a crawler shake; following now tracks the crawler's position on that grid rather than the whole tile they stand on, which would jump a metre at a time. The height eases between floors and then settles exactly. The panel's close button cleared the selected crawler but not the selected thing the panel reads, so it never closed. Roster of live portraits down the left side: each crawler drawn alone (their own layer), lit by whatever light is on them, one portrait refreshed a frame; click one to select and follow them.

v2.0.4

2026-09-23 00:18 EDT — the grid comes out; the textures are the fix

The user: the camera grid was unpleasant and did not cure the wiggle. Removed, with the pixel snap: the camera glides. The wiggle was the textures: 128-texel pictures shrunk to about one texel a pixel and sampled nearest-only jump between texels as the view moves by less than a texel. They now blend between mip levels (and sample at an angle) when shrunk, and stay hard-edged when enlarged. The peek-down angle is back at 55 degrees.

v2.0.5

2026-09-23 00:22 EDT — a slower turn, steady lamps

The quarter turn swings at half the speed (new sheet knob camera_turn_rate, 6; it was 12, shared with the tilt). Carried lights no longer jitter their position to flicker — a light a metre off the floor moving 3 cm swung every shadow round the room; the flicker is now brightness only. The arm holding a light carries it steady in front instead of swinging it.

v2.0.6

2026-09-23 00:28 EDT — lit portraits, a fire on flat ground

Portraits were lit only by the figure's own fill: three.js lights only light what shares their layer, and each portrait draws its crawler on a layer of its own. Every light and the skylight are now on every layer. The campfire went on the room's centre node, which could be a slope or stair where a hall runs through the room; it now goes on the flat open square nearest the middle whose eight neighbours are flat floor at the same height.

v2.0.7

2026-09-23 00:35 EDT — the fire reaches the portraits

Measured before fixing: a crawler 0.7 m from the fire, portrait brightness 24.9 with every light on and 24.9 with every light off — the lights added nothing. A test light 1 m in front of the face with shadows off lit it to 153; with shadows on, 24.9. The portrait drew with the shadow maps left over from the main picture, and in the portrait's pass they put the whole face in shadow. The portrait now re-casts shadows from its own crawler alone (cheap: one figure), and the same measure reads 41.4 on against 25.0 off.

v2.0.8

2026-09-23 00:38 EDT — the page fetches its own freshest copy

GitHub lets a browser keep the page ten minutes, so a reload could show the build before last. publish.py now also writes version.json; the page reads it past every cache when it opens and, at the main address, switches itself to the newest build. While playing it checks once a minute and puts a button on the bar when a newer build is up. A build opened by its own version name is left alone.

v2.0.9

2026-09-23 00:45 EDT — a square each, skin tones, the ledger moves

One crawler to a square: a crawler holds the square they stand on and, mid-step, the one they left too. Somebody in the way is waited for a moment and then walked round; a job's work spot is never one somebody already stands on; they arrive on six different squares. Skin and hair come from new sheet tabs (skin, 8 tones from pale to deep brown; hair, 8), and every bare slot -- head, hands, arms, chest, legs, feet -- wears the crawler's own tone. The ledger sits at the bottom right; the pinned panel is kept short enough to stay above it.

v2.0.10

2026-09-23 00:49 EDT — starting attributes total 18-24

The user: "starting character attributes should total 18-24". Each attribute is still thrown 1-6; the six are thrown again until their total lands in the range (sheet knobs attr_total_min / attr_total_max). Added to the rulebook, 2.11, as gospel.

v2.0.11

2026-09-23 00:54 EDT — portraits finished like the screen

Portraits are drawn into an off-screen target, and three.js gives an off-screen target neither the tone curve nor the screen's colour encoding: every portrait came out as raw linear light, far darker than the same crawler on screen, with the skylight lost in the bottom of the range. The portrait is now drawn at full precision and finished in the page with the same ACES curve, exposure and sRGB encoding the screen uses.

v2.0.12

2026-09-23 00:56 EDT — a carried light comes from the flame

A carried light was placed at the carrier's waist, inside their own body, so in the portrait (which casts shadows from its own crawler alone) their torso, cloak and hood shadowed their face. The light now sits at the flame in their hand.

v2.0.13

2026-09-23 00:59 EDT — round the fire, off the bedrolls

Walking now weighs each square: a lit campfire costs 50 extra steps and a laid bedroll 4 (sheet knobs path_cost_fire, path_cost_bedroll), so a walk goes round them whenever it can and crosses only when there is no other way. Idle pottering never steps onto either. The skylight's colour (a cold blue-grey, #8a9ab8) moved from the code into the sheet as skylight_colour.

v2.0.14

2026-09-23 01:04 EDT — ambient occlusion

Every corner of every block face is shaded by the three blocks round it just in front of the face (the two beside the corner and the one across it; both sides filled is fully dark), so floors darken where they meet walls and inside corners go deep. Full strength on the skylight, half on lamp and fire light; each quad is cut along the diagonal that keeps the shading even. Sheet knobs ao_strength (0.7; 0 turns it off) and ao_direct (0.5).

v2.0.15

2026-09-23 01:08 EDT — debug menu

"Debug" on the bar (or F3): frame rate and frame time; for each light (candle, lantern, torch, campfire) brightness, falloff, shadow darkness, flicker and colour; shadows' type (hard / soft / softer / blurred), detail and bias; ambient occlusion and how much of it shows under lamps; skylight brightness and colour; exposure. Changes are live and not saved; "Copy changed values" puts them on the clipboard to be written into the sheet. The ambient occlusion's lamp share became a live value so its slider works without a rebuild.

v2.0.16

2026-09-23 01:15 EDT — a shadow blur that works; ambient occlusion at full

The shadow-type menu did nothing visible: every light here is a point light, and three.js point-light shadows only honour plain filtered shadows — the "softer" and "blurred" kinds quietly fall back. The menu is replaced by a blur slider that widens the filter (shadow.radius), and shadows are now filtered by default (sheet knob shadow_blur, 1). Ambient occlusion is stored at full strength (a fully enclosed corner goes black) and two live sliders take it back: one on the skylight (ao_sky), one on lamp and fire light (ao_lamps); both start at 1 as asked.

v2.0.17

2026-09-23 01:23 EDT — the user's shadow settings; lamps and the fire move a little

Into the sheet, as set by the user in the debug menu: ao_sky 1, ao_lamps 1, shadow_blur 8, shadow_map_size 192 (new), shadow_bias 0.01 (new). A carried lamp now sways a little in the hand, more with the stride, and the light goes with it. The campfire's light drifts slowly (new sway column in the lights tab: campfire 0.06 m, the others 0).

v2.1.0

2026-09-23 01:29 EDT — the six needs

Hunger, Comfort, Fatigue, Sanity, Cleanliness and Camaraderie, 6 down to 0, each resisted by one attribute (build refuses otherwise). What moves them is what the crawler is doing, paid as it happens: a row per activity in the new activities tab (walking, working, idle, sleeping, eating, warming, resting, washing, socialising) plus rows for where they are (in the dark, alone, near the fire, in company); drain is scaled by the resisting attribute. At rung 3 a crawler drops their job and sees to the lowest need (bedroll to sleep, a seat by the fire for the rest; eating takes a ration from the larder); with nothing to do they top up anything under 5. Need costs speed and time only: the worst need below 3 slows walking and work. At 0 they throw the resisting attribute's dice against one success (the rulebook's one exception to law 2, teaching nothing): a success holds for 30 s, a failure puts them down where they stand. Somebody lifts them (Grappling) and carries them home; unreached for 3 minutes they die. Laid up, they are tended (Tending) and get up with a scar: a mark on the figure and one point off the resisting attribute. The larder starts with 24 rations; under 8, a foraging job goes up for whoever carries a light. On the figure: grime with dirt, slumped when exhausted/hungry/lonely, shivering when cold, twitchy when frayed; they sit by the fire, eat, lie down to sleep; the dead go grey. Portraits carry six need bars; the panel shows each need. New sheet: needs, activities, tasks forage/rescue/tend, 13 knobs. Placeholder, flagged: washing happens at the camp without water (the rulebook says water answers cleanliness; there is no water in the world yet). The watch is not built.

v2.1.1

2026-09-23 01:30 EDT — the needs, measured

tests/needs.mjs runs a real match, empties the larder and starves one crawler: collapse, lifted, carried home, tended, scar, and foraging when the larder ran low all fired (seed 7); with nobody able to help, the fallen crawler died at three minutes. In an ordinary 25-minute match nobody went below 5 — they keep themselves up, as the rulebook says survivors do. Fixed: a hungry crawler with an empty larder repeated "drops everything" every second; now they say once that the larder is empty and leave it to the foragers.

v2.2.0

2026-09-23 01:31 EDT — a window through the walls

A round see-through window at the middle of the view (where a followed crawler is): any wall or rock nearer the camera than that spot, and more than 30 cm above the watched floor, is not drawn inside it; the rim is dithered away pixel by pixel rather than faded, so nothing is ever half-transparent. Floors, crawlers and everything at or behind the middle are untouched. Pointing obeys the same window, so what you can see through it is what you can hover. "See-through" on the bar (or V) turns it off. Sheet knob see_radius (4 m).

v2.3.0

2026-09-23 01:34 EDT — the labyrinth goes on

The world is a plain of 64 m pieces, 8 across (512 m; the camp starts in the middle), made as the crawlers come within 20 m of an edge. Each piece is worked out from the match seed and its address alone, in a world of its own, and then copied into place, so it comes out the same whatever order pieces are made in. Neighbouring pieces meet through doorways: both work out the same doorways (where along the seam, which level) from the seam's own numbers, each digs its half out to the rim and in to its nearest room on that level. Each piece has a character from the new themes tab: ruined halls (the starting piece), natural caves (rounded chambers, earth and gravel, no masonry), an old mine (timber and cobble, gravel floors, pillars) and a crypt (blackstone, pillars, low walls). Light is cleared only where it was, not across the whole plain; foraging looks within 30 m of camp. Knobs: piece_size, pieces_across, piece_margin (world_width/world_depth retired).

v2.3.1

2026-09-23 01:35 EDT — measured; one piece a frame

tests/endless.mjs, seed 7, 30 game minutes: 18 pieces made across all four themes, crawlers stood in 11 of them and got 135 m from the first — the doorways really join — and all six alive. Making a piece is the one expensive moment (the slowest simulated second was 331 ms), so at most one piece is made per frame.

v2.3.2

2026-09-23 07:26 EDT — walls are shells

The user undid the see-through window and asked instead: no tops on walls, no sides of blocks that cannot be seen (only sides facing open air), no insides. Only sides facing open air were already the only faces built, and only their fronts drawn; what added tops and insides was the black "lid" drawn from the inside of every face wherever the cut went through rock. That is gone, so a cut wall is an open shell: no top, and you look over it into whatever lies behind. The see-through window, its button and its knob are removed.

v2.4.0

2026-09-23 07:38 EDT — stories

The user: "I want the labyrinth separated into Stories. within a story, tile elevation can go up and down, but not overlap on top of itself." The world is now five stories (-2 to 2) of 8 m each, Story 0 in the middle, each a plain of 64 m pieces (6 across). Within a story every column has one floor: rooms stand at heights 1-4 m, and every hall between two floors of different height gets a ramp on its longest straight stretch (slope, stairs, or a gentle slope at two squares a metre), so the ground rises and falls but never lies over itself. Rock stands a wall's height (2 m) above the floor nearest it and is marked as wall: its top is never drawn and the air over it is void -- not walkable, and light and sight do not pass over it, so a fire still lights only its own room. Stories are joined by stairwells: both stories work out where from the same numbers, each lays a small landing there, and the two landings are joined as one step. Only the story being looked at is drawn -- no slicing -- and riding a crawler shows theirs. The camera's Up/Down now change story, with "Story N" between them; [ and ] do the same. The frame rate sits at the right of the bar. Pieces of the stories above and below are made where a crawler stands. The old levels, chasm room and cut are gone (and their sheet rows); new knobs story_height, stories_below, stories_above, floor_min, floor_max, wall_height, stairwells, rooms_per_story. DECISIONS and the rulebook carry the user's words in place of "real stacked blocks".

v2.4.1

2026-09-23 07:39 EDT — explorers get out; walls never lower than their floor

Measured on v2.4.0 (tests/endless.mjs, seed 7, 30 min): nobody left the first piece. Every room was reachable and the stairwells were joined; the explorers were stuck on a square beside the void over a wall, which is never "seen", so it counted as unexplored for ever. Exploring now ignores that void. Also, the rock between a high floor and a low one could come out lower than the high floor (the nearer floor claimed it); the higher floor now claims it. After: 27 pieces made, 19 stairwells joined, crawlers stood on all five stories and in two pieces, all alive.

v2.5.0

2026-09-23 10:45 EDT — the labyrinth goes on for ever, and remembers

No edges and no bottom or top: cells are addressed by where they are (a million metres each way, thousands of stories), and a piece of any story is worked out from the seed and its address alone whenever it is needed. Only pieces within keep_radius (1) of a crawler, the camp or the view -- and the stories either side -- are held; a piece nobody has been near for forget_after (20 s) is forgotten, keeping only what changed there in play (every write goes through one door that records it) and a bit-packed record of what has been seen. When somebody returns it is worked out again from the seed and the record is laid back on top. Every read of the world now goes through that door too, so the renderer, light, sight, walking and the needs all work on the same held pieces. The camp's first piece now sits at 0,0. Knobs pieces_across, stories_below and stories_above are gone; keep_radius and forget_after are new. Also: the frame counter could show a negative number for one frame (the clock read before the frame's own timestamp); clamped.

v2.5.1

2026-09-23 10:46 EDT — remembering, measured

tests/remember.mjs (seed 7, 30 game minutes): a cell changed in play and a cell seen both survived their piece being forgotten and worked out again from the seed; the piece came back identical to the cell apart from that one change (1 of 32,768); 28 pieces were made across the match, never more than 22 held at once, 15 held at the end. The test's hook (__game.tick) is the only change to the page.

v2.6.0

2026-09-23 10:51 EDT — they look before they camp; teams; need arrows; rooms

The user asked whether the crawlers knew where they were going: they did -- the camp was chosen from the whole map. Now nobody knows the labyrinth on arrival: the company looks round together, and after seeing scout_rooms (3) rooms, or scout_seconds (90), camps in the largest room it has actually seen. Teams: every crawler carrying a light leads, and everyone else follows the nearest of them, keeping within team_close (2.5 m); a leader walks explore_leg (10) squares at a time and waits for any of theirs further than team_spread (6 m). Need arrows as in The Sims: one to three either side of each bar in the panel, left and red when falling, right and green when rising (arrow_1/2/3 set the speeds). Rooms: a room is found when its middle is seen, and read by a Studying job (three units, in the light -- in the dark it cannot be done), which reveals its name. Names are composed from the new words tab: what it was built to be (from its stretch of the labyrinth), what has happened to it (from what its ground shows: rubble, a pit, pillars...), and sometimes whose it was; each word carries its tags. Hovering a square names the room it is in; "Rooms" on the bar (or R) lists every room found, read or not, with its story, size, floor height, stretch, features, whether it is lit, and whether it is the camp; click one to look at it.

v2.7.0

2026-09-23 10:55 EDT — the fire needs feeding; the watch [potential slop]

Built unsupervised; every choice below is [potential slop] for the user to review. The campfire burns a minute of fuel a minute (8 minutes when first lit, at most 12). Below 4 minutes a "Keep the fire fed" job goes up (Labouring, one easy unit; one load of fuel adds 3 minutes); below 4 loads a "Gather fuel" job goes up for someone carrying a light (Foraging; earth, moss or timber floors 6-30 m from camp; 3 loads). If the fire runs out it goes out: its light and Illumination go, so the camp is blind (rulebook 4: sight is light), and a "Relight the fire" job (Tinkering, easier with fire in hand) needs a load of fuel. The watch (rulebook 7): while anyone sleeps, the least tired of the rest keeps it, sitting by the fire -- one watcher, never two -- and the feeding job goes to the watcher first. New "watching" activity row (tires a little). The fire's tooltip shows fuel, loads and rations.

v2.7.1

2026-09-23 10:56 EDT — the watch stays put [potential slop]

Measured (tests/events.mjs, seed 7, 30 min): fed 9 times, fuel gathered 3 times, never went out, nobody died -- but the watch changed hands 47 times, because "least tired" changes every few seconds. The watcher now keeps it until nobody is sleeping, they cannot keep it, or their own fatigue falls to 3. Forcing the fire out mid-match (15 min): it went out once and was relit once; the watch changed hands 5 times.

v2.8.0

2026-09-23 10:57 EDT — the order of work [potential slop]

The user, early on: "we will need to build a job priority system like Dwarf Fortress." "Orders" on the bar (or O): the camp's one shared list of work, top row first; move a row up or down, or untick it and nobody does it. A crawler looking for work goes down the list and takes the nearest job of the first kind they can do; skills still decide who can do what. The list starts as the sheet's tasks tab in its row order (reordered so rescue, tending and the fire come first) with reading rooms and exploring among the rows. Built unsupervised: the list, its starting order and "reading" and "exploring" being rows on it are [potential slop].

v2.9.0

2026-09-23 10:58 EDT — kit wears, is mended, ends as salvage [potential slop]

Rulebook 2.9, the user's words: gear wears down a ladder, new to broken, each rung with less durability than the last; mending knocks it down a rung; damaged kit is mended at camp in downtime or salvaged for its materials; Tinkering mends. Built unsupervised: the new wear tab has six rungs (new, worn, chipped, cracked, failing, broken) with 40 down to 16 minutes of use and a shade each. A tool wears while working, footwear while walking, other clothing slowly all the time (knobs wear_tool/feet/worn). When a rung is used up the owner gets a "Mend worn kit" job at camp (Tinkering; the Tool Belt now counts as the mending tool); worn-through kit no longer counts as a tool. At the last rung it is "Salvage broken kit" instead: it comes off, and its materials (leather, cloth, iron, wood...) go into the camp's salvage, shown on the fire's tooltip. Each rung darkens it on the figure. Not built: kit handed between crawlers, pinning.

v2.9.1

2026-09-23 11:00 EDT — wear you can see in a sitting [potential slop]

Measured on v2.9.0 (seed 7, 40 min): nothing needed mending -- boots lost about 6 minutes of 40 in 10 minutes of play, so the mechanic never showed. Durability quartered (10, 8.5, 7, 5.5, 4, 0 minutes). After: 7 items wanted mending and all 7 were mended in 40 minutes; nothing reached salvage yet (it takes five mends).

v2.10.0

2026-09-23 11:01 EDT — water, and rust [potential slop]

Water (rulebook: cleanliness is answered by water; wet ground takes more to cross): some rooms in caves and halls now hold a shallow pool -- the middle of the room a metre down, its floor water (the texture is the pack's cyan concrete powder, drawn dark, cold and glossy), with a slope in and out. Wading is slower (wade_speed) and a walk keeps out of water where it can (path_cost_water). Washing is done in the nearest pool anyone has seen; with none known, at camp as before. New words Flooded and Drowned name pool rooms. Rust (rulebook 2.8, the user's words: skills very slowly lose their earned pips; attributes never rust): a skill unused for rust_after (10) minutes loses a Lesson every rust_every (2) minutes, and when the Lessons run out an earned pip goes; using the skill stops it.

v2.10.1

2026-09-23 11:05 EDT — pools that are really there [potential slop]

Measured on v2.10.0 (seed 7, 40 min): 3 rooms were called pool rooms and none had water in it -- a room too small for the pool kept the name without the pool (the same was true of pits). A room is now named for a pool or pit only when one is really dug, smaller rooms can hold a pool, and pools are more common (caves and halls). After, 30 min each: seed 7 -- 9 pools, 4 seen, all 105 s of washing done in water; seed 3 -- 6 pools, 103 of 119 s in water; seed 11 -- 9 pools, 123 of 140 s in water (the rest at camp before any pool was known).

v2.11.0

2026-09-23 11:06 EDT — kit found, brought home, shared; pinning [potential slop]

Rulebook 2.9, the user's words: "Kit is shared and handed off between crawlers on its own unless it's pinned to one." Built unsupervised: a room sometimes has a piece of kit lying in it (loot_chance 0.35, from the gear tab, a little worn), drawn as a small bundle in its colour that can be pointed at. Once seen, a "Bring kit back to camp" job goes up (Labouring); whoever takes it carries it to the fire and it goes into the camp store (listed on the fire's tooltip). A crawler at camp with an empty slot, or worse kit in it, takes the best piece from the store that fits and leaves their old one. In the crawler's panel each piece of gear has a "pin" button: pinned kit stays with them. Kit on the floor is kept when its piece of the world is forgotten.

v2.12.0

2026-09-23 11:08 EDT — hot meals and songs [potential slop]

From the needs table the user approved (a shared hot meal is the best food; a song answers camaraderie). Built unsupervised: with the fire lit, rations in and fewer than 3 hot meals ready, a "Cook a hot meal" job goes up (Tinkering; one ration makes one meal). Eating takes a hot meal before a ration; a hot meal adds a "hot meal" row on top of eating (more hunger, some comfort and company). When at least two others are sitting by the fire, somebody with nothing to do sings: "Sing by the fire" (Entertaining, three units); every success lifts each listener's camaraderie (and a little sanity), more with surplus successes; every failure teaches the singer. One singer at a time; both are rows on the order of work. The fire's tooltip counts the hot meals.

A crawler whose camaraderie and sanity have both sat at rung 1 or below for leave_seconds (240) shoulders their pack and walks away with their kit, and is gone; the last one alive never leaves. A failed throw at a trap sets it off 30% of the time (trap_spring): it takes 2 rungs of comfort and 1 of sanity from whoever was working it, and the trap is spent. Every failure still teaches. v2.18.0

2026-09-23 12:09 EDT — things that grow, and gardens

Farming is Tending: a cutting is planted in one of up to garden_beds (4) beds in a ring by the fire ("Plant a bed"), the bed grows on its own to its grow time, and "Harvest a bed" brings bed_yield (2) times its food and starts it again. Beds are turned earth in a board frame, and what is in them grows up visibly. Also: pointing at kit on the floor no longer upsets the selection outline.

v2.21.0

2026-09-23 12:21 EDT — rooms do something to the people in them [potential slop]

A room's name is built from words, and the words carry tags; now some tags wear on (or ease) whoever is in the room, as rows in the activities tab ("room: haunted" and so on): haunted and burial rooms fray sanity (haunted badly), a silent one a little, and it is lonely; burnt rooms leave you dirty; moss is a little comfortable, standing water a little cold; a chapel or a library eases the mind; a refectory is companionable; a bathhouse keeps you clean. It works whether or not the room has been read. Once it has been read, the Rooms list shows what it does (e.g. "Sanity ↓"), and the company choosing a camp weighs it: a haunted room makes a poor camp.

v2.22.0

2026-09-23 12:24 EDT — four idle skills put to work [potential slop]

Persisting, Maneuvering, Focusing and Leading were in the skills table and nothing ever threw them. Now each comes up on its own, never as a job: Persisting -- after persist_every (120) seconds in the dark or with a need down at the seeking rung, a throw; a success halves every drain for the next two minutes. Maneuvering -- every maneuver_every (30) steps, a throw; a success walks the next 30 steps 1.2x quicker. Focusing -- climbing or dropping a metre while carrying someone, hauling, or in the dark, a throw; a failure is a stumble: down in a crouch for 2 seconds and a little comfort lost. Leading -- a light-holder with a team, every 2 minutes, a throw; a success lifts each follower's camaraderie and sanity. Every failure teaches, as always. All the throws are in the ledger.

v2.35.0

2026-09-23 13:57 EDT — shadows sharp at contact, soft further off

The user: sharp where a thing touches what it shadows (feet on the floor), as soft as now where the caster is well off the surface (a torch held above the ground). Contact-hardening shadows: before blurring, 27 samples round the point find how far out the blockers are, and the blur radius is the full shadow_blur times (gap between receiver and blocker) / (blocker's distance from the light) times shadow_spread (0.5), never less than shadow_contact (0.08) of the full blur, never more than all of it. The frame of a lantern or the hand round a torch, right beside the flame, gets the full blur; a foot on the floor almost none. Then the same 64-sample blur across that radius. Cost: 27 more samples a shadowed pixel on top of 64, in the small buffer; no measurable change in the frame time here (12 fps software-rendered either way). [potential slop]: shadow_spread and shadow_contact, both in the sheet.

v2.36.0

2026-09-23 15:51 EDT — creatures, first version

Built from the user's three tags and twelve traits (rulebook 9). A kind of creature is worked out -- the same every time for the same seed -- from a lineage (12), an affinity (6) and an archetype (6), 432 kinds, each rolling its twelve traits from the sheet: disposition (hostile, benign, uncommonly beneficial), size (tiny to huge, only the sizes its lineage allows), sociality (solitary, pair, pack; swarm is only for tiny creatures, many bodies acting as one), home biome, light (drawn to it, shy of it, indifferent), sense, how it moves, what it eats, what it leaves, temper, tameable or wild, lair or wanderer. Its six attributes run 1-4 with its archetype's attribute 4-6 and size moving Might and Endurance. Its name is an affinity word and a lineage word ("Gloom Crawler", "Ember Newt"). New sheet columns: lineage body/words/moves/diets/leaves/sizes, affinity words/colour/places; new tab creature_traits with a weight for every value. Where: 35% of rooms have creatures, their affinity following the room 70% of the time (crypt Erebos, cave and mine Gaea, a flooded room Hydor, a room with a hot vent Pyr, ruined halls Aer or Aether); fish only where there is water. They shift away with the piece they live in. What they do (until fighting and hunting are designed): a lair-keeper keeps near home, a roamer wanders; within 8 m of a crawler the skittish and the light-shy run, the curious and the light-drawn come to look, and the hostile close in and stand over somebody -- who is frightened by it ("menaced": sanity -0.5, comfort -0.2 a minute). Crawlers walk round anything bigger than tiny. The first time somebody sees a creature it goes in the ledger, and the first of each kind says so. What they look like: a body plan per lineage (four-legged mammals, low-slung reptiles with tails, birds with wings, squat amphibians, finned fish, six-legged arthropods, shelled molluscs, segmented worms, see-through oozes, capped fungi, leafy plants, clusters of crystal), in their affinity's colours, scaled by size; legs stride, tails and wings sway, a swarm seethes. Seen only where there is light on them. Point at one for its tags; the panel shows its attributes and its traits. All of the looks and behaviour: [potential slop].

v2.36.1

2026-09-23 16:08 EDT — a lineage's movement, diet and size are tendencies

The user, "critical": how a creature moves, what it eats and its size are TENDENCIES of its lineage, and now and then there should be an aberration -- "a lithic dust swarm that feeds on blood". Each lineage's lists are now narrow (a Lithic tends to walk, eat stone and be large; an Aves tends to fly, eat meat and be small), and the whole range of each lives in creature_traits (movement: walk, climb, fly, swim, burrow, creep, seep, root; diet: meat, plants, carrion, fungus, stone, light, blood, heat; the five sizes). For each of the three, a creature_aberration (0.04) chance draws from the whole range instead. An aberrant kind carries the tag "aberrant", and its panel says in what. At 0.1 it was 96 of the 432 kinds -- more than "now and then" -- so 0.04: about one kind in nine. A swimmer is only placed where there is water, whatever its lineage.

v2.37.0

A creature's body now follows the word in its name, not only its lineage: a Bat is small with two flapping wings, a Bear bulkier with a stub tail, a Rat long with a long tail, a Boar has tusks; an Adder or a Wyrm is a legless snake of nine segments; a Spider has eight legs and a round abdomen, a Centipede many segments with a pair of legs each, a Crab a wide flat shell and claws; an Eel is long and thin; a Heron stands on long legs with a long neck and bill, an Owl is rounder. And how it moves shows: a flier holds itself up off the floor and bobs (wings flap, faster when moving), a burrower sits half sunk into the floor. [potential slop]: all of the shapes.

v2.38.0

2026-09-23 17:32 EDT — 432 kinds, each named from its traits

The build refuses a kinds tab that misses a combination, doubles one, repeats a name, uses a trait value creature_traits does not know, makes a swarm that is not tiny, or has an attribute outside 1-6 (each refusal tried and seen). How the traits were assigned [potential slop]: the game's own generator, run once (tools/roll_kinds.mjs), now also reads a leanings tab -- an affinity or archetype pulling its creatures' traits the way the user described it (Herald: swarms and packs; Warden: beneficial, pairs and packs, tameable; Bulwark: a lair, large or huge; Brute: hostile, territorial; Skirmisher: roaming, ambushing; Arcanist: solitary, ambushing; Pyr: eats and senses heat; Aethēr: drawn to light, sight, eats light; Erebos: shy of light, carrion and blood, smell; Gaea: burrows, eats stone, tremor; Hydōr: swims; Aēr: flies, hearing). Such a leaning joins the lineage's tendencies, so an Aēr fish can fly. Result: 28 aberrant kinds, 10 swarms, 182 hostile / 208 benign / 42 beneficial. v2.39.0

2026-09-23 18:05 EDT — the bestiary renamed

The user: not everything should be a compound word -- split any that gain nothing by it ("Gale Bat"); try not to reuse words; prefer alliteration and word smashes; and some can be words of their own, real or invented ("Skitterer", "florp"). 242 of the 432 kinds renamed: compounds that gained nothing split (Gale Bat, Cave Cod, Star Squid, Gravel Grub), overworked prefixes thinned out (Gale-, Rime-, Glim-, Halo-, Hearth-, Necro-, Frost-, Storm-), alliteration where it fits (Slush Slug, Frost Flan, Surf Serpent, Hallowed Hart, Marsh Muskie, Crag Crake), smashes (Beavalanche, Newtron, Squidcicle, Plasmort, Searsucker, Glacigator), and words of their own (Rotter, Skulker, Tempest, Boggart, Bellower, Ermine, Kelpie, Cenotaph, Dolmen, Fizzle, Nubbin, Sipper, Blinder). No two names share a word now except six animal nouns in alliterations (slug, frog, cricket, snail, toad, skink). Traits unchanged.

v2.40.0

2026-09-23 19:41 EDT — the bestiary, rated

The user rated every name on a page built for it (thumbs down, acceptable, thumbs up, or go back to the name before). 113 up, 134 acceptable, 146 down, 41 sent back to their earlier name. Those 41 reverts are taken as asked (Rimecroc, Gustpike, Graveslug, Emberlet...). The 145 downvoted names that were not reverted are renamed. Reading the ratings, what they liked: short words of their own, real or invented (Gustle, Dewdle, Nubbin, Mizzle, Squelch, Sipper, Hob, Clinker), smashes that read as one real-sounding word (Thornado, Hushroom, Enchanterelle, Quickslime, Obsidiant), real monument words (Dolmen, Cenotaph), and a few two-word names with some bite (Fog Fang, Pit Piper, Cavern Cur). What they downvoted: learned prefixes (Pyro-, Necro-, Geo-, Aero-, Astro-), the -lith and -oze endings, and "adjective + animal" alliteration (Frost Flan, Kiln Crab, Slush Slug) [potential slop: this is my reading of the votes, not their words]. So the new names lean on real English words that fit the creature (Slowburn, Deadwood, Chilblain, Inglenook, Windsock, Gravedigger, Menhir, Brock) and short invented ones (Mossle, Murkle, Hottle, Glowb, Lotl). The acceptable names are left as they are.
