v2.41.0

2026-09-23 20:05 EDT — a fact for every creature

The user: "for each one make up a unique ecological fact as flavor text. Get lots of variety as we don't want them to be ignored as random fluff slop (This is all info crawlers can study)" [gospel]. The ratings of round 5 are in too: every name passed. All 432 kinds now carry a fact in a new lore column of the kinds tab, and the build refuses a kind with none. The facts are meant to be things a crawler could use or look out for: signs a creature is near (square droppings on a stone mean a Wombrock border; a Hushroom deadens sound for four metres), things it leaves that are useful (Candlefish burn as candles, Obsidiant flakes are blades, Beamble honey glows), warnings (Frostbite venom kills in an hour by a fire and in a day in the cold), and links between creatures (Tidewrasse clean Rimecroc teeth; Mossle grows only where a Coilossus has slept) [potential slop: every fact is mine]. For now the fact shows in the panel when a creature is selected; it is not yet locked behind studying.

v2.42.0

2026-09-23 20:07 EDT — a creature's fact is learned by studying it

The user chose: the fact stays hidden until a crawler studies that creature [gospel: "B"]. A new job, "Study a creature" (tasks tab, Studying, 3 units), done the way reading a room is: a crawler standing in the light, with a creature of a kind nobody has studied yet in the light within study_reach (10 m, new knobs row) [potential slop], stands and watches it, one Studying roll a unit. Three successes and the kind is studied: the fact goes in the log and shows in that kind's panel from then on; before that the panel says "Nobody has studied one yet." It stops if the creature walks off, goes dark, or the crawler is called to something else. Failures teach, as every Studying roll does. It can be switched off in the orders like any job. Measured by tests/study.mjs over 3 seeds, 2 minutes of game time each (it said an hour; the test stepped 1/30 s at a time -- corrected in v2.44.2): seed 1 saw no creature; seeds 3 and 7 each saw one kind and studied it (a Glowbell, a Cindorel).

v2.43.0

2026-09-23 20:22 EDT — studying a creature, stage by stage, with the lore last

The user: "I want the facts to be the last thing a crawler can learn about a creature by extensive study. A lore reward after all the mechanical stuff" [gospel]. A new study tab lists the stages in order, what each reveals and how many successful Studying rolls it takes: Habits (how it moves, what it eats and leaves, where it sleeps; 3), Temper (temper, tameness, what is aberrant; 4), Senses (light, sense; 4), Measure (the six attributes; 5), Lore (the fact; 6) -- 22 successes in all, over five separate sittings, each one needing the creature in sight and in light again [potential slop: the grouping and the numbers are mine]. The panel now shows only what has been studied, with "Studied 2 of 5" and what is next; the tags on the tooltip (lineage, affinity, archetype, size, disposition, sociality) stay visible, since those are what is seen (rule 8). The build refuses a study tab that reveals something a kind does not have, reveals any part twice or never, or does not end on the lore alone (checked: moving the lore to stage 1 stops the build). Measured by tests/study.mjs over seeds 1, 3 and 7, 4 minutes of game time each (it said two hours; see v2.44.2): seed 1 saw one kind and studied none of it; seed 3 saw four and studied the Glowbell and the Emberbruin to the end (76 rolls); seed 7 saw two and studied the Cindorel to the end (36 rolls). Different crawlers take different stages of the same kind.

v2.44.0

2026-09-23 20:37 EDT - v2.44.2 — meeting creatures: fight, drive off, back off, hunt, and study first

The user took all four suggestions -- quick deadly fights, crawlers also hunt for food, taming waits, crawlers can die -- and added: "I also feel like I want to see instances of ALL of them at times. Like perhaps they would scare off a superior but timid enemy if it is blocking the path to good loot" and "They would probably try to safely sneak around and study before deciding how to act" [gospel]. Everything below is src/fight.js, and every rule and number in it is [potential slop].

  • What a crawler does depends on what the company knows (the study stages). The creature's measure gives its true fighting dice; unknown, they guess from its size, one size up. They fight if the crawlers near them are at least as strong (Brawling dice x wounds left, against its dice x wounds left); drive it off (Leading against its Presence + Willpower) if it is stronger but its measure shows less nerve than their Leading, and it is on them or stands between them and the camp or kit on the floor; otherwise back off and study it. Cornered, they fight.
  • Studying is done unseen where it can be. A crawler with a light creeps up on a creature whose study is unfinished (within creep_reach, 20 m) until it stands in their own light; inside its notice every sitting is a Sneaking throw against its Intellect + Willpower, and a creature does not react to someone sneaking unless it has caught them. Caught, they back off.
  • A fight: every fight_round (2 s) the creature throws its Brawling pair once and goes for one of them; each crawler throws Brawling against its successes through attempt() (so failing teaches). A win is a wound on it (two on a big win); a loss, for the one it went for, is a wound on them. crawler_wounds (3) puts a crawler down -- the old rescue, tending, scar (Endurance) and death all apply. A creature takes 1 + its size step (+1 for Endurance 4+) wounds. A side with break_off (1) wound left breaks off unless it has no choice (struck, cornered, defending the camp; territorial creatures and hunted prey fight on). A hostile creature standing over somebody for menace_strike (4 s) strikes. Wounds close in wound_heal_seconds at camp, a quarter as fast away.
  • Hunting: when the larder is low, a crawler with a light goes after something seen near the fire that is not stone and that the company reckons it can take; Sneaking to get close unseen (an unseen approach carries ambush_carry successes into the first throw), then the fight, then the meat carried home (a ration per wound it could take).
  • Visible: wounds pale and slump a crawler, a hurt creature lists; the panel shows wounds; Fight, Drive off and Hunt are orders the player can switch off.
  • Measured (tests/meet.mjs, 30 game minutes each on seeds 1, 3, 7 and 11, v2.44.2): 35 fights, 21 kills, 6 hunts with meat home, 56 back-offs, 4 drive-offs and 1 failed one (all seed 3), 13 crawlers wounded, nobody down, nobody dead; choices made 99 fight, 34 back, 5 drive off. v2.44.0 and v2.44.1 drove nothing off at all: a skittish creature runs away of its own accord so never needed driving, and crawlers who backed away ended up outside their own light and could never study what they had backed away from. v2.44.2 judges timidity by nerve against Leading, lets a timid creature on top of them be faced down, and adds the creeping.
  • A wrong number, owned: the study figures in v2.42.0 and v2.43.0 said an hour and two hours of game time. The tests stepped the game a thirtieth of a second at a time; they were two and four minutes. Corrected above; the tests now step a whole second.

v2.45.0

2026-09-23 21:02 EDT — a weapon, and a light, count in a meeting

The fight and the drive-off now use the same rule every job uses for the right kit [potential slop]: the task's tool_tag, worn and not worn through, takes need_hand - need_tool off the successes needed. fight is tagged weapon (only the Old Sword carries it in the gear tab today) and scare is tagged light (lantern, candle, torch); both rows are now need_tool 0, need_hand 1, so the kit is worth one success. The old drive-off rule (+1 needed without a light) is gone, replaced by this. Armour is not built: nothing in the framework says what a padded gambeson does, and that is a question for the user (roadmap). tests/wounds.mjs (new) proves the chain nobody had seen in play: a crawler is set on by a creature made huge and territorial with every attribute at 6, and on seeds 1, 3 and 7 they are wounded three times and go down every time; on seed 1 nobody reached them and they died; on seeds 3 and 7 they were lifted, tended, and got up with a scar and Endurance one lower. PASS.

v2.46.0

2026-09-23 21:04 EDT — a kill is butchered, and remains lie where they fell

The skills tab says Foraging covers hunting, so the meat is now Foraging's [potential slop]: a kill leaves its remains on the floor (lying on their side, and a pointer on them says "Remains of a ..."), and a job goes up, "Butcher the kill" (Foraging, 2 units, easier with a cutting tool). Whoever finishes it carries the meat home -- a ration for each wound the creature could take, as before. Stone (Lithic) falls apart into rubble and leaves nothing. Remains nobody butchers rot away after rot_seconds (20 minutes). Previously the meat went straight onto the killer's back.

v2.47.0

2026-09-23 21:05 EDT — a meeting is a contest, as the rulebook says

Rulebook 2.6 [gospel]: a contest is one exchange, each side throws once, there is no difficulty number, more successes wins and a tie goes to the defender, and the loser takes one Lesson. v2.44 had the crawler needing to match the creature's successes, so every tie went to the crawler, and the wall (too few dice to try) applied to a contest, where it does not belong. Now attempt() takes a contest flag that skips the wall, and every meeting throw is a contest: in a fight the crawlers hold their ground (win ties) when they were set on, cornered or are keeping their camp, and the creature holds it when they went at it; a wound needs beating the other side outright, and beating it by three is two wounds; driving off and sneaking past are contests the crawler starts, so a tie is a failure (it stands its ground; it sees them). The weapon still lowers what must be beaten, as a tool does.

v2.47.1

2026-09-23 21:06 EDT — packs fight for their own, and the creature's throw is on the ledger

A hostile or territorial pair or pack: when one of them is fought, the rest of it within 8 m come to its aid, each its own fight, walking in first [potential slop]. A crawler can be in more than one fight at once and throws against each. Rulebook 10 [potential slop in the rulebook]: a creature's throw now goes on the ledger of whoever it is fighting, and its number is hidden ("? dice → ?") until its measure has been studied. Measured on v2.47.0 (tests/meet.mjs, 30 minutes each, seeds 1, 3, 7, 11): 39 fights, 19 kills, 14 butchered, 1 fell to rubble, 19 wounds, 2 crawlers down and both died (seed 3), 16 fled, 55 backed off, no drive-offs this time. tests/wounds.mjs failed on v2.47.0 for the test's own reason: seed 1's crawler went for the creature before it struck, and the test only counted being struck; it now counts either.

v2.48.0

2026-09-23 21:11 EDT — the dead are buried

A crawler who dies leaves a job, "Bury the dead" (Labouring, 3 units, easier with a digging tool) [potential slop]. Whoever finishes it lays them under a cairn where they fell -- a mound heaped with stones and a standing stone, "The grave of ..." under the pointer -- and brings their kit back to the camp store, where it is handed round like any other kit. Until then the body lies where it fell, still wearing what they wore.

v2.49.0

2026-09-23 21:13 EDT — in the dark a creature is heard, not seen

Rulebook 9 [potential slop in the rulebook]: "In the dark, a creature is heard, not seen... what a crawler is doing changes what they can hear." Every hear_seconds (3 s) each crawler listens: a creature moving in the dark within hear_reach (8 m) is a contest of their Studying against its quiet (Agility + Intellect); a crawler working or fighting needs one more, one reading a room or studying one less [potential slop]. A new order, "Listen in the dark", can switch it off. Heard, it goes in the log ("hears something moving in the dark", naming the kind only if it has been seen before), the map marks where it was with a mark that fades over five minutes -- where it WAS, not a live tracker, as the rulebook asks -- and a crawler with a light may creep towards the sound to get it into their light, which is how a thing heard becomes a thing seen and studied. tests/wounds.mjs now also proves burial: on v2.48.0, seed 3's crawler died and was buried, and their grave stands in the camp's structures. (The first run of it failed for the test's reason: the test kept the killer standing over the body and menacing, so nobody could reach it; it now takes the creature away once the crawler is dead.) Measured on v2.47.1 (tests/meet.mjs, seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, 30 minutes each): packs came to one another's aid 8 times, 1 drive-off, 45 fights, 8 crawlers down, 2 dead.

v2.50.0

2026-09-23 21:15 EDT — a bestiary, and the badly hurt stay near camp

A Bestiary window (button, or B) lists every kind the company has seen, and shows only what has been studied of each: its tags (seen at a glance), "studied n of 5", the traits and measure learned so far, and the fact once the last stage is done [potential slop]. Checked in the built page, seed 7 after 25 minutes: 8 kinds, the window drawn with no page errors. A crawler one wound from going down no longer leads exploring, hunts, or creeps up on things to study them; they stay near camp until the wound closes [potential slop].

v2.51.0

Rulebook 9 [potential slop in the rulebook]: "Once met, the map keeps where the creature was last known to be, drawn so it reads as old rather than as a live tracker." A creature standing in the light near a crawler leaves its position on the map as a dot in its kind's colour, kept for ten minutes; things heard in the dark keep their fading mark (v2.49.0). Checked on seed 3 after 10 minutes: 6 creatures with a last-seen mark and 5 with a heard one, drawn on the map with no page errors.

v2.52.0

2026-09-23 21:21 EDT — what a kill leaves comes home as materials

Every lineage already says what it leaves (meat, hide, bone, chitin, shell, feathers, fibre, spores, slime, glow, venom, crystal, stone...). Butchering now brings those home with the meat, one of each (two from anything large or huge), into the camp's materials, where crafting can use them [potential slop]: hide is taken as leather and fibre as cloth, so boots, belts, packs, gloves, hoods and tunics can be made from a kill; bone is already a recipe material (the Bone Charm). The rest keep their own names in the store until a recipe wants them. Stone creatures (Lithic) no longer crumble to nothing: their remains are broken up for crystal and stone, with no meat. Measured (tests/meet.mjs, seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, 30 minutes each): 19 butchered, 1 broken up, 20 loads of materials brought home ("Wren butchers the graven (meat (2), 1 feathers, 1 bone)", "Fen butchers the kelpie (meat (2), 1 shell, 1 slime)"); 40 fights, 29 wounded, 2 down, none dead; 65 heard lines in two hours where v2.49.0 had 157.

v2.53.0

2026-09-23 21:24 EDT — carrion-eaters go for the remains

A creature that eats carrion goes to any remains within carrion_reach (15 m) and eats them, taking carrion_bite (2 minutes) off how long they last each time it feeds; eaten away, the butchering job is dropped and the log says so [potential slop]. So a kill left lying is a kill that can be lost, and a fight draws the scavengers. In the usual run it never happened (tests/meet.mjs, 2 game hours: 19 kills, all 19 butchered before anything reached them), so tests/carrion.mjs (new) lays remains five squares from a carrion-eater that is near the company and watches: on seeds 3, 7 and 11 a Bubblob, a Barrow Borer and a Blipple each walked over and ate the remains to nothing within six minutes; seed 1 had no walking carrion-eater near the company. PASS. (Its first draft also picked a Duskadder far from everyone, which did nothing -- creatures nobody is near rest, by design -- so the test now picks from those within 40 m.)

v2.54.0

2026-09-23 21:29 EDT - v2.54.1 — a room's words say what it is likely to hold

Rulebook 9 [potential slop in the rulebook]: "Reading a room has consequences... a store room worth entering." Two new columns on the words tab, loot and life, carry the other half of what a word means [potential slop, all of it]: a Counting House or a Vault is three times as likely to hold kit, a Barracks, Granary, Tomb, Smugglers' or Kings' room twice, a Forgotten one half as likely again; Ruined and Burnt rooms half as likely. A Den is three times as likely to have something living in it, a Charnel House twice, an Ossuary, a Grotto or a Forgotten room half as likely again, a Silent room or a Chapel half as likely. Multiplied across a room's words. Reading a room is therefore worth something: its name now says where the kit and the creatures tend to be. An older bug, found by measuring this: the creatures in a room were drawn from the same random stream as the room's name (both used tag 11), so the name was already secretly deciding whether anything lived there -- the first tests/roomwords.mjs run showed rooms promising life had creatures 13% of the time against 27% for the rest, the wrong way round. Creatures now have a stream of their own (tag 12). This moves which creatures live where on every seed; the rooms, the ground and the loot do not move. tests/roomwords.mjs (new), 12 seeds' opening pieces: rooms whose words promise kit hold some 44 of 54 times (81%) against 10 of 43 (23%); rooms whose words promise life have creatures 10 of 23 (43%) against 21 of 74 (28%). PASS.

v2.54.2

2026-09-23 21:31 EDT — the Rooms window says what a read room is likely to hold

Once a room is read, its line in the Rooms window says "likely to hold kit" or "picked over", and "likely lived in" or "seldom lived in", from its words' loot and life. Checked on seed 3 with every seen room read: 13 rooms listed, 17 such notes, no page errors.

v2.55.0

2026-09-23 21:32 EDT — a death is grieved

When a crawler dies, everyone left loses grief (1) Sanity; burying them gives half of it back to the one who buries them and whoever is at camp [potential slop]. tests/wounds.mjs on this build: seeds 1 and 7 went down, were tended and got up scarred (Endurance 6 to 5, 5 to 4); seed 3 died, was buried, and the grave stands. One earlier run of it failed for time: a crawler who went down only 3,153 of the test's 3,600 seconds in had not been reached yet -- the test now waits twice as long. A three-hour soak (tests/meet.mjs 10800 5, v2.53.0) ran clean: 17 fights, 10 kills, all butchered, 3 down and none dead, no page errors. The tests themselves were not repeatable, and that is fixed. A seed played twice through __game.step gave two different games whenever the test had clicked the briefing away: that starts the real-time loop, and the frames it runs before the test pauses the game depend on the machine. Measured on seed 7, ten minutes of game: begun twice, different; not begun, identical every time. meet, wounds, study and carrion no longer dismiss the briefing, so a number they print is a number the next run will print again. wounds twice and carrion once on this build: PASS, PASS, PASS. The older tests still click it and so still wander a little from run to run.

v2.56.0

2026-09-23 21:44 EDT — wounds are dressed (Tending)

A wounded crawler at camp gets a job put up for them, "Dress a wound" (Tending, 2 units, easier with a medicine tool); whoever finishes it closes one wound at once, as long as the patient is still at camp [potential slop]. Wounds still close on their own, slowly. Measured (tests/meet.mjs, seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, 30 minutes each): 22 wounds dressed.

v2.56.1

2026-09-24 07:57 EDT — breaking off is from the whole pack, and a pack is weighed whole

The same run had seed 1 lose three crawlers in half an hour, and its log said why, twice over: a crawler who "breaks off, hurt, and backs away" was still being wounded as they went, because breaking off only took them out of the fight with the one creature, not the fights with its packmates; and crawlers deciding whether to take on a creature counted it alone, never the pair or pack that would join it -- so three of them, one after another, took on a pack of solarocs (huge) and went down around it, where nobody could reach them. Now breaking off is from every fight they are in, and a hostile or territorial pair or pack is weighed with every one of it within 8 m. Measured on the same seeds after: seed 1 lost nobody (0 down, 0 dead; 29 times the company chose to back off where it chose to fight 25), and none of the four seeds lost anyone.

v2.57.0

2026-09-24 08:02 EDT — comfort is not only the fire

Rulebook 7 [gospel]: "Comfort is not only the fire. Hot vents and magma in certain rooms, clothing that insulates and can overheat you, torches carried for light and warmth, and a hot meal that pays Hunger and Comfort together, all belong to the same system." The vents and the hot meal were built; the clothing and the torch were not. Three new rows on the activities tab [potential slop, the rows and their rates]: carrying a flame (a light tagged fire in the off hand: the torch) warms, Comfort +0.15 a minute; wrapped up (at least warm_kit, 2, pieces of wool or padded kit: the cloaks, the gambeson) keeps the cold off, Comfort +0.2; and the same kit by the fire or a hot vent is overheated, Comfort -0.3, Fatigue -0.05, Cleanliness -0.1 (sweat). Reachable, counted on seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, 13 and 17 after 30 minutes: 3 to 6 crawlers carrying a torch in every company, and one crawler in five of the six companies wearing enough to be wrapped up.

v2.57.1

2026-09-24 08:05 EDT — rough ground takes more to cross

Rulebook 3 [gospel]: "Wet, loose or cluttered ground takes more successes to cross, never a worse roll." Water already slowed a wader, but the throws made while walking did not know about the ground. Now the Maneuvering throw (covering ground) and the Focusing throw (keeping their feet) need rough_need (1) more success on standing water, a rubble floor, or a square with something growing or lying on it [potential slop: the number]. Counted over 30 minutes on seeds 1, 3, 7 and 11: 25,836 steps, 38 in water, 547 on rubble (525 of them on seed 11), 234 cluttered; the Maneuvering throw needed the extra success 42 times in 1,282, the Focusing throw once in 404.

v2.58.0

2026-09-24 08:10 EDT — a load is a burden

Rulebook 2.10 [gospel]: "Past that they're encumbered: clumsier, slower and tiring faster... Labouring reduces the burden of encumbrance but never removes it." How much a crawler can carry is still the user's open question (roadmap), so this takes the loads the game already has -- kit hauled home, a person carried, meat and materials from a kill -- as a burden, whatever they weigh [potential slop]. Burdened, a crawler walks slower by burden_slow / (1 + burden_ease x Labouring) (0.5 and 0.15: a crawler with 6 Labouring dice walks at about three quarters of their speed, never full), and a new activities row, burdened, tires them faster while they walk (Fatigue -0.2, Hunger -0.05 a minute).

v2.58.1

2026-09-24 08:30 EDT — a dressed wound is bound, and it shows

Rule 4 [gospel]: what is on a character is visible on the character. A wound dressed at camp now leaves a binding on the figure -- an off-white band round the brow and a wrap on the forearm -- for bandage_seconds (10 minutes), then it comes off [potential slop]. Checked in the built page: the band reads on the crawler's portrait in the roster.

v2.59.0

2026-09-24 08:31 EDT - v2.59.1 — torches and candles burn out

A torch burns torch_minutes (45) and a candle candle_minutes (90) of being carried, then goes out and is gone; a lantern does not [potential slop, all of it]. The camp makes new ones from what the recipes already say (a torch from a load of wood, a candle from wax), and a spent light's hand takes the next one from the store. Light becomes something the company has to keep up, which is what rulebook 4 means by "light is something you carry in or build". v2.59.0 alone was a death spiral on one seed, measured by tests/lights.mjs (new; seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, two game hours): seed 3's lit crawlers went 7, 7, 7, 7, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 0, 0, 0 at each ten minutes -- with no light nobody can gather the wood a torch is made from. v2.59.1 puts a torch ahead of anything else the camp makes whenever the company (and its store) holds fewer than one light more than min_party_lights. Same test: seed 3 went 7 ... 3, 2, 2, 2, 2, 1, 1, 1 -- never dark, but thin; seeds 7 and 11 held 8 or 9 all the way; seed 1 fell to 1 by the end. PASS by the test's own bar (never dark), and flagged here as thin: what the thin seeds are short of (wood, or crawlers) is being measured next. v2.59.2: it was wood. Counting crawlers and loads alongside the lights, the thin seeds had everyone alive and no wood at all -- the fire takes a load every three minutes and a trip brings three back, so a torch has nothing to be made from. Now torches burn 90 minutes and candles 180 (halving what lights cost in wood), and when the company is short of lights the fire is not fed its last load, so it becomes a torch [potential slop, both]. Same test: seeds 1 and 7 held 9 of 9 lit at the end, seed 11 6 of 9 (its wood ran out in the last half hour), seed 3 2 of 7. Seed 3's camp had 0 wood at every one of the twelve readings, from the first ten minutes on, before any torch had burned out: nothing near that camp can be gathered for fuel. That is older than this change and is on the roadmap. v2.59.3 looked twice as far for fuel when nothing lay within 30 m [potential slop]; it changed nothing on seed 3, and the log said why: crafting was eating the wood. In its first two minutes seed 3's camp made a torch, two staffs and three more torches out of every load it had, before the campfire was even built, and the fire never had wood again. Older than any of this -- the burning-out lights only made it matter. v2.59.4: ordinary making never takes wood below wood_low; only a torch made because the company is short of light may [potential slop]. Same test: seeds 1, 3 and 7 held 9 of 9 lit with 4 to 6 loads in hand at the end -- seed 3 among them, which had had none -- and seed 11, grown to nine people, ran its wood out in the last forty minutes and ended with 5 lit of 9. PASS. tests/wounds.mjs had three faults of its own found this round, all fixed: it borrowed a creature that might already be fleeing (it is now reset first); it kept its unbeatable creature menacing after its crawler went down, so whoever came to tend them was set on too (it now leaves once they are down); and with the Fight order on, the rest of the company sometimes killed it (the test now turns that order off, since it is about one crawler who cannot win). A death with nobody left standing to bury them counts: on seed 3 the unbeatable creature killed five of the seven before it was taken away.

"backs away from ..., not knowing what it can do" and "... notices ... watching it" were the two loudest lines in the game: on v2.59.4 (tests/meet.mjs, seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, 30 minutes each) 100 back-offs and 139 noticings, 47 and 54 on single seeds. v2.68.0

2026-09-24 11:03 EDT - v2.68.1 — strangers must be made welcome

Bargaining was the one skill of the fifteen nothing ever threw. A stranger who walks in to the fire is now not one of the company straight away: a "Welcome a stranger" job goes up at the fire (Bargaining, 2 units, 2 successes; the stranger can't welcome themselves), and whoever takes it talks them round. Welcomed, they stay and get a bedroll. If nobody has done it within welcome_wait (240 s), they walk on into the dark. The job sits high in the order of work, just after dressing wounds, and can be switched off there like any other. A waiting stranger's tooltip says so. Every choice here is [potential slop]. tests/welcome.mjs: on seeds 1, 3, 7 and 11 over two hours, 13 strangers arrived and 13 were welcomed, with 30 Bargaining throws between them. With the job switched off (seed 1), every stranger walks on.

v2.69.0

With nobody to do it, they still walk away as before. When somebody walks away, any job about them is dropped. v2.69.1

2026-09-24 11:49 EDT — the ledger draws once a frame

Every line written to the ledger rebuilt all eighty shown entries and forced the page to lay itself out again. At high speed that happened many times in one frame. It now draws at most once a frame, however many lines came in. The People tab now also catches the new welcome and talk-round lines.

v2.70.0

2026-09-24 11:51 EDT — walks are worked out faster

A profile of 20 game minutes (seed 7) put a third of all the time in working out walks, and most of that in asking, over and over, which squares a square leads to. That answer only changes when the ground does, so the endless world now remembers it per square and forgets all of it when any ground, piece or stairwell changes. The same three worlds (seeds 1, 7, 11) run 20 minutes to exactly the same place, same crawlers on the same squares, same creatures, same 227/350/256 ledger lines, in 16–32% less time. The worst single second goes from 88/83/107 ms to 66/59/83.

v2.70.1

2026-09-24 11:56 EDT — big creatures go the way they fit

The profile also showed the ground changing over a thousand times in 20 minutes on seed 11: a huge creature had smashed 275 squares of rock. Large and huge creatures worked out their walks as if they were one square wide, then broke through whatever they did not fit, even when only wandering. Now they route along ground their whole footprint fits [potential slop]. Only when chasing, fleeing or going for food with no way round do they take the narrow way and break it open. Wandering never breaks rock. Seeds 1, 7, 11 and 3 over 20 minutes: 166/25/275/123 squares smashed before, 39/2/0/0 now. The remembered steps are also only forgotten when a square really changes. tests/sizes.mjs passes.

The store rule took a piece with a better wear rung even when it was worn through, and took a working piece over worn-through kit, so broken Boots and whole Wraps kept beating each other. Worn-through kit is now never taken from the store. Five worlds (seeds 1, 3, 7, 11, 23) run for 40 minutes: no line repeats more than 14 times, and the ones that do are ordinary (meals, rooms found, fuel running low).

Exploring waited for the square to clear, gave up after a moment, and chose it again. On the same seed, Rook stood with a 62-step route home for over 7 minutes because another creature rested at the top of the only stairwell back.

  • Exploring now never aims at, or walks through, a square somebody or something stands on (v2.70.3).

v2.70.7

2026-09-24 13:40 EDT - v2.70.9 — the long way home, and a clash of names
  • v2.70.7: On seed 11, Marlo stood "heading back to camp" 31 m from the fire with nothing in the way. The walk home was searched with a limit of 20,000 squares, and on a big world of several stories the way back (95 steps) lay beyond it. A walk to one known square is now aimed at it (A*: the search looks toward the goal first, and the walk found is still the shortest). Heading home gets a limit of 60,000.
  • v2.70.8: the other walks to one known square (to a seat by the fire, back from a job someone was in the way of, leaving the company, going to a need) are aimed the same way.
  • v2.70.9: v2.70.5's squeeze timer was called blockT, and so was the note, from the creature-meeting code, that a creature guarding loot has been dealt with. Each overwrote the other, so a crawler near such a creature re-decided to back off every second and stood still (Arden, seed 11). The squeeze timer is now squeezeT.

v2.70.10

2026-09-24 16:25 EDT - v2.70.13 — the picture no longer freezes for a second at a time

A playtester reported lag for a few seconds at a time, intermittently. Making new stretches of labyrinth was not it: a piece takes 6–26 ms to make, and meshing its ground about 1.5 ms per block, a few blocks a frame. Game steps take 0.2 ms typically and at most 64 ms (seeds 7 and 11, 30 minutes). The freezes were the graphics library building shaders mid-play.

  • The number of lights switched on is built into every surface's shader. The light pool switched lights off when unused, so each time a light-carrier walked onto or off the story in view, a torch burned out or the fire was lit, every shader was rebuilt: 7–9 at once, and frames of 0.8–1.6 s in the test browser. Every light in the pool now stays on, an unused one at no brightness (v2.70.10).
  • A surface's shader is also built the first time something of its sort is drawn: the first glowing creature part, the first chest, the first portrait. That cost 0.2–0.45 s each. Ten frames in, behind the briefing, the game now draws one of every creature kind, loose and locked kit, every plant, a portrait and a selection ring once, so all of them exist before play (v2.70.10, trimmed in v2.70.11 to 2 s in the test browser, far less on a graphics card).
  • Tried and taken back: drawing a portrait only every fourth frame (v2.70.12). It made most frames quicker but every fourth one slower, the same total; uneven is worse than steady, so v2.70.13 is v2.70.11 again.

Measured in the test browser (software drawing, so only the comparison means anything), 2 minutes at 8x speed. Seed 7: worst frame 1,600 → 272 ms, shaders built during play 58 → 2. Seed 11 (v2.70.9 → v2.70.13): worst frame 1,226 → 303 ms, shader builds during play 44 → 2.

v2.70.14

2026-09-24 17:14 EDT — light and sight worked out faster

Light and sight took a third of the game's time: for each square they touched, they asked "is it there, is it open, how lit is it", and set its light, each as a separate lookup of the same square. Each neighbour is now looked up once and read straight from its piece. Seeds 1, 7 and 11 over 20 minutes come out identical to v2.70.13 (the same crawlers and creatures on the same squares, the same total light, the same squares seen, the same ledger) in 20–28% less time.

v2.70.15

2026-09-24 17:17 EDT - v2.70.16 — big creatures' fit remembered

Creatures were 40% of the game's time, most of it asking whether a large or huge creature's whole body fits on a square, nine lookups a square, over and over for every walk it worked out. The answer only changes when the ground does, so it is remembered per square and size and forgotten with the ground's other memories. v2.70.15 keyed it by the square's number doubled, which is past the largest whole number JavaScript holds exactly, so different squares shared answers and seeds 1 and 11 played differently. The check against the previous build caught it, and v2.70.16 keeps one table per size. Seeds 1, 7 and 11 over 20 minutes: identical to v2.70.14, 7–17% faster.

v2.70.17

2026-09-24 17:22 EDT - v2.70.18 — tried and taken back: aiming creatures' walks

Aiming a creature's wandering and chasing walks at their target, as the crawlers' walks home are, changed which of several equally short routes they took, so matches played differently. Over the same three seeds it was no faster (30.3 s against 32.0 s in all), so v2.70.18 is v2.70.16 again.

v2.70.19

2026-09-24 17:24 EDT — wet squares remembered

Every walk worked out asks, of every square it considers, whether it stands in water: three lookups a square. The answer is now remembered per square and forgotten with the ground's other memories, which are now also forgotten when a square's material changes, not only its shape. Seeds 1, 7 and 11 over 20 minutes: identical to v2.70.18, 3–8% faster. Since v2.70.13 the same 20 minutes on seed 7 have gone from 15.3 s to 9.6 s of work.

v2.71.0

2026-09-24 18:14 EDT - v2.71.1 — breaking camp and moving on

The user: "they will eventually break camp and move on as they exhaust areas, perhaps even building sledges and eventually wagons pulled by tamed beasts" [gospel]. The world already goes on for about 524 km each way (checked at 64, 384 and 524 km), so what held the company in was the camp. The user's choices [gospel]: they move when the area runs dry, when the coach suggests it, or for danger; they take what they can carry; bedrolls are rolled up and carried, only the cold fire and the graves stay. How, and every number [potential slop]:

  • When. Every move_check_seconds (30) they weigh it. Danger: something hostile they have seen stays within hold_reach of the fire for move_danger_seconds (120), or somebody died there. Dry: nothing new seen within move_dry_reach (60 m) of the fire for move_dry_seconds (30 min) (v2.71.1: counted near the camp, since explorers far off always see something). The coach: a new "Move on" button. The best at Leading talks it over with the rest, a "Talk over moving on" throw needing move_suggest_need (2) successes. Failing, they stay put, and the throw teaches like any other. They never leave anybody who cannot walk.
  • Where. The best room they know of, scored as the first camp was, at least move_min_m (40 m) from the old fire. Knowing of nowhere, they say so and stay.
  • Packing. Every job is dropped, and each bedroll becomes a "Roll up a bedroll" job (Labouring). Each crawler shoulders pack_loads (4) loads: a bedroll first, then food (food_per_load 6 rations), fuel, kit and salvage (mats_per_load 3). Whatever does not fit is left behind, and the ledger says what. A full pack is a burden on the march.
  • The march. The fire is put out and stays, cold, with the graves (both still drawn). Everyone walks to the new room. Arrived, or after move_march_seconds (15 min), they lay out a camp as the first one was laid out and empty the packs into it.

tests/movecamp.mjs: six runs, each after half an hour of play, with the reason forced. They moved for the coach (seeds 1 and 5), for a dry area (seed 3) and for danger (seed 23), 46–55 m each time, and raised the new camp. On seed 11 they knew of nowhere far enough and stayed. On seed 7 a death had already moved them. The "Move on" button is in the top bar.

v2.71.2

2026-09-24 18:47 EDT — thin things no longer drop pixels

Reported: thin models like staffs and stool legs have gaps in their pixels. The picture draws about 18 pixels to the metre along the ground (about 20 upright), so a staff 5 cm across, or a stool leg 4–6 cm across on four sides, was under one pixel wide, and the graphics card only fills a pixel whose centre a shape covers. Whole rows went missing as they turned. Every rod, leg and handle, on crawlers, their kit, stools and structures, creatures, kit on the floor and plants, is now widened across its length until even its narrowest side covers thin_min_px (1.1 pixels, new in the knobs tab) [potential slop]. It is turned into metres from the sheet's own tile_px, so it follows if the scale moves. Nothing already that thick changes. The staff comes out about 7 cm across. How we got to 18 px per metre: the first game's rule was the user's "lock our artificial pixelized resolution to 32px per meter tall". This game's tile_px of 32 (v2.0.0) measures one floor square's diamond instead, about 18 per metre along the ground and 20 upright, so a crawler is about 31 pixels tall where the first game's was 52. Nobody chose that, and it is the user's to decide.

v2.71.3

2026-09-24 18:50 EDT — the picture's scale on a slider

The user asked for renderer sizes in the debug menu, to play with pixel scales. The Debug window opens with a Picture section:

  • px per metre: game pixels to a metre across the view, 8 to 64. Today's 22.6 is the sheet's tile_px 32 across one square's diamond.
  • pixel size: screen pixels to a game pixel, 1 to 8, the same as zoom.
  • A readout: the scale upright, and roughly how tall a crawler stands. Today: 19.6 px a metre, about 33 px. At 36 on the slider: 31.2 px a metre and about 53 px, the first game's "32px per meter tall".

Nothing is saved: "Copy changed values" gives the numbers for the sheet. Models already made keep their thin-part widening from the scale they were made at.

v2.71.4

2026-09-24 18:51 EDT — the march made safe

Read back over moving camp, and fixed before anybody met it:

  • Somebody carrying kit home, or carrying a fallen friend, when camp was struck would have arrived at a camp that no longer existed and stopped the game with an error. They now keep hold of it until there is a camp again.
  • On the march, anybody who reached the new room and had nothing to do went exploring, so the company was rarely all there at once and most marches ran to the 15-minute limit. Now nobody wanders off on the march.
  • Somebody who went down on the march had no camp to send help from, and would have died where they fell. When the new camp goes up, a rescue goes out to anyone down.

v2.72.0

2026-09-24 19:20 EDT - v2.72.1 — arms and armour

The user's choices [gospel]: weapons graded as tools (A) and deciding the skill thrown (C); armour that cancels part of a hit (A) and takes a landed hit instead of the body (C); "3 damage types, 3 weapon weights, and corresponding armor damage type resistances"; light = Focusing, medium = Brawling, heavy = Labouring; creatures' arms from their traits; "stings would pierce". Everything else [potential slop], all in the sheet:

  • Damage types: cut, pierce, crush (a new damage tab). Weights: light, medium, heavy (a new weights tab names the skill each fights with).
  • Weapons: the gear tab has new columns weight, damage, grade, resist_cut, resist_pierce, resist_crush. Eight new weapons make the grid with the Old Sword: Knife (light, pierce), Hatchet (light, cut), Cosh (light, crush), Spear (medium, pierce), Old Sword (medium, cut), Club (medium, crush), War Pick (heavy, pierce), Great Axe (heavy, cut), Maul (heavy, crush). Staff, Spade and Mallet are improvised (grade 1).
  • Grade: a made weapon makes the fight throw a success easier; an improvised one is even; bare hands make it a success harder, and bare hands crush.
  • Hitting a creature: what gets through is the margin less the creature's resistance to that damage type. A heavy weapon ignores heavy_breaks (1) of it. A blow that gets nothing through glances off, and says so.
  • Being hit: the margin less the worn armour's resistance to that type (all pieces together, at most resist_cap 2). Nothing left, and the armour turns the blow. A blow that lands is taken by the piece that resists that type best: it drops a rung of wear (chipped, cracked, broken), and the wearer takes no wound. Broken armour takes nothing, and is mended at camp like any other kit.
  • Armour: Gambeson (cut 1, crush 2), Jerkin (cut 1, pierce 1), Cap (cut 1), and three new pieces from the salvage that had no use: Chitin Coat (chitin; cut 1, pierce 2), Bone Lamellar (bone; cut 2, pierce 1), Shell Cap (shell; 1 of each). All are drawn on the figure.
  • Recipes: all eleven new pieces. Crafting now makes arms first: whoever has no made weapon gets one of the weight whose skill they are best at, and whoever has nothing on body or head that turns a blow gets armour, when the store has the makings. The store hands out better arms and armour over worse. A tool somebody works with (Spade, Mallet) is never swapped for a weapon.
  • Creatures (natural_arms tab): the form first (stings, teeth and horns pierce; claws cut; shells and plates resist), then archetype (a Brute crushes; a Bulwark resists 1 of everything), then size (large and huge crush), then lineage (chitin, shell, scales, stone and ooze resist). Across the 432 kinds: 176 pierce, 171 crush, 85 cut. What a creature deals and resists shows in its panel and the bestiary once its measure is studied. A crawler's panel shows what they fight with and their armour.

tests/arms.mjs, seeds 1, 3, 7, 11 and 23, 90 minutes each: fights thrown with Focusing, Brawling and Labouring; blows glanced off creatures 8 times; armour turned blows 8 times and took 8; 20 weapons and pieces of armour made; every sting pierces. Clubs are the most common weapon made (two loads of wood): tuning, for later.

v2.72.2

2026-09-24 19:29 EDT - v2.72.3 — moving camp, fixed from three hours of watching

Four worlds (seeds 2, 7, 13, 21), three game hours each. The company moved on of its own accord on every one, once or twice, for a dry area or for danger. Two things were wrong:

  • Every move left the bedrolls behind ("leave behind: 8 bedrolls"). Packing meant to put one rolled bedroll in each crawler's pack, but a slip filled only the first crawler's space and counted just one. Each crawler now carries one while there are bedrolls (v2.72.2).
  • Every march ran the full 15 minutes. The march waited for everybody to reach the new room, and one crawler with no way there, or two steps short behind a friend standing in a doorway, held the whole company for the rest of the march. The stuck test caught five crawlers standing "on the march" for over ten minutes on seed 7. Somebody with no way there found three times running no longer holds the march (v2.72.2), and on the march friends shuffle past each other after squeeze_seconds, as they already do past a creature (v2.72.3).

v2.73.0

2026-09-24 20:27 EDT — 24 pixels a metre, and the phone's panel
  • The user set the scale: 24 game pixels to a metre across the view (it was 22.6). The confusing tile_px (pixels across one floor square's diamond) is replaced by px_per_m 24, read straight. Upright, a metre is now about 21 pixels, and a crawler about 35 pixels tall. The debug slider and the thin-part floor both use it.
  • The user, on phones: "character sheet covers the screen so while a character is selected, replace portraits and ledger with it". On a narrow screen, while something is picked, its panel takes the bottom of the screen and the portraits and ledger are hidden. They come back when it is closed.

v2.74.0

2026-09-24 21:12 EDT — the bestiary, to flip through

The user: "Create a website where I can flip through them all, organized by categories and filterable, watch their animations, and rotate them." At https://myrmecbruh.github.io/Project-Crawlers/bestiary.html, built by build.py from the game's own creature code (beasts.js, creatures.js, the sheet) and published beside the game every time, so it always shows what the game shows. The game's Bestiary window links to it.

  • The list: all 432 kinds, grouped by lineage, affinity, archetype, size, disposition, sociality or damage. Filters combine: within a group any chip, across groups all of them. A search runs over names, forms, tags and lore. Each card has a small picture in the game's own pixels.
  • One kind: click a card. It stands on a stage, idle, walking or menacing. Drag to turn it round and tip the view. The wheel or +/- zooms. It can turn by itself, or be shown as the group it is met in (a pair, a pack, a swarm). "Pixels" switches between the game's look (24 px a metre, blown up) and smooth. ← → flip through the kinds in the list's order, and Esc goes back. Beside it are its tags and traits, its six attributes, what it deals and resists, and its lore. A link to one kind is the page address with #Name.

v2.74.1

2026-09-24 21:30 EDT - v2.74.2 — armour eased, and running out of fuel is running dry
  • The wounds test found a crawler in good armour unhurt for four game hours against a huge creature of sixes: the armour cancelled most of each blow and then took whatever landed, whole. It now takes a light blow (1 through) whole, but a heavy one (2 or more through) wears it and wounds as well [potential slop]. The wounds test takes its victim's armour off, since it is about wounds (armour is tests/arms.mjs).
  • Seed 2 ran three hours with no fuel in camp and nothing near to gather: fire out, half the company unlit, and no move. No fuel in camp and none within reach to gather for move_nofuel_seconds (10 minutes, new in the knobs tab) now counts as the area having run dry: "There is nothing left to burn near here."
  • Marches: with v2.72.3's shuffling past, a march traced on seed 3 settled 92 seconds after setting off, everyone within 5 m of the new fire.

v2.74.3

2026-09-24 21:32 EDT - v2.74.4 — creatures potter instead of searching
  • v2.74.3: the route search keeps its queue in two flat arrays instead of a small array per square. The same walks exactly (seeds 1, 7, 11 identical), and no measurable speed-up, so allocation was not the cost.
  • v2.74.4: a wandering creature searched up to 500 squares for a way to a random spot near home, often one it could not reach at all, every few seconds for every creature near the company. It now potters: a walk of up to eight steps along open ground, drifting toward a spot near home, never leaving its range, never onto a stairwell, and only where a big one fits [potential slop]. Seeds 1, 7 and 11 over 20 minutes: 31%, 34% and 3% less work. Matches play differently from here on, since creatures wander differently.
  • v2.74.5: a creature's panel in the game links to its page in the bestiary viewer.

v2.74.6

Whether a square is beside a hot vent (27 lookups, asked twice for every crawler every step) is remembered per square and forgotten with the ground's other memories, and whenever a vent appears. Seeds 1, 7 and 11: identical to v2.74.5, 1–10% faster.

Rule 4: what a crawler carries shows. A pack shouldered to move camp now rides high on the back: a rolled bedroll, with a bundle stacked on it for every other load, so a full pack (4 loads) is a tall one [potential slop: the shapes]. It comes off when the packs are set down at the new camp.

v2.75.0

2026-09-24 21:41 EDT - v2.75.1 — glow, feathers and crystal find a use

Three of the leftover materials make things through rules the game already has [potential slop, all three]. Slime, venom and spores would need new rules (poison and the like), so they stay the user's to decide.

  • Glow Lamp (2 glow, 1 shell; offhand): a shell bowl holding a knot of something that glows, pale and steady, 5 m of light (a new lights row). It is not a flame, so it never burns out and warms nobody. When the company is short of lights, a glow lamp is made first if the store has the makings, then a torch (v2.75.1).
  • Feather Cloak (3 feathers; neck): tagged padded, so it keeps the cold off like wool, and is too warm by the fire. Drawn as a cloak with rows of feathers.
  • Crystal Charm (1 crystal; trinket), for looks.

2026-09-24 22:28 EDT — fuel from somewhere they can reach

Three-hour games on seeds 4 and 9 (v2.75.1) sat at no fuel for over two hours; on seed 4 the lights went out one by one (1 of 7 lit) while the larder filled (94 rations), and the move for "nothing left to burn" never came. A spot to gather from had been found, and nobody could reach it: the gather job stayed open forever, no other spot was ever tried, and the move rule saw a spot and waited. A gather job nobody has managed in five minutes is now dropped, so another spot is found; and ten minutes with no fuel at all, for whatever reason, counts as the area run dry. The same two seeds on v2.75.2: fuel 3–6 loads and 8–9 of 9 lit at every half-hour for three hours, nobody lost. A six-hour game (seed 5, v2.75.1) held steady: memory flat at 50 MB, 14 pieces held, no errors, all nine alive, one move made on its own.

v2.76.0

2026-09-24 22:41 EDT — the bestiary stage is the game

The user: "make sure it always draws from game as law, so that if things change the viewer stays up to date. zooming isn't working. make them selectable so the highlight stroke appears like in game. use a togglable real room with a moveable torch and skylight - I want to see what they will really look like."

  • The stage no longer draws anything of its own. It makes a real match (the game's newMatch, seed 7), shows one of its rooms through the game's own Renderer, and stands the kind in it as a real creature: the game's lights and shadows, skylight, pixel scale (px_per_m), cutaway, materials, the thin-part floor, and the rule that nothing in the dark is drawn. The game's code is shared with a switch (window.VIEWER) so the viewer can make matches without starting a game. Whatever changes about how the game looks shows here at the next publish, since both are built from the same code.
  • Zoom is the game's own (+/-, the wheel); Q/E and the < > buttons turn the view as the game does, "Angle" is the game's two angles, dragging turns it freely, and "Spin" turns it slowly.
  • Clicking the creature selects it and draws the game's highlight ring round it; pointing at it draws the paler one.
  • A real crawler carrying a real torch stands two squares off. Clicking the floor sends them walking there, and the light is worked out again every square they reach. "Torch" puts it out or lights it; "Skylight" switches the skylight; "Room" hides the room, leaving the creature, the torch-bearer and the light.
  • "Walk" has the creature really walk about the room, a step at a time; "Menace" turns it to face the torch-bearer; "Group" shows the pair, pack or swarm it is met in.

The list's thumbnails are still drawn with the game's own creature models, on a plain light.

v2.77.0

2026-09-24 22:46 EDT — desperate arrivals, hunted food, a quieter pointer

The user's words [gospel]: "do not highlight terrain blocks unless clicked. Mouseover info boxes should carry name only - players will click to learn more"; "remove the 6 food plants we made initially - crawlers will have to find stuff from the bestiary to eat"; "crawlers should not start with gear that is crafted from exotic materials underground like chitin etc. they are desperate to venture here."

  • Pointing: a square of ground is ringed only when clicked, never just for being pointed at (a crawler, a creature or a structure still is). The hover box shows the name alone; the rest is in the panel a click opens.
  • Food: the eight forageable plants are gone from the forageables tab (the tab is kept, empty, for anything the user adds), so nothing grows wild in rooms and there are no cuttings to plant. Foraging also used to turn any patch of earth into rations when there was no plant to pick; that is gone too. Food comes from hunting and butchering creatures, plus the rations they arrive with. Fishing a pool still brings back a catch without any creature, and is left for the user to decide.
  • Starting kit: a new start column on the gear tab: 0 for anything made from what is only found down here (Chitin Coat, Bone Lamellar, Shell Cap, Glow Lamp, Feather Cloak, Crystal Charm) [potential slop: which ones]. Nobody arrives with those, strangers included. 40 companies checked: 1,907 pieces of starting kit, none of them from underground.

vThe sheet as an artifact

The user: "can the spreadsheet be here as an artifact? so I don't have to keep downloading it?" At https://claude.ai/artifact/1bESHkvkKnkhB9BBJxo5sq, made by tools/sheet_page.py from docs/crawlers.xlsx: every tab in the sheet's order, a search over one tab or all of them, and a row count on every tab. A cell can be changed in place: the change is kept on the page as a pending edit (marked, the old value struck through, with Undo) until Claude puts it into the sheet, which stays the authority (rule 9). Tell Claude "apply the sheet edits". The page is republished whenever the sheet changes. [potential slop: the page's design]

v2.77.1

2026-09-24 22:53 EDT - v2.77.3 — food from the bestiary

With the plants gone, a three-hour game on seed 1 had an empty larder from the first hour on, and four crawlers starved. The user's words were "find stuff from the bestiary to eat", and the bestiary has plants and fungi of its own. Foraging now gathers one [potential slop: which, and how near]: a Flora or Fungi creature that is not hostile, whose square has been seen (v2.77.2: its own "seen" mark only comes when a crawler stands near it, which a rooted thing never does), within 80 m of the fire on the same story. It is a Foraging throw like any other, and it takes the creature whole: rations for its size (meat_per_wound a size step), and whatever it leaves (fibre as cloth, seeds, spores) into the store. An empty larder for move_nofuel_seconds (10 minutes) now counts as an area hunted and gathered out, and the company moves on (v2.77.3), as for fuel. Measured on v2.77.2, two hours: seed 4 fed itself (9 meat hauls, 20 catches, 1 death); seeds 1 and 9 still sat at 0 food from about the first hour (seed 1: 3 gatherings, 5 hauls of meat, 5 deaths). Nine people eat faster than hunting and gathering near one camp supply, so this is a balance question put to the user; moving on is what the company can do about it meanwhile.
